## A Foreword / 前言

===Original===
> “Amoto quoeramus seria ludo.”

> **To 

 Sinclair Lewis.**

===Chinese===
> 「撇开玩笑，让我们探究正经之事。」

> **致

辛克莱·刘易斯（Sinclair Lewis）。**

### I / 一

===Original===
**My Dear Lewis:**

To you (whom I take to be as familiar with the Manuelian cycle of romance as is any person now alive) it has for some while appeared, I know, a not uncurious circumstance that in the *Key to the Popular Tales of Poictesme* there should have been included so little directly relative to Manuel himself. No reader of the *Popular Tales* (as I recall your saying at the Alum when we talked over, among so many other matters, this monumental book) can fail to note that always Dom Manuel looms obscurely in the background, somewhat as do King Arthur and white-bearded Charlemagne in their several cycles, dispensing justice and bestowing rewards, and generally arranging the future, for the survivors of the outcome of stories which more intimately concern themselves with Anavalt and Coth and Holden, and with Kerin and Ninzian and Gonfal and Donander, and with Miramon (in his role of Manuel’s seneschal), or even with Sclaug and Thragnar, than with the liege-lord of Poictesme. Except in the old sixteenth-century chapbook (unknown to you, I believe, and never reprinted since 1822, and not ever modernized into any cognizable spelling), there seems to have been nowhere an English rendering of the legends in which Dom Manuel is really the main figure.

Well, this book attempts to supply that desideratum, and is, so far as the writer is aware, the one fairly complete epitome in modern English of the Manuelian historiography not included by Lewistam which has yet been prepared.

===Chinese===
**我亲爱的刘易斯：**

致你——我认定你对曼努埃尔系列传奇（Manuelian cycle）的熟稔，不亚于当世活着的任何人——我知道，许久以来你便觉得有桩不算不古怪的事情：在《波伊捷斯民间故事钥匙》（Key to the Popular Tales of Poictesme）里，直接关乎曼努埃尔本人的内容竟这样少。《民间故事》（Popular Tales）的读者（我记得你在阿卢姆（the Alum）说过这话——那天我们在数不清的别的话题之余，也谈论过这部皇皇巨著）无一不会注意到：曼努埃尔大人（Dom Manuel）总是隐隐约约地耸立在背景之中，颇像亚瑟王（King Arthur）与白须的查理曼（Charlemagne）在他们各自的系列传奇里那样，施行公道，颁赐恩赏，并且总揽着替故事结局的幸存者安排未来——而那些故事本身，更贴身地围绕阿纳瓦尔特（Anavalt）与科斯（Coth）与霍尔登（Holden），围绕凯林（Kerin）与宁齐安（Ninzian）与贡法尔（Gonfal）与多南德（Donander），围绕米拉蒙（Miramon，他以曼努埃尔总管的身份出场），甚至围绕斯克劳格（Sclaug）与斯拉格纳尔（Thragnar）展开，而不是围绕波伊捷斯的宗主。除了一册十六世纪的旧故事书（chapbook，我相信你并未见过；自 1822 年以来它从未重印，也从未被改写成任何可辨认的现代拼写），世上似乎还不曾有过一部英文的传说译述，让曼努埃尔大人当真做主角。

喏，本书便试图补上这一缺项；而且据笔者所知，在迄今编就的、以现代英语写成的曼努埃尔史学种种梗概之中，这是唯一一部相当完备、而又未被刘易斯塔姆（Lewistam）收进去的。

===Original===
It is obvious, of course, that in a single volume of this bulk there could not be included more than a selection from the great body of myths which, we may assume, have accumulated gradually round the mighty though shadowy figure of Manuel the Redeemer. Instead, my aim has been to make choice of such stories and traditions as seemed most fit to be cast into the shape of a connected narrative and regular sequence of events; to lend to all that wholesome, edifying and optimistic tone which in reading-matter is so generally preferable to mere intelligence; and meanwhile to preserve as much of the quaint style of the gestes as is consistent with clearness. Then, too, in the original medieval romances, both in their prose and metrical form, there are occasional allusions to natural processes which make these stories unfit to be placed in the hands of American readers, who, as a body, attest their respectability by insisting that their parents were guilty of unmentionable conduct; and such passages of course necessitate considerable editing.

===Chinese===
当然，显而易见：在这样一部篇幅的单卷之中，所能收进的，无非是从那一大堆神话里拣选出的部分——我们不妨假定，这些神话是绕着救主曼努埃尔（Manuel the Redeemer）那伟岸而面目朦胧的身影渐渐积聚起来的。是以，笔者的目标在于：拣选那些看来最宜铸成连贯叙事与规整事件序列的故事与传说；赋与全书那种健全、有益世道人心而乐观的格调——在读物之中，这格调素来比单纯的识见更可人意；与此同时，又要在不碍明晰的限度内，尽量保住武功歌（gestes）古雅文体的风味。再者，在中世纪罗曼史的原作里，无论散文体还是韵文体，都偶有涉及自然过程的语句，这便使这些故事不宜交到美国读者手中——美国读者作为一个整体，惯以坚称自己的父母曾犯下不可名状的行径，来证明自身的体面；这样的段落，自然免不了需要相当一番删削。

### II / 二

===Original===
No schoolboy (and far less the scholastic chronicler of those last final upshots for whose furtherance “Hannibal invaded Rome and Erasmus wrote in Oxford cloisters”) needs nowadays to be told that the Manuel of these legends is to all intents a fictitious person. That in the earlier half of the thirteenth century there was ruling over the Poictoumois a powerful chieftain named Manuel, nobody has of late disputed seriously. But the events of the actual human existence of this Lord of Poictesme﻿—very much as the Emperor Frederick Barbarossa has been identified with the wood-demon Barbatos, and the prophet Elijah, “caught up into the chariot of the Vedic Vayu,” has become one with the Slavonic Perun﻿—have been inextricably blended with the legends of the Dirghic Manu-Elul, Lord of August.

Thus, even the irregularity in Manuel’s eyes is taken by Vanderhoffen, in his *Tudor Tales*, to be a myth connecting Manuel with the Vedic Rudra and the Russian Magarko and the Serbian Vii﻿—“and every beneficent storm-god represented with his eye perpetually winking (like sheet lightning), lest his concentrated look (the thunderbolt) should reduce the universe to ashes.﻿ ﻿… His watery parentage, and the storm-god’s relationship with a swan-maiden of the Apsarasas (typifying the mists and clouds), and with Freydis the fire queen, are equally obvious: whereas Niafer is plainly a variant of Nephthys, Lady of the House, whose personality Dr. Budge sums up as ‘the goddess of the death which is not eternal,’ or Nerthus, the Subterranean Earth, which the warm rainstorm quickens to life and fertility.”

===Chinese===
如今，任何一个学童（更遑论那位学究式编年史家——「汉尼拔进兵罗马、伊拉斯谟在牛津僧舍里著书」，正是为促成他所记述的那些最终的结局）都无需别人来告知：这些传说中的曼努埃尔，就一切实际的意图而言，是个虚构的人物。至于十三世纪上半叶确曾有一位名叫曼努埃尔的强大首领统治着波伊图穆瓦（Poictoumois），晚近已没有人当真提出异议。然而这位波伊捷斯领主实际人生中的种种行事——恰如皇帝腓特烈·巴巴罗萨（Frederick Barbarossa）被认作木魔巴巴托斯（Barbatos），先知以利亚（Elijah）「被接上吠陀伐由（Vayu）的战车」之后与斯拉夫的佩伦（Perun）合而为一——早已同迪尔吉克的曼努-埃卢尔（Manu-Elul）、「威严之主」（Lord of August）的诸般传说难分难解地混融在一处了。

于是，连曼努埃尔眼睛的斜视，也被范德霍芬（Vanderhoffen）在其《都铎故事集》（Tudor Tales）中解作一桩神话，把曼努埃尔同吠陀的楼陀罗（Rudra）、俄罗斯的马加尔科（Magarko）和塞尔维亚的维伊（Vii）牵连在一起——「每一位降福的风暴之神，都被绘成一只眼睛永久眨动（如片状闪电）的模样，惟恐他那凝聚的一瞥（霹雳）会把宇宙烧作灰烬。……他出于水泽的家系，以及风暴之神同阿普萨拉斯（Apsarasas）的一位天鹅少女（象征雾与云）、同火焰女王弗蕾迪丝（Freydis）的亲缘，也同样一目了然：而妮亚菲尔则分明是家宅之女主人奈芙蒂斯（Nephthys）的一个变体——巴奇（Budge）博士将这位女神的品格概括为『非永恒之死的女神』——要不然，便是涅尔图斯（Nerthus）、那片地下的大地，温热的暴雨将它催醒，使它生机丰饶。」

===Original===
All this seems dull enough to be plausible. Yet no less an authority than Charles Garnier has replied, in rather indignant rebuttal: “*Qu’ont étè en réalité Manuel et Siegfried, Achille et Rustem? Par quels exploits ont-ils mérité l’éternelle admiration que leur ont vouée les hommes de leur race? Nul ne répondra jamais à ces questions.﻿ ﻿… Mais Poictesme croit à la réalité de cette figure que ses romans ont faite si belle, car le pays n’a pas d’autre histoire. Cette figure du Comte Manuel est réelle d’ailleurs, car elle est l’image purifiée de la race qui l’a produite, et, si on peut s’exprimer ainsi, l’incarnation de son génie.*”

—Which is quite just, and, when you come to think it over, proves Dom Manuel to be nowadays, for practical purposes, at least as real as Dr. Paul Vanderhoffen.

===Chinese===
然而，权威不亚于夏尔·加尼耶（Charles Garnier）的人，曾以相当愤然的驳斥作答：「曼努埃尔与齐格弗里德、阿喀琉斯与鲁斯泰姆，实际上是何等人物？他们凭怎样的功业，博得了本族之人向他们奉献的永恒赞叹？这些问题，永远无人能够回答。……然而波伊捷斯相信，它的罗曼史所妆点得这样美好的这个人物是真实的，因为这个国度再没有别的历史。何况曼努埃尔伯爵这个人物本来就是真实的：他是造就了他的那个种族净化了的形象，而且，倘若可以这样说的话，还是这个种族天才的化身。」

——这话相当公允；而且细想之下，它证明了曼努埃尔大人时至今日、就实际的用途而言，至少和保罗·范德霍芬博士一样真实。

### III / 三

===Original===
Between the two main epic cycles of Poictesme, as embodied in *Les Gestes de Manuel* and *La Haulte Histoire de Jurgen*, more or less comparison is inevitable. And Codman, I believe, has put the gist of the matter succinctly enough.

Says Codman: “The Gestes are mundane stories, the History is a cosmic affair, in that, where Manuel faces the world, Jurgen considers the universe.﻿ ﻿… Dom Manuel is the Achilles of Poictesme, as Jurgen is its Ulysses.”

===Chinese===
在体现于《曼努埃尔武功歌》（Les Gestes de Manuel）与《于尔根高史》（La Haulte Histoire de Jurgen）的波伊捷斯两大史诗系列之间，多多少少的比较总在所难免。而科德曼（Codman），我相信，已经把这件事的要旨说得够扼要了。

科德曼这样说：「武功歌讲的是尘世的故事，《高史》却是宇宙的大事：曼努埃尔面对的是世界，于尔根思量的是宇宙。……曼努埃尔大人是波伊捷斯的阿喀琉斯，正如于尔根是它的尤利西斯。」

===Original===
And, roughly, the distinction serves. Yet minute consideration discovers, I think, in these two sets of legends a more profound, if subtler, difference, in the handling of the protagonist: with Jurgen all of the physical and mental man is rendered as a matter of course; whereas in dealing with Manuel there is, always, I believe, a certain perceptible and strange, if not inexplicable, aloofness. Manuel did thus and thus, Manuel said so-and-so, these legends recount: yes, but never anywhere have I detected any firm assertion as to Manuel’s thoughts and emotions, nor any peep into the workings of this hero’s mind. He is “done” from the outside, always at arm’s length. It is not merely that Manuel’s nature is tinctured with the cool unhumanness of his father the water-demon: rather, these old poets of Poictesme would seem, whether of intention or no, to have dealt with their national hero as a person, howsoever admirable in many of his exploits, whom they have never been able altogether to love, or entirely to sympathize with, or to view quite without distrust.

There are several ways of accounting for this fact﻿—ranging from the hurtful as well as beneficent aspect of the storm-god, to the natural inability of a poet to understand a man who succeeds in everything: but the fact is, after all, of no present importance save that it may well have prompted Lewistam to scamp his dealings with this always somewhat ambiguous Manuel, and so to omit the hereinafter included legends, as unsuited to the clearer and sunnier atmosphere of the *Popular Tales*.

===Chinese===
大体而论，这一区分是站得住的。然而细究之下，我以为在这两系传说里，还能发现一种处理主角时更深刻、虽说更隐微的差别：写于尔根时，这个人的身心全部，都被当作理所当然之事和盘托出；而在写曼努埃尔的时候，始终——我是这样相信的——存着一种可以察觉的、古怪的、即便说不上全然费解也够疏远的隔膜。曼努埃尔做了这样那样的事，曼努埃尔说了如此这般的话，这些传说如此讲述：是了，可是无论在何处，我都不曾发现哪一句关于曼努埃尔思想与情绪的坚定断言，也不曾窥见这位英雄心底的运作。他是「从外面」被描写的，永远隔着一条手臂的距离。这不仅仅因为曼努埃尔的天性里浸着他父亲那位水魔的冷淡的非人之气：更应当说，波伊捷斯的这些老诗人，无论出于有意与否，把他们这位民族英雄写成了一个人物——纵然他的许多功业何等可钦可叹——他们对这个人物始终不能全然去爱，不能彻底地体恤，也不能全无猜忌地看待。

这一事实有好几种解释——从风暴之神既降灾又赐福的两副面孔，到一个诗人天生没法理解一个事事成功的人：但这一事实终究无关眼前的宏旨，只除了一点：它很可能正是促动刘易斯塔姆草草打发这位始终有些暧昧的曼努埃尔、从而漏收了下文所收这些传说的缘故——这些传说与《民间故事》较为澄澈、较为晴和的氛围不相合宜。

===Original===
For my part, I am quite content, in this Comedy of Appearances, to follow the old romancers’ lead. “Such and such things were said and done by our great Manuel,” they say to us, in effect: “such and such were the appearances, and do you make what you can of them.”

I say that, too, with the addition that in real life, also, such is the fashion in which we are compelled to deal with all happenings and with all our fellows, whether they wear or lack the gaudy name of heroism.

Dumbarton Grange 

 October, 1920

===Chinese===
至于我，在这出表象的喜剧（Comedy of Appearances）里，倒十分愿意追随老罗曼史作家们的指引。「我们伟大的曼努埃尔说过这样那样的话，做过这样那样的事，」他们实际上是这么对我们说的：「表象便是如此这般，你们且各凭本事去参详罢。」

我也这样说，只添上一句：在现实生活里同样如此——我们不得不以这副样子去应付一切发生的事、应付我们的一切同类，无论他们佩戴着还是缺少英雄主义那件俗艳的名号。

邓巴顿农庄（Dumbarton Grange）

1920 年 10 月

## XXV Affairs in Poictesme / 二十五 波伊捷斯的诸般事务

===Original===
They of Poictesme narrate how Manuel and Niafer traveled east a little way and then turned toward the warm South; and how they found a priest to marry them, and how Manuel confiscated two horses. They tell also how Manuel victoriously encountered a rather terrible dragon at La Flèche, and near Orthez had trouble with a Groach, whom he conquered and imprisoned in a leather bottle, but they say that otherwise the journey was uneventful.

“And now that every obligation is lifted, and we are reunited, my dear Niafer,” says Manuel, as they sat resting after his fight with the dragon, “we will, I repeat, be traveling every whither, so that we may see the ends of this world and may judge them.”

“Dearest,” replied Niafer, “I have been thinking about that, and I am sure it would be delightful, if only people were not so perfectly horrid.”

===Chinese===
波伊捷斯（Poictesme）地方的人讲述说，曼努埃尔（Manuel）与妮亚菲尔（Niafer）先朝东走了一小程，随后便折向温暖的南方；讲述他们如何寻到一位教士替他们主婚，曼努埃尔又如何征用了两匹马。他们还讲述，曼努埃尔如何在拉弗莱什（La Flèche）与一头相当可怕的巨龙交手并大获全胜，在奥尔泰兹（Orthez）附近又如何与一个格罗阿赫（Groach，水泽女妖）起了纠纷——他将她降服，收进一只皮瓶里；不过他们说，除此之外，这趟旅程倒也平安无事。

「如今一切义务既已解除，咱们又团圆了，亲爱的妮亚菲尔，」曼努埃尔说——他们这时正坐着歇息，他刚同那巨龙斗过一场——「我再说一遍，咱们要云游四方，去看这世界的尽头，并且评断它们。」

「最亲爱的，」妮亚菲尔回答，「我一直在想这件事。我敢说那一定妙极了——只可惜世人实在可恶得十全十美。」

===Original===
“What do you mean, dear snip?”

“You see, Manuel, now that you have fetched me back from paradise, people will be saying you ought to give me, in exchange for the abodes of bliss from which I have been summoned, at least a fairly comfortable and permanent terrestrial residence. Yes, dearest, you know what people are, and the evil-minded will be only too delighted to be saying everywhere that you are neglecting an obvious duty if you go wandering off to see and judge the ends of this world, with which, after all, you have really no especial concern.”

“Oh, well, and if they do?” says Manuel, shrugging lordily. “There is no hurt in talking.”

“Yes, Manuel, but such shiftless wandering, into uncomfortable places that nobody ever heard of, would have that appearance. Now there is nothing I would more thoroughly enjoy then to go traveling about at adventure with you, and to be a countess means nothing whatever to me. I am sure I do not in the least care to live in a palace of my own, and be bothered with fine clothes and the responsibility of looking after my rubies, and with servants and parties every day. But you see, darling, I simply could not bear to have people thinking ill of my dear husband, and so, rather than have that happen, I am willing to put up with these things.”

===Chinese===
「你这话是什么意思，亲爱的小家伙？」

「你瞧，曼努埃尔，如今你既把我从天堂接了回来，人们就会说了：我可是从我那些极乐居所里被召出来的，你总该赔我一处至少还算舒适、可以长住的尘世居所。是了，最亲爱的，你知道世人都是什么材料；你要是跑出去游荡，去看去评断这世界的尽头——那些地方说到底同你实在没有什么特别相干——那些坏心眼的人只会乐得到处嚷嚷，说你把一桩明摆着的本分丢在了脑后。」

「哦，好吧，他们嚷嚷便嚷嚷了，那又怎样？」曼努埃尔说，带着君侯气派耸了耸肩。「嘴上说说，伤不着人。」

「是了，曼努埃尔，可这样没根没底地四处游荡，一头扎进那些谁都没听说过的不舒服地方，就难免给人那副观感了。要知道，世上再没有一件事，比跟着你闯荡四方、行止随缘更叫我称心快意；而当伯爵夫人，于我根本算不得什么。我肯定，我一点也不想住自己的宫殿，不想被华服烦扰，不想为看管我的红宝石担干系，更不想天天被仆人和宴请缠住。可是你瞧，亲爱的，我实在受不了让人说亲爱丈夫的坏话；所以与其让这种事发生，这些苦头我都甘愿承受。」

===Original===
“Oh, oh!” says Manuel, and he began pulling vexedly at his little gray beard, “and does one obligation beget another as fast as this! Now whatever would you have me do?”

“Obviously, you must get troops from King Ferdinand, and drive that awful Asmund out of Poictesme.”

“Dear me!” says Manuel, “but what a simple matter you make of it! Shall I attend to it this afternoon?”

“Now, Manuel, you speak without thinking, for you could not possibly reconquer all Poictesme this afternoon﻿—.”

“Oh!” says Manuel.

“No, not single-handed, my darling. You would first have to get troops to help you, both horse and foot.”

===Chinese===
「哦，哦！」曼努埃尔说，一边恼恼地扯着他那撮小小的灰白胡子，「一项义务竟这样快地又生出一项来！那么你究竟要我怎么办？」

「这还不明白么：你得向费迪南德国王（King Ferdinand）讨来兵马，把那个可怕的阿斯蒙德（Asmund）赶出波伊捷斯。」

「哎呀！」曼努埃尔说，「你可把这事情说得真轻巧！要不要我就在今天下午把它办妥？」

「瞧你说的，曼努埃尔，你讲话不动脑子，因为你今天下午根本不可能收复整个波伊捷斯——」

「哦！」曼努埃尔说。

「不，单枪匹马是不成的，我的宝贝。你总得先讨来帮手的兵马，马队步卒都要。」

===Original===
“My dearest, I only meant﻿—”

“﻿—Even then, it will probably take quite a while to kill off all the Northmen.”

“Niafer, will you let me explain﻿—”

“﻿—Besides, you are miles away from Poictesme. You could not even manage to get there this afternoon.”

Manuel put his hand over her mouth. “Niafer, when I spoke of subjugating Poictesme this afternoon I was attempting a mild joke. I will never any more attempt light irony in your presence, for I perceive that you do not appreciate my humor. Meanwhile I repeat to you, No, no, a thousand times, no! To be called Count of Poictesme sounds well, it strokes the hearing: but I will not be set to root and vegetate in a few hundred spadefuls of dirt. No, for I have but one lifetime here, and in that lifetime I mean to see this world and all the ends of this world, that I may judge them. And I,” he concluded, decisively, “am Manuel, who follow after my own thinking and my own desire.”

===Chinese===
「我最亲爱的，我不过是想——」

「——就算那样，要把北方人统统杀光，多半也得费上好一阵子呢。」

「妮亚菲尔，你能不能容我把话说完——」

「——再说，你离波伊捷斯还隔着老远的路呢。今天下午你连赶到那里都办不到。」

曼努埃尔伸手捂住她的嘴。「妮亚菲尔，我说什么今天下午收服波伊捷斯，不过是想开个小小的玩笑。往后当着你的面，我再也不敢轻试含蓄的讥诮了，因为我看出来，你并不赏识我的幽默。与此同时，我再对你说一遍：不，不，一千个不！人称波伊捷斯伯爵，听着确实响亮，熨帖耳朵：可我不肯被人栽进几百锹泥土里，去生根抽枝、当一株蔬菜。不；因为我在此世只得一趟人生，而这一趟人生里，我要看遍这个世界和这世界的一切尽头，好评断它们。而我，」他斩截地下了断语，「就是曼努埃尔，追随我自己的思考、我自己的欲望。」

===Original===
Niafer began to weep. “I simply cannot bear to think of what people will say of you.”

“Come, come, my dear,” says Manuel, “this is preposterous.”

Niafer wept.

“You will only end by making yourself ill!” says Manuel.

Niafer continued to weep.

“My mind is quite made up,” says Manuel, “so what, in God’s name, is the good of this?”

===Chinese===
妮亚菲尔哭了起来。「我一想到人家要怎么说你，就简直受不住。」

「好了，好了，亲爱的，」曼努埃尔说，「这可太不像话了。」

妮亚菲尔哭。

「你再哭下去，到头来只会把自己哭出病来！」曼努埃尔说。

妮亚菲尔继续哭。

「我的心意已经拿定，」曼努埃尔说，「那么看在上帝分上，这样哭究竟有什么好处？」

===Original===
Niafer now wept more and more broken-heartedly. And the big champion sat looking at her, and his broad shoulders relaxed. He viciously kicked at the heavy glistening green head of the dragon, still bleeding uglily there at his feet, but that did no good whatever. The dragon-queller was beaten. He could do nothing against such moisture, his resolution was dampened and his independence was washed away by this salt flood. And they say too that, now his youth was gone, Dom Manuel began to think of quietness and of soft living more resignedly than he acknowledged.

“Very well, then,” Manuel says, by and by, “let us cross the Loir, and ride south to look for our infernal coronet with the rubies in it, and for your servants, and for some of your palaces.”

So in the Christmas holidays they bring a tall burly squinting gray-haired warrior to King Ferdinand, in a lemon grove behind the royal palace. Here the sainted King, duly equipped with his halo and his goose-feather, was used to perform the lesser miracles on Wednesdays and Saturdays.

===Chinese===
妮亚菲尔这下越哭越伤心。这位魁伟的壮士坐在那里望着她，宽阔的肩膀松垮了下来。他恶狠狠地踢向那颗沉甸甸、绿莹莹的龙头——它还在他脚边淌着丑陋的血——可这全然无济于事。屠龙者败下阵来。对着这样的水势，他一筹莫展：他的决心被浸得潮软，他的独立精神被这场盐水的洪流冲刷净尽。他们还说，如今青春已逝，曼努埃尔大人开始思念清静与安逸的生活，那份俯首认命，比他自己肯承认的还要深几分。

「那么，很好，」曼努埃尔隔了半晌说道，「咱们就渡过卢瓦河（Loir），骑马往南，去寻咱们那顶镶着红宝石的、见鬼的冠冕，寻你的仆人，再寻几座你的宫殿。」

于是，圣诞假期里，他们把一个身材高大、筋骨粗壮、斜着眼睛、头发花白的武士领到费迪南德国王面前，地点在王宫后面的一座柠檬园里。这位成圣的国王装束一应俱全——头顶光环，手执鹅毛笔——惯于每逢星期三和星期六，在此施行些较小的奇迹。

===Original===
The King was delighted by the change in Manuel’s looks, and said that experience and maturity were fine things to be suggested by the appearance of a nobleman in Manuel’s position. But, a pest! as for giving him any troops with which to conquer Poictesme, that was quite another matter. The King needed his own soldiers for his own ends, which necessitated the immediate capture of Cordova. Meanwhile here were the Prince de Gâtinais and the Marquess di Paz, who also had come with this insane request, the one for soldiers to help him against the Philistines, and the other against the Catalans.

“Everybody to whom I ever granted a fief seems to need troops nowadays,” the King grumbled, “and if any one of you had any judgment whatever you would have retained your lands once they were given you.”

“Our deficiencies, sire,” says the young Prince de Gâtinais, with considerable spirit, “have not been altogether in judgment, but rather in the support afforded us by our liege-lord.”

This was perfectly true; but inasmuch as such blunt truths are not usually flung at a king and a saint, now Ferdinand’s thin brows went up.

===Chinese===
国王见曼努埃尔容颜的改换，很是欢喜，说一位曼努埃尔这般地位的贵族，仪表之间便透出阅历与老成，实在是桩好事。可是——讨厌！——要他拨出兵马供曼努埃尔去征服波伊捷斯，那可完全是另一码事了。国王自己的兵要派他自己的用场，而这用场要求即刻攻取科尔多瓦（Cordova）。偏巧这时，加蒂讷王子（Prince de Gâtinais）与帕兹侯爵（Marquess di Paz）也带着这桩疯念头来了：一个讨兵去帮他打非利士人，一个讨兵去打加泰罗尼亚人。

「凡从我手里领过封地的人，如今好像个个都要兵，」国王咕哝道，「你们随便哪一位，但凡有点儿见识，封地一经到手，也就该守得住了。」

「陛下，我们的短处，」年轻的加蒂讷王子颇有些气盛地说，「并不全在见识上，倒是在我们宗主给予我们的支援上。」

这话千真万确；只是这般生硬的实话，寻常总不至于劈面掷向一位国王兼圣人，于是费迪南德那两道细眉吊了起来。

===Original===
“Do you think so?” said the King. “We must see about it. What is that, for example?”

He pointed to the pool by which the lemon-trees were watered, and the Prince glanced at the yellow object afloat in this pool. “Sire,” said de Gâtinais, “it is a lemon which has fallen from one of the trees.”

“So you judge it to be a lemon. And what do you make of it, di Paz?” the King inquired.

The Marquess was a statesman who took few chances. He walked to the edge of the pool, and looked at the thing before committing himself: and he came back smiling. “Ah, sire, you have indeed contrived a cunning sermon against hasty judgment, for, while the tree is a lemon-tree, the thing that floats beneath it is an orange.”

“So you, Marquess, judge it to be an orange. And what do you make of it, Count of Poictesme?” the King asks now.

===Chinese===
「你当真这样想？」国王说。「咱们倒要考察考察。比方说，那是什么？」

他指着浇灌柠檬树的那口水池；王子瞥了一眼浮在池中的黄色物件。「陛下，」加蒂讷说，「那是一颗从树上落下来的柠檬。」

「这么说，你断定它是一颗柠檬。那么帕兹，你又作何判断？」国王问道。

这位侯爵是个不肯多冒险的政治家。他走到池边，先把那东西看个清楚，再亮出己见，然后笑吟吟地走回来。「啊，陛下，您着实设下了一场针砭轻率论断的巧妙布道：那树固然是柠檬树，树下浮着的那个东西，却是一颗橙子。」

「这么说，侯爵，你断定它是一颗橙子。那么波伊捷斯伯爵，你又作何判断？」国王这时问道。

===Original===
If di Paz took few chances, Manuel took none at all. He waded into the pool, and fetched out the thing which floated there. “King,” says big Dom Manuel, sagely blinking his bright pale eyes, “it is the half of an orange.”

Said the King: “Here is a man who is not lightly deceived by the vain shows of this world, and who values truth more than dry shoes. Count Manuel, you shall have your troops, and you others must wait until you have acquired Count Manuel’s powers of judgment, which, let me tell you, are more valuable than any fief I have to give.”

So when the spring had opened, Manuel went into Poictesme at the head of a very creditable army, and Dom Manuel summoned Duke Asmund to surrender all that country. Asmund, who was habitually peevish under the puckerel curse, refused with opprobrious epithets, and the fighting began.

===Chinese===
若说帕兹不肯多冒险，曼努埃尔则一点儿险也不冒。他蹚进池中，把浮着的东西捞了出来。「王啊，」高大的曼努埃尔大人说，那双明亮的淡色眼睛煞有介事地眨着，「这是半个橙子。」

国王说道：「这才是一位不轻易被这尘世虚浮表象所欺的人，一位把真理看得比干鞋还重的人。曼努埃尔伯爵，你准能得到你的兵马；你们二位也只得等着，等你们也练出了曼努埃尔伯爵这般的判断力再说——让我告诉你们，这比我能给出的任何封地都更值钱。」

于是春光一放，曼努埃尔便统领一支颇为像样的军队开进了波伊捷斯；曼努埃尔大人传召阿斯蒙德公爵，命他献出整个国土。阿斯蒙德长年在帕克雷尔诅咒（puckerel curse）底下动辄烦躁易怒，这时用一连串不堪入耳的骂名回绝了，战事就此开场。

===Original===
Manuel had, of course, no knowledge of generalship, but King Ferdinand sent the Conde de Tohil Vaca as Manuel’s lieutenant. Manuel now figured imposingly in jeweled armor, and the sight of his shield bearing the rampant stallion and the motto *Mundus vult decipi* became in battle a signal for the more prudent among his adversaries to distinguish themselves in some other part of the conflict. It was whispered by backbiters that in counsel and in public discourse Dom Manuel sonorously repeated the orders and opinions provided by Tohil Vaca: either way, the official utterances of the Count of Poictesme roused everywhere the kindly feeling which one reserves for old friends, so that no harm was done.

To the contrary, Dom Manuel now developed an invaluable gift for public speaking, and in every place which he conquered and occupied he made powerful addresses to the surviving inhabitants before he had them hanged, exhorting all right-thinking persons to crush the military autocracy of Asmund. Besides, as Manuel pointed out, this was a struggle such as the world had never known, in that it was a war to end war forever, and to ensure eternal peace for everybody’s children. Never, as he put it forcefully, had men fought for a more glorious cause. And so on and so on, said he, and these uplifting thoughts had a fine effect upon everyone.

“How wonderfully you speak!” Dame Niafer would say admiringly.

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔当然全然不懂将略，费迪南德国王便派了托希尔·瓦卡伯爵（Conde de Tohil Vaca）来做他的副将。曼努埃尔如今顶盔贯甲、缀宝嵌珠，出场好不威风；他那面绘着后足跃立的牡马、题着铭言 *Mundus vult decipi*（世界甘愿受欺）的盾牌，在战场上成了一个信号，令敌方阵营里较为谨慎的几位，赶紧到战场的别的地段去建树功勋。背后嚼舌的人窃窃私语，说曼努埃尔大人在议事和公开演说里朗声复诵的，全是托希尔·瓦卡拟好的命令与见解；但无论如何，波伊捷斯伯爵的官方文告处处唤起人们对老朋友才存的那份好感，所以倒也无伤大雅。

非但如此，曼努埃尔大人如今还练出了一种无价的本事——当众演说；每攻占一地，他都在把幸存的居民绞死之前，先向他们发表几篇铿锵有力的演说，勉励一切头脑端正的人奋起粉碎阿斯蒙德的军事独裁。再者，照曼努埃尔的指点，这是一场世间前所未见的斗争：它是一场一劳永逸终结战争的战争，要为每一个人的儿孙确保永久的和平。照他有力的说法，人类从不曾为比这更光荣的事业战斗过。如此如此，这般这般，他说；这些催人向上的思想，对人人都有极好的效果。

「你讲得多妙啊！」妮亚菲尔夫人总会赞叹道。

===Original===
And Manuel would look at her queerly, and reply: “I am earning your home, my dear, and your servants’ wages, and some day these verbal jewels will be perpetuated in a real coronet. For I perceive that a former acquaintance of mine was right in pointing out the difference between men and the other animals.”

“Ah, yes, indeed!” said Niafer, very gravely, and not attaching any particular meaning to it, but generally gathering that she and Manuel were talking about something edifying and pious. For Niafer was now a devout Christian, as became a Countess of Poictesme, and nobody anywhere entertained a more sincere reverence for solemn noises.

“For instance,” Dame Niafer continued, “they tell me that these lovely speeches of yours have produced such an effect upon the Philistines yonder that their Queen Stultitia has proffered an alliance, and has promised to send you light cavalry and battering-rams.”

“It is true she has promised to send them, but she has not done so.”

===Chinese===
而曼努埃尔便会古里古怪地看她一眼，答道：「亲爱的，我挣下的是你的家宅、你仆人的工钱；有朝一日，这些言辞的珍宝将铸进一顶真正的冠冕，永传不朽。因为我看出来，我从前的相识之中有一位说得不错：他指出了人与其他动物的分别。」

「啊，是了，可不是！」妮亚菲尔说，神色极庄重，并不怎么追究这话里特定的含义，只大致理会到，她和曼努埃尔谈的是某种有益人心、虔敬圣洁的事情。因为妮亚菲尔如今已是虔诚的基督徒——这才配得上波伊捷斯伯爵夫人的身份——而普天之下，再没有谁比她更诚心诚意地敬重庄严的声响。

「比如，」妮亚菲尔夫人接着说，「我听说，你这些可爱的演说，在对面非利士人身上产生了这样的大效，连他们的斯图尔提蒂亚女王（Queen Stultitia）都主动提议结盟，还答应给你送轻骑兵和攻城槌来。」

「她答应送，这不假；可她并没有送。」

===Original===
“None the less, Manuel, you will find that the moral effect of her approbation will be invaluable; and, as I so often think, that is the main thing after all﻿—”

“Yes, yes,” says Manuel, impatiently, “we have plenty of moral approbation and fine speaking here, and in the South we have a saint to work miracles for us, but it is Asmund who has that army of splendid reprobates, and they do not value morality and rhetoric the worth of an old fingernail.”

So the fighting continued throughout that spring, and in Poictesme it all seemed very important and unexampled, just as wars usually appear to the people that are engaged in them. Thousands of men were slain, to the regret of their mothers and sweethearts, and very often of their wives. And there was the ordinary amount of unparalleled military atrocities and perfidies and ravishments and burnings and so on, and the endurers took their agonies so seriously that it is droll to think of how unimportant it all was in the outcome.

For this especial carnage was of supreme and worldwide significance so long ago that it is now not worth the pains involved to rephrase for inattentive hearing the combat of the knights at Perdigon﻿—out of which came alive only Guivric and Coth and Anavalt and Gonfal﻿—or to speak of the once famous battle of the tinkers, or to retell how the inflexible syndics of Montors were imprisoned in a cage and slain by mistake. It no longer really matters to any living person how the Northmen burned the bridge of boats at Manneville; nor how Asmund trod upon a burned-through beam at the disastrous siege of Évre, and so fell thirty feet into the midst of his enemies and broke his leg, but dealt so valorously that he got safe away; nor how at Lisuarte unarmored peasants beat off Manuel’s followers with scythes and pitchforks and clubs.

===Chinese===
「尽管如此，曼努埃尔，你终会发现，她这一嘉许，道义上的效果是无价之宝；而且我一向时常在想，这到头来才是顶要紧的事——」

「是了，是了，」曼努埃尔不耐烦地说，「咱们这边道义嘉许和漂亮话绰绰有余，南方又有一位圣徒替咱们行奇迹；可是阿斯蒙德手里才有那一支由堂堂恶棍组成的大军，而他们把道德和雄辩看得连一片旧脚指甲都不值。」

于是战事贯穿了整个春天。在波伊捷斯，这一切显得极其重大、旷古未有，正如战争在身在局中的人们眼里通常显出的样子。数以千计的人被杀了，令他们的母亲与心上人哀恸，也往往令他们的妻子哀恸。而那些空前绝后的军事暴行、背信弃义、奸掠焚掠等等，也照常有寻常那么多；受苦的人把自己的痛楚看得如此郑重其事，以致一想到这一切到头来多么无足轻重，便觉得可笑。

因为这场特出的屠戮，在太久以前原有过至高无上的世界性意义，久到如今已不值得费那个力气，为心不在焉的耳朵重述佩尔迪贡（Perdigon）骑士之战——那场恶战里活着出来的，只有吉夫里克（Guivric）、科斯（Coth）、阿纳瓦尔特（Anavalt）和贡法尔（Gonfal）——也不值得再提那场一度有名的补锅匠之战，更不必重述蒙托尔（Montors）那几位宁折不弯的市政官如何被关进笼里、又如何被误杀。北方人如何在曼纳维尔（Manneville）烧掉舟桥；阿斯蒙德如何在埃夫尔（Évre）那场灾祸横生的围城里踩上一根烧穿的横梁，就此跌进三十英尺下的敌群当中，折断了腿，却又厮杀得那般骁勇，终于安然脱身；在利苏阿尔特（Lisuarte），不着甲胄的农夫又如何用长镰、草叉和棍棒打退了曼努埃尔的部众——这一切，对任何一个活人都已真的无关紧要了。

===Original===
Time has washed out the significance of these old heroisms as the color is washed from flimsy cloths; so that chroniclers act wisely when they wave aside, with undipped pens, the episode of the brave Siennese and their green poison at Bellegarde, and the doings of the Antipope there, and grudge the paper needful to record the remarkable method by which gaunt Tohil Vaca levied a tax of a livre on every chimney in Poictesme.

It is not even possible, nowadays, to put warm interest in those once notable pots of blazing sulphur and fat and quicklime that were emptied over the walls of Storisende, to the discomfort of Manuel’s men. For although this was a very heroic war, with a parade of every sort of high moral principle, and with the most sonorous language employed upon both sides, it somehow failed to bring about either the reformation or the ruin, of humankind: and after the conclusion of the murdering and general breakage, the world went on pretty much as it has done after all other wars, with a vague notion that a deal of time and effort had been unprofitably invested, and a conviction that it would be inglorious to say so.

Therefore it suffices to report that there was much killing and misery everywhere, and that in June, upon Corpus Christi day, the Conde de Tohil Vaca was taken, and murdered, with rather horrible jocosity which used unusually a heated poker, and Manuel’s forces were defeated and scattered.

===Chinese===
时光把这些古老英雄事迹的意义洗刷净尽，正如颜色从轻薄的布匹上洗淡；所以编年史家们以不蘸墨的笔，挥手略过贝勒加德（Bellegarde）勇敢锡耶纳人与他们那绿色毒药的一段公案、略过当地那位对立教皇（Antipope）的种种行事，又吝惜纸张，不肯记下清瘦的托希尔·瓦卡那套奇妙的办法——他凭这办法在波伊捷斯向每一根烟囱征收一里弗尔（livre）的税款——凡此种种，都是明智之举。

如今，就连对那些一度出名的陶罐，也没法再热心起来了——罐里烧滚着硫磺、油脂和生石灰，曾被倾下斯托里森德（Storisende）的城墙，害得曼努埃尔的部下好生难受。因为这场战争虽然极其英勇，形形色色的崇高道德原则全都招摇过市了一过，双方用的又都是最响亮的言辞，它却不知怎么，既没有促成人类的改造，也没有促成人类的毁灭：而在那场杀戮与全面的破坏告终之后，世界照旧往前走，同它在历次别的战争之后的走法大致不差——心里揣着个模糊的念头，觉得一大笔光阴与气力都投得不上算，又揣着个坚定的信念，觉得把这话说出口未免不体面。

因此，报告如下也就够了：到处都有大量的杀戮与苦难；到了六月，基督圣体节（Corpus Christi day）那一天，托希尔·瓦卡伯爵被俘，遇害身亡——那场杀戮带着相当可怕的谐谑，异乎寻常地动用了一根烧红的拨火棍；曼努埃尔的兵马也被击溃驱散。

## X Alianora / 十 阿莉亚诺拉

===Original===
They of Poictesme narrate that after dinner King Raymond sent messengers to his wife, who was spending that Christmas with their daughter, Queen Meregrett of France, to bid Dame Beatrice return as soon as might be convenient, so that they might marry off their daughter Alianora to the famous Count Manuel. They tell also how the holiday season passed with every manner of festivity, and how Dom Manuel got on splendidly with his Princess, and how it appeared to onlookers that for both of them, even for the vaguely condescending boy, lovemaking proved a very marvelous and dear pursuit.

Dom Manuel confessed, in reply to jealous questionings, that he did not think Alianora quite so beautiful nor so clever as Niafer had been, but this, as Manuel pointed out, was hardly a matter which could be remedied. At all events, the Princess was a fine-looking and intelligent girl, as Dom Manuel freely conceded to her: and the magic of the Apsarasas, in which she was instructing him, Dom Manuel declared to be very interesting if you cared for that sort of thing.

The Princess humbly admitted, in reply, that of course her magic did not compare with his, since hers was powerful only over the bodies of men and beasts, whereas Dom Manuel’s magic had so notably controlled the hearts and minds of kings. Still, as Alianora pointed out, she could blight corn and cattle, and raise tempests very handily, and, given time, could smite an enemy with almost any physical malady you selected. She could not kill outright, to be sure, but even so, these lesser mischiefs were not despicable accomplishments in a young girl. Anyhow, she said in peroration, it was atrocious to discourage her by laughing at the best she could do.
===Chinese===
波伊捷斯人讲述：某日用过正餐，雷蒙德（Raymond）国王便派出信使去见他的妻子——她正伴着他们的女儿、法兰西王后梅蕾格蕾特（Meregrett）一起过圣诞节——请贝阿特丽丝夫人（Dame Beatrice）一俟方便便尽早归来，好让他们把女儿阿莉亚诺拉（Alianora）许配给名满天下的曼努埃尔伯爵。他们还讲到，节期如何在五花八门的欢庆里度过，曼努埃尔大人同他的公主处得如何融洽；还讲到在旁观者眼里，谈情说爱于他们两人——连对那个隐隐带着居高临下神气的小伙子也不例外——竟是一桩何等奇妙、何等可心的营生。

面对醋意隐隐的盘问，曼努埃尔大人承认，他觉得阿莉亚诺拉并不及妮亚菲尔当年那样美、那样聪明；不过，正如曼努埃尔所指出的，这实在算不得一桩可以补救的事。无论如何，公主是个模样标致、头脑伶俐的姑娘——这一点，曼努埃尔大人当着她的面也爽爽快快地承认；至于她正传授给他的阿普萨拉斯（Apsarasas）的魔法，曼努埃尔大人宣称，倘若你喜好那一类事情，倒也十分有趣。

公主谦逊地作答：她的魔法自然不能同他的相比，因为她的魔法只对人与走兽的身体有威力，而曼努埃尔大人的魔法却已如此显著地慑服过列位国王的心与头脑。话虽如此，阿莉亚诺拉指出，她能叫庄稼与牛羊遭殃枯败，能轻巧便当地掀起风暴，而且只消给她时日，你随便指定哪一种肉身的病痛，她几乎都能降落到仇人头上。不错，她没法当场取人性命；可即便如此，这些小小的祸祟，在一个年轻姑娘身上，也算不得寒碜的本事。不管怎么说——她收束道——拿人家竭尽全力所能拿出的最好的本事取笑、叫人灰心丧气，实在是可恶。

===Original===
“Ah, but come now, my dear,” says Manuel, “I was only teasing. I really think your work most promising. You have but to continue. Practice, that is the thing, they say, in all the arts.”

“Yes, and with you to help me﻿—”

“No, I have graver matters to attend to than devil-mongering,” says Manuel, “and a bond to lift from myself before I can lay miseries on others.”

For because of the geas that was on him to make a figure in the world, Dom Manuel had unpacked his two images, and after vexedly considering them, he had fallen again to modeling in clay, and had made a third image. This image also was in the likeness of a young man, but it had the fine proud features and the loving look of Alianora.
===Chinese===
「哎呀，好了，亲爱的，」曼努埃尔说，「我不过是逗你玩儿。我真觉得你的功夫大有前途。你只管接着练下去。练，才是要紧的——据说在一切艺术里都是如此。」

「是啊，再有你帮着我——」

「不成，我有比捣鼓魔鬼更要紧的事情要料理，」曼努埃尔说，「何况，我总得先从自己身上解掉一道束缚，才谈得上把苦难降给别人。」

原来，只因身上负着要在这世上立起一个形象的誓约，曼努埃尔大人已把他那两座泥像从行囊里取了出来；他烦躁地把两座像端详了一阵，便又动手和起泥来，塑了第三座。这一座依旧是个年轻人的模样，却生着阿莉亚诺拉那副高贵而骄傲的眉目，带着她那含情脉脉的神情。

===Original===
Manuel confessed to being fairly well pleased with this figure, but even so, he did not quite recognize in it the figure he desired to make, and therefore, he said, he deduced that love was not the thing which was essential to him.

Alianora did not like the image at all.

“To have made an image of me,” she considered, “would have been a very pretty compliment. But when it comes to pulling about my features, as if they did not satisfy you, and mixing them up with your features, until you have made the appearance of a young man that looks like both of us, it is not a compliment. Instead, it is the next thing but one to egotism.”

“Perhaps, now I think of it, I am an egotist. At all events, I am Manuel.”

“Nor, dearest,” says she, “is it quite befitting that you, who are now betrothed to a princess, and who are going to be Lord of Provence and King of Arles, as soon as I can get rid of Father, should be always messing with wet mud.”
===Chinese===
曼努埃尔承认，对这座像他相当满意；可即便如此，他在这座像里仍旧辨认不出他想要塑成的那个形象；于是他说，他由此推断：爱并不是他必不可少的东西。

阿莉亚诺拉一点也不喜欢这座泥像。

「倘或你塑的是我一个人的像，」她思忖道，「那原是一句很动听的恭维。可你竟把我的眉目拉来扯去，仿佛它们不中你的意似的，又把它们同你的眉目搅和到一处，直到塑出一个既像你又像我的年轻人的相貌，这就不是恭维了。这反倒是离自我中心只差一步了。」

「也许——如今我想起来——我正是个自我中心的人。不管怎么说，我是曼努埃尔。」

「再说，亲爱的，」她说，「你已经同一位公主订了婚，等我一打发掉父王，你就将做普罗旺斯（Provence）领主、阿尔勒（Arles）国王；这样一个人，成天价摆弄湿泥，也不大相称。」

===Original===
“I know that very well,” Manuel replied, “but, none the less, a geas is on me to honor my mother’s wishes, and to make an admirable and significant figure in the world. Apart from that, though, Alianora, I repeat to you, this scheme of yours, about poisoning your father as soon as we are married, appears to me for various reasons ill-advised. I am in no haste to be King of Arles, and, in fact, I am not sure that I wish to be king at all, because my geas is more important.”

“Sweetheart, I love you very much, but my love does not blind me to the fact that, no matter, what your talents at sorcery, you are in everyday matters a hopelessly unpractical person. Do you leave this affair to me, and I will manage it with every regard to appearances.”

“Ah, and does one have to preserve appearances even in such matters as parricide?”

“But certainly it looks much better for Father to be supposed to die of indigestion. People would be suspecting all sorts of evil of the poor dear if it were known that his own daughter could not put up with him. In any event, sweetheart, I am resolved that, since very luckily Father has no sons, you shall be King of Arles before this new year is out.”

“No, I am Manuel: and it means more to me to be Manuel than to be King of Arles, and Count of Provence, and seneschal of Aix and Brignoles and Grasse and Massilia and Draguignan and so on.”
===Chinese===
「这个我清楚得很，」曼努埃尔答道，「可尽管如此，我身上负着一道誓约，要我遵行母亲的心愿，要我在这个世上立起一个可敬而有分量的形象。不过撇开这个不谈，阿莉亚诺拉，我再向你说一遍：你那个一成婚便毒死你父亲的计谋，在我看来，出于种种缘由，未免失于轻率。我并不急于当阿尔勒国王；说实在的，我根本不能确定我想不想当国王，因为我的誓约更要紧。」

「心肝，我非常爱你，可我的爱并没有叫我瞎了眼，看不见这样一桩事实：不管你在法术上有怎样的天分，在日常事务上，你都是个无可救药的不切实际的人。这件事你交给我来办，我自会把诸般体面照管得妥妥帖帖。」

「啊，难道连弑父这样的事情，也须顾全体面么？」

「那自然：让人看作害消化不良而死，父王的下场岂不光鲜得多。倘若传扬出去，说他亲生的女儿都容他不下，人们免不了要对这位可怜的亲爱的大人猜疑出千百样坏事来。总之，心肝，我已拿定主意：幸而父王没有儿子，所以不等这个新年过完，你就要当上阿尔勒国王。」

「不，我是曼努埃尔：做曼努埃尔，在我看来，比做阿尔勒国王、普罗旺斯伯爵，比做艾克斯（Aix）、布里尼奥勒（Brignoles）、格拉斯（Grasse）、马西利亚（Massilia）、德拉吉尼昂（Draguignan）等地的总管，分量都要重得多。」

===Original===
“Oh, you are breaking my heart with this neglect of your true interests! And it is all the doing of these three vile images, which you value more than the old throne of Boson and Rothbold, and oceans more than you do me!”

“Come, I did not say that.”

“Yes, and you think, too, a deal more about that dead heathen servant girl than you do about me, who am a princess and the heir to a kingdom.”

Manuel looked at Alianora for a considerable while, before speaking. “My dear, you are, as I have always told you, an unusually fine looking and intelligent girl. And yes, you are a princess, of course, though you are no longer the Unattainable Princess: that makes a difference certainly﻿—But, over and above all this, there was never anybody like Niafer, and it would be nonsense to pretend otherwise.”

The Princess said: “I wonder at myself. You are schooled in strange sorceries unknown to the Apsarasas, there is no questioning that, after the miracles you wrought with Helmas and Ferdinand: even so, I too have a neat hand at magic, and it is not right for you to be treating me as though I were the dirt under your feet. And I endure it! It is that which puzzles me, it makes me wonder at myself, and my sole comfort is that, at any rate, this wonderful Niafer of yours is dead and done with.”
===Chinese===
「噢，你这样怠慢自己真正的利益，真叫我心都碎了！这全是那三座可恶泥像干的好事——你把它们看得比波松（Boson）与罗特博尔德（Rothbold）传下的古老公座还重，比看重我，更重出千百倍！」

「好了，我可没有这样说。」

「还有，你惦记那个死了的异教使女，也比惦记我多得多——我可是个公主，是一个王国的继承人。」

曼努埃尔瞧着阿莉亚诺拉，好一阵子没有作声。「亲爱的，我一向对你说，你是个容貌出众得罕见、头脑聪明得罕见的姑娘。是啊，你当然是位公主，虽说你已不再是那位高不可攀的公主（the Unattainable Princess）：这自然有分别——可是，撇开这一切不谈，天底下从来没有一个人比得上妮亚菲尔，硬要装作不是这样，便是胡说。」

公主说：「我自己都诧异。你在阿普萨拉斯闻所未闻的种种古怪法术里受过教，这无可置疑——你在黑尔马斯（Helmas）与费迪南德（Ferdinand）身上行下的奇迹便是明证：即便如此，我玩起魔法来也有一手干净漂亮功夫，你却把我当作你脚底下的尘土一般作践，这实在不该。而我居然忍受了！叫我不解的正是这一点，它叫我诧异于我自己；我唯一的安慰是：你那位了不得的妮亚菲尔，好歹已经死了，一了百了。」

===Original===
Manuel sighed. “Yes, Niafer is dead, and these images also are dead things, and both these facts continually trouble me. Nothing can be done about Niafer, I suppose, but if only I could give some animation to these images I think the geas upon me would be satisfied.”

“Such a desire is blasphemous, Manuel, for the Eternal Father did no more than that with His primal sculptures in Eden.”

Dom Manuel blinked his vivid blue eyes as if in consideration. “Well, but,” he said, gravely, “but if I am a child of God it is only natural, I think, that I should inherit the tastes and habits of my Father. No, it is not blasphemous, I think, to desire to make an animated and lively figure, somewhat more admirable and significant than that of the average man. No, I think not. Anyhow, blasphemous or not, that is my need, and I must follow after my own thinking and my own desire.”

“If that desire were satisfied,” asks Alianora, rather queerly, “would you be content to settle down to some such rational method of living as becomes a reputable sorcerer and king?”

“I think so, for a king has no master, and he is at liberty to travel everywhither, and to see the ends of this world and judge them. Yes, I think so, in a world wherein nothing is certain.”
===Chinese===
曼努埃尔叹了口气。「是啊，妮亚菲尔死了，这些泥像也是死物，这两桩事实无时无刻不烦扰着我。妮亚菲尔的事，我想是无计可施了；可只要我能给这些泥像注入几分生气，我想我身上的誓约便算圆满了。」

「这样的愿望是渎神的，曼努埃尔，因为永恒的圣父在伊甸园（Eden）里造他最初的泥塑时，所做的也不过如此。」

曼努埃尔大人眨了眨那双明亮的蓝眼睛，仿佛在思量。「唔，不过，」他神色庄重地说，「倘若我是上帝之子，那么我承袭我父亲的趣味与习性，我想也是顺理成章。不，想造一个活生生、有生气的形象，比寻常人的形象更可敬、更有分量——我以为这并不渎神。不，我想并不。反正呢，渎神也罢，不渎神也罢，这是我的需要，我必须追随我自己的思想与我自己的欲望。」

「倘若那欲望得了满足，」阿莉亚诺拉问得相当古怪，「你可肯安顿下来，过上一种与体面的魔法师和国王身份相称的、合乎理性的生活？」

「我想肯的。因为国王没有主人，尽可以游遍四方，亲眼看看这个世界的诸般末端，并对它们加以评判。是了，我想肯的——在这样一个没有任何事情确然无疑的世界上。」

===Original===
“If I but halfway believed that, I would endeavor to obtain Schamir.”

“And what in the devil is this Schamir?”

“A slip of the tongue,” replied Alianora, smiling. “No, I shall have nothing to do with your idiotic mud figures, and I shall tell you nothing further.”

“Come now, pettikins!” says Manuel. And he began coaxing the Princess of Provence with just such cajoleries as the big handsome boy had formerly exercised against the peasant girls of Rathgor.
===Chinese===
「这话我但凡信上一半，我也要设法去弄沙米尔（Schamir）了。」

「这沙米尔究竟是个什么鬼玩意儿？」

「失言罢了，」阿莉亚诺拉微笑着答道，「不，我不要同你那些蠢泥像有任何干系，别的话我也不告诉你半句。」

「好了好了，小宝贝儿（pettikins）！」曼努埃尔说。于是他哄起普罗旺斯公主来，用的正是当年那个高大英俊的小伙子对付拉斯戈尔（Rathgor）农家姑娘们的那一套温存软语。

===Original===
“Schamir,” said Alianora, at last, “is set in a signet ring which is very well known in the country on the other side of the fire. Schamir has the appearance of a black pebble; and if, after performing the proper ceremonies, you were to touch one of these figures with it the figure would become animated.”

“Well, but,” says Manuel, “the difficulty is that if I attempt to pass through the fire in order to reach the country behind it, I shall be burned to a cinder, and so I have no way of obtaining this talisman.”

“In order to obtain it,” Alianora told him, “one must hard-boil an egg from the falcon’s nest, then replace it in the nest, and secrete oneself near by with a crossbow, under a red and white umbrella, until the mother bird, finding one of her eggs resists all her endeavors to infuse warmth into it, flies off, and plunges into the nearest fire, and returns with this ring in her beak. With Schamir she will touch the boiled egg, and so restore the egg to its former condition. At that moment she must be shot, and the ring must be secured, before the falcon can return the talisman to its owner. I mean, to its dreadful owner, who is”﻿—here Alianora made an incomprehensible sign﻿—“who is Queen Freydis of Audela.”
===Chinese===
「沙米尔，」阿莉亚诺拉终于说道，「镶在一枚图章戒指上，这枚戒指在火之彼岸的那个国度里是大名鼎鼎的。沙米尔形如一粒黑色的卵石；行了应有的仪式之后，你拿它触碰这些泥像中的一座，泥像便会活起来。」

「唔，可是，」曼努埃尔说，「难处在于：我若想穿过那片火、到火背后的国度去，就会被烧成一块焦炭；这么着，我便没有办法弄到这件法宝。」

「要得到它，」阿莉亚诺拉告诉他，「须先从隼巢里取一枚蛋，煮得老老的，再放回巢中；然后带一张弩，撑一把红白两色的伞，埋伏在近旁，等着母鸟发觉自己的一枚蛋任她怎样也焐不热，于是飞出去，一头扎进最近的火里，衔着这枚戒指回来。她会拿沙米尔碰一碰那枚煮过的蛋，让蛋恢复原状。就在那一刻，必须把她射落，把戒指抢到手中——要抢在隼把法宝归还她主人之前。我是说，归还她那位可怕的主人——」说到这里，阿莉亚诺拉打了一个无人能懂的手势——「那位主人，便是奥德拉（Audela）的弗蕾迪丝女王。」

===Original===
“Come,” said Manuel, “what is the good of my knowing this in the dead of winter! It will be months before the falcons are nesting again.”

“Manuel, Manuel, there is no understanding you! Do you not see how badly it looks for a grown man, and far more for a famed champion and a potent sorcerer, to be pouting and scowling and kicking your heels about like that, and having no patience at all?”

“Yes, I suppose it does look badly, but I am Manuel, and I follow﻿—”

“Oh, spare me that,” cried Alianora, “or else, no matter how much I may love you, dearest, I shall box your jaws!”

“None the less, what I was going to say is true,” declared Manuel, “and if only you would believe it, matters would go more smoothly between us.”
===Chinese===
「得了，」曼努埃尔说，「在这隆冬时节让我知道这个，又有什么用处！离隼儿再行筑巢，还得有好几个月呢。」

「曼努埃尔，曼努埃尔，真没法懂得你！一个长大成人的人——更不必说一位名满天下的豪杰、一位法力高强的魔法师——竟那样噘嘴皱眉、蹬脚踢腿，半点耐性也没有，你可看不出这有多难看么？」

「是，我想这确实难看；可我是曼努埃尔，而我追随——」

「噢，饶了我，别来这一套，」阿莉亚诺拉叫道，「要不然，不管我多么爱你，亲爱的，我可要掴你的嘴巴了！」

「话虽如此，我方才要说的话是真的，」曼努埃尔声称，「只要你肯相信，你我之间的事情便会顺当得多。」

## III Ascent of Vraidex / 三 弗赖德克斯的攀登



===Original===
Now it was evening, and the two sought shelter in a queer windmill by the roadside, finding there a small wrinkled old man in a patched coat. He gave them lodgings for the night, and honest bread and cheese, but for his own supper he took frogs out of his bosom, and roasted these in the coals.

Then the two boys sat in the doorway, and watched that night’s dreams going down from Vraidex to their allotted work in the world of visionary men, to whom these dreams were passing in the form of incredible white vapors. Sitting thus, the lads fell to talking of this and the other, and Manuel found that Niafer was a pagan of the old faith: and this, said Manuel, was an excellent thing.

“For, when we have achieved our adventure,” says Manuel, “and must fight against each other for the Count’s daughter, I shall certainly kill you, dear Niafer. Now if you were a Christian, and died thus unholily in trying to murder me, you would have to go thereafter to the unquenchable flames of purgatory or to even hotter flames: but among the pagans all that die valiantly in battle go straight to the pagan paradise. Yes, yes, your abominable religion is a great comfort to me.”

===Chinese===
且说此时已是黄昏，两人在路旁一座古里古怪的风车里寻个栖身之处，发现里面有一个满脸皱纹的小老头，穿一件打补丁的外套。他留他们过夜，给他们老老实实的面包和奶酪；至于他自己的晚饭，却是从怀里掏出几只青蛙，搁在炭火里烤了吃。

随后两个少年坐在门口，望着当夜的一场场梦从弗赖德克斯（Vraidex）上下来，到耽于幻梦的世人世界里去赴它们各安其位的职司——它们正化作一缕缕难以置信的白色水汽，渡到那些人身上去。两人这般坐着，渐渐谈起这个又说起那个，曼努埃尔发觉妮亚菲尔是个信奉旧教的异教徒：而这，曼努埃尔说，是件极妙的事。

「因为，等咱们成就了这场奇遇，」曼努埃尔说，「不得不再为伯爵之女彼此决斗的时候，我准会杀了你，亲爱的妮亚菲尔。你若是个基督徒，在谋害我时这样不清不洁地死去，此后便得坠入炼狱那扑不灭的火焰，或是更热的火焰里去：可在异教徒当中，凡在战斗中死得英勇的，都径直升入异教的乐园。是了，是了，你那门可憎的宗教，于我实在是一大安慰。」

===Original===
“It is a comfort to me also, Manuel. But, as a Christian, you ought not ever to have any kind words for heathenry.”

“Ah, but,” says Manuel, “while my mother Dorothy of the White Arms was the most zealous sort of Christian, my father, you must know, was not a communicant.”

“Who was your father, Manuel?”

“No less a person than the Swimmer, Oriander, who is in turn the son of Mimir.”

“Ah, to be sure! and who is Mimir?”

===Chinese===
「这于我也是桩安慰，曼努埃尔。不过，你身为基督徒，本不该对异教说出半句好话。」

「啊，可是，」曼努埃尔说，「我母亲白臂的多萝西（Dorothy of the White Arms）固然是最热忱不过的基督徒，至于我父亲嘛，你须知道，他并不领受圣餐。」

「你父亲是谁，曼努埃尔？」

「不是别人，正是泅水者奥里安德（Oriander），而奥里安德又是密米尔（Mimir）的儿子。」

「啊，原来如此！那密米尔又是何人？」

===Original===
“Well, Niafer, that is a thing not very generally known, but he is famed for his wise head.”

“And, Manuel, who, while we speak of it, is Oriander?”

Said Manuel:

“Oh, out of the void and the darkness that is peopled by Mimir’s brood, from the ultimate silent fastness of the desolate deep-sea gloom, and the peace of that ageless gloom, blind Oriander came, from Mimir, to be at war with the sea and to jeer at the sea’s desire. When tempests are seething and roaring from the Aesir’s inverted bowl all seamen have heard his shouting and the cry that his mirth sends up: when the rim of the sea tilts up, and the world’s roof wavers down, his face gleams white where distraught waves smite the Swimmer they may not tire. No eyes were allotted this Swimmer, but in blindness, with ceaseless jeers, he battles till time be done with, and the love-songs of earth be sung, and the very last dirge be sung, and a baffled and outworn sea begrudgingly own Oriander alone may mock at the might of its ire.”

“Truly, Manuel, that sounds like a parent to be proud of, and not at all like a churchgoing parent, and of course his blindness would account for that squint of yours. Yes, certainly it would. So do you tell me about this blind Oriander, and how he came to meet your mother Dorothy of the White Arms, as I suppose he did somewhere or other.”

===Chinese===
「呃，妮亚菲尔，这事知道的人不多，不过他那颗智慧的头颅倒是出了名的。」

「那么，曼努埃尔，既然说到这个，奥里安德又是何人？」

曼努埃尔说道：

「哦，从那虚空之中，从密米尔族类栖息的幽暗之中，从荒凉深海晦冥那至极无声的堡穴里，从那无始无终的晦冥的安宁里，瞎眼的奥里安德来了——他出自密米尔，来与大海交战，来嘲弄大海的欲望。当风暴从阿萨神族（Aesir）倒扣的巨碗里沸涌咆哮，一切水手都听见过他的呐喊，听见过他的欢畅发出的长啸：当大海的边缘翘起，世界的穹顶摇摇欲坠，在那狂乱的波涛击打泅水者、却终不能磨垮他的地方，他的脸泛出白光。这位泅水者没有分得眼睛，却在目盲之中，带着无休无止的嘲弄厮杀下去，直杀到时间告终，杀到大地的情歌都唱罢，杀到最后一支挽歌也唱罢，杀到那受挫而力竭的大海老大不情愿地承认：唯独奥里安德一人，可以嘲笑它怒火的威势。」

「说实在的，曼努埃尔，听上去这真是位令人骄傲的父亲，绝不是个上教堂的父亲；他这双眼盲了，自然也就说通了你那斜眼的毛病。是啊，一定是这样。那么你给我讲讲这位瞎眼的奥里安德吧，讲讲他怎么遇上了你母亲白臂的多萝西——我料想他总在什么地方遇上了她。」

===Original===
“Oh, no,” says Manuel, “for Oriander never leaves off swimming, and so he must stay always in the water. So he never actually met my mother, and she married Emmerick, who was my nominal father. But such and such things happened.”

Then Manuel told Niafer all about the circumstances of Manuel’s birth in a cave, and about the circumstances of Manuel’s upbringing in and near Rathgor and the two boys talked on and on, while the unborn dreams went drifting by outside; and within the small wrinkled old man sat listening with a very doubtful smile, and saying never a word.

“And why is your hair cut so queerly, Manuel?”

“That, Niafer, we need not talk about, in part because it is not going to be cut that way any longer, and in part because it is time for bed.”

===Chinese===
「哦，没有的事，」曼努埃尔说，「因为奥里安德从不停止游水，所以他必须永远待在水里。因此他实际上从未同我母亲见过面，母亲嫁的是埃默里克（Emmerick），我名义上的父亲。不过，如此这般的事倒是发生了。」

于是曼努埃尔把曼努埃尔如何降生在一座山洞里的种种情由、把曼努埃尔如何在拉斯戈尔（Rathgor）及其附近长大的种种情由，一一讲给妮亚菲尔听，两个少年就这样谈个不休；未出世的梦在外头一幕幕飘过；屋里那满脸皱纹的小老头带着一个满含疑窦的微笑坐在那里听，一句话也不说。

「还有，你的头发怎么铰得这么古怪，曼努埃尔？」

「那个嘛，妮亚菲尔，咱们不必谈了：一来它往后不会再铰成那样了，二来也到上床睡觉的时候了。」

===Original===
The next morning Manuel and Niafer paid the ancient price which their host required. They left him cobbling shoes, and, still ascending, encountered no more bones, for nobody else had climbed so high. They presently came to a bridge whereon were eight spears, and the bridge was guarded by the Serpent of the West. This snake was striped with blue and gold, and wore on his head a great cap of hummingbirds’ feathers.

Manuel half drew his sword to attack this serpentine design, with which Miramon Lluagor made sleeping terrible for the red tribes that hunt and fish behind the Hesperides. But Manuel looked at Niafer.

And Niafer displayed a drolly marked small turtle, saying, “Maskanako, do you not recognize Tulapin, the turtle that never lies?”

The serpent howled, as though a thousand dogs had been kicked simultaneously, and the serpent fled.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，曼努埃尔和妮亚菲尔付清了主人索要的那份古来的价钱。他们离开时，他正坐着补鞋；两人继续攀登，再没有遇见骸骨，因为再没有旁人爬到过这么高。不久他们来到一座桥前，桥上竖着八支长矛，守桥的是西方巨蛇（Serpent of the West）。这条蛇身上布满蓝金相间的条纹，头顶戴着一顶硕大的蜂鸟羽毛帽。

曼努埃尔半抽出剑来，要向这件蛇形设计进攻——靠着它，米拉蒙·卢亚戈尔（Miramon Lluagor）叫那些在赫斯珀里得斯（Hesperides）圣园后方渔猎的红种部族连睡觉都睡得心惊胆战。但曼努埃尔看了看妮亚菲尔。

妮亚菲尔则亮出一只花纹滑稽的小乌龟，说道：「马斯卡纳科（Maskanako），你认不出图拉平（Tulapin）么——这只从不说谎的乌龟？」

巨蛇嗥叫起来，仿佛一千条狗同时挨了一脚，随即便逃走了。

===Original===
“Why, snip, did he do that?” asked Manuel, smiling sleepily and gravely, as for the third time he found that his charmed sword Flamberge was unneeded.

“Truly, Manuel, nobody knows why this serpent dreads the turtle: but our concern is less with the cause than with the effect. Meanwhile, those eight spears are not to be touched on any account.”

“Is what you have a quite ordinary turtle?” asked Manuel, meekly.

Niafer said: “Of course it is. Where would I be getting extraordinary turtles?”

“I had not previously considered that problem,” replied Manuel, “but the question is certainly unanswerable.”

===Chinese===
「咦，小家伙，他干吗那样啊？」曼努埃尔问道，一面睡意朦胧而又郑重其事地微笑着——这已是第三回，他发觉他那柄魔剑弗兰贝尔热（Flamberge）派不上用场了。

「说真的，曼努埃尔，没人知道这条蛇为什么怕乌龟：不过咱们关心的，与其说是缘由，不如说是效果。还有，那八支长矛是无论如何碰不得的。」

「你手里那只是只顶平常的乌龟么？」曼努埃尔恭顺地问。

妮亚菲尔说：「当然是。不然我上哪儿去弄不同寻常的乌龟呢？」

「这个问题我先前倒不曾考虑过，」曼努埃尔回答，「不过这问题确乎无从解答。」

===Original===
They then sat down to lunch, and found the bread and cheese they had purchased from the little old man that morning was turned to lumps of silver and virgin gold in Manuel’s knapsack. “This is very disgusting,” said Manuel, “and I do not wonder my back was near breaking.” He flung away the treasure, and they lunched frugally on blackberries.

From among the entangled blackberry bushes came the glowing Serpent of the South, who was the smallest and loveliest and most poisonous of Miramon’s designs. With this snake Niafer dealt curiously. Niafer employed three articles in the transaction: two of these things are not to be talked about, but the third was a little figure carved in hazel-wood.

“Certainly you are very clever,” said Manuel, when they had passed this serpent. “Still, your employment of those first two articles was unprecedented, and your disposal of the carved figure absolutely embarrassed me.”

“Before such danger as confronted us, Manuel, it does not pay to be squeamish,” replied Niafer, “and my exorcism was good Dirgham.”

===Chinese===
随后两人坐下吃午饭，却发现那天早晨从小老头那里买来的面包和奶酪，在曼努埃尔的背包里变成了大块的白银和天然纯金。「这真是恶心透顶，」曼努埃尔说，「难怪我的背险些压断。」他把财宝远远抛开，两人便俭省地拿黑莓当了午饭。

缠结的黑莓丛中，钻出了通体发光的南方巨蛇（Serpent of the South）——在米拉蒙的全部设计里，数它最小、最可爱，也最毒。对付这条蛇，妮亚菲尔使的手段颇为离奇。他在这场交易里动用了三样东西：其中两样不便谈论，第三样则是一尊榛木雕成的小像。

「你实在是聪明得很，」走过这条蛇之后，曼努埃尔说。「不过，你动用头两样东西的架势可谓史无前例，你处置那尊木雕小像的路数，则当真叫我窘极了。」

「曼努埃尔，面对方才那样临头的危险，过分拘谨可是划不来的，」妮亚菲尔回答，「况且我那道驱邪咒念的是地道的迪尔加姆（Dirgham）文。」

===Original===
And many other adventures and perils they encountered, such as if all were told would make a long and most improbable history. But they had clear favorable weather, and they won through each pinch, by one or another fraud which Niafer evolved the instant that gullery was needed. Manuel was loud in his praises of the surprising cleverness of his flat-faced dark comrade, and protested that hourly he loved Niafer more and more: and Manuel said too that he was beginning to think more and more distastefully of the time when Niafer and Manuel would have to fight for the Count of Arnaye’s daughter until one of them had killed the other.

Meanwhile the sword Flamberge stayed in its curious blue scabbard.

===Chinese===
此外他们还遭逢了许许多多别的奇遇与险境，若是尽数讲出来，足可写成一部冗长而最不可信的历史。但一路天气晴好宜人，每逢紧要关头他们总能闯过，靠的是妮亚菲尔的一计又一计骗术——每每当需要诳骗的当口，他转瞬间便生出一计来。曼努埃尔高声赞美他这位扁脸黑发同伴那令人称奇的机灵，还声言自己一个钟头比一个钟头更爱妮亚菲尔了：曼努埃尔还说，对于妮亚菲尔和曼努埃尔将不得不为阿尔奈（Arnaye）伯爵之女厮杀、直到其中一个杀死另一个的那段时日，他已越来越感到兴味索然了。

而与此同时，那柄剑弗兰贝尔热一直待在它那古怪的蓝色剑鞘里。

## XV Bandages for the Victor / 十五 为胜者裹伤

===Original===
They came out of the enclosure, to the old altar of Vel-Tyno, while the moon was still void and powerless. The servitors of Freydis were thronging swiftly toward Upper Morven, after a pleasant hour of ravening and ramping about Poictesme. As spoorns and trows and calcars and as other long forgotten shapes they came, without any noise, so that Upper Morven was like the disordered mind of a wretch that is dying in fever: and to this side and to that side the witches of Amneran sat nodding in approval of what they saw.

Thus, one by one, the forgotten shapes came to the fire, and cried, “A penny, a penny, twopence, a penny and a half, and a halfpenny!” as each entered into the fire which was the gateway to their home.

“Farewell!” said Freydis: and as she spoke she sighed.
===Chinese===
他们出了围场，朝维尔-蒂诺（Vel-Tyno）的古老祭坛走去，其时月亮仍空虚而无力。弗蕾迪丝（Freydis）的仆役们刚在波伊捷斯四野掠夺饕餮、横行欢闹了一个痛快的时辰，此刻正急急涌向上莫尔文（Upper Morven）。他们化作斯普恩、特罗与卡尔卡之属，化作种种久被遗忘的形影，悄无声息地前来，于是上莫尔文宛似一个高热垂死之人的错乱心神：而阿姆内兰（Amneran）的女巫们分坐两侧，点着头称许眼前所见的一切。

就这样，那些被遗忘的形影一个个来到火前，高喊着「一便士，一便士，两便士，一便士半，再加半便士！」逐一走进火中——那火是通向他们家园的门径。

「别了！」弗蕾迪丝说道；说话间，她叹息了一声。

===Original===
“Not thus must be our parting,” Manuel says. “For do you listen now, Queen Freydis! it was Helmas the Deep-Minded who told me what was requisite. ‘*Queen* is the same as *cwen*, which means a woman, no more nor less,’ said the wise King. ‘You have but to remember that.’ ”

She took his meaning. Freydis cried out, angrily: “Then all the foolishness you have been talking about my looks and your love for me was prearranged! And you have cheated me out of the old Tuyla mystery by putting on the appearance of loving me, and by pestering me with such nonsense as a plowman trades against the heart of a milkmaid! Now, certainly, I shall reward your candor in a fashion that will be whispered about for a long while.”

With that, Queen Freydis set about a devastating magic.
===Chinese===
「我们的分别不能是这个样子，」曼努埃尔（Manuel）说道，「弗蕾迪丝女王，你且听好！是深谋的黑尔马斯（Helmas the Deep-Minded）把必需的门道指点给了我。『*Queen* 与 *cwen* 本是同一个词，意思无非是女人，不多也不少，』那位贤明的国王说，『你只需记住这一点。』」

她听懂了他的意思。弗蕾迪丝怒声叫道：「原来你先前讲的那些关于我的容貌、关于你对我的爱恋的蠢话，全都是预先安排好的！你装出一副爱我的样子，拿庄稼汉哄挤奶姑娘芳心的那类废话来缠磨我，就这样把古老的图伊拉（Tuyla）秘法从我手里骗了去！好啊，这回我定要赏一赏你的坦白，赏法保管叫人窃窃私议上好一阵子。」

说罢，弗蕾迪丝女王便着手施展一场毁灭性的法术。

===Original===
“All, all was prearranged save one thing,” said Manuel, with a yapping laugh, and not even looking at the commencing terrors. He thrust into the fire the parchment which Freydis had given him. “Yes, all was prearranged except that Helmas did not purge me of that which will not accept the hire of any lying to you. So the Deep-Minded’s wisdom comes, at the last pinch, to naught.”

Now Freydis for an instant waved back two-thirds of an appalling monster, which was as yet incompletely evoked for Dom Manuel’s destruction, and Freydis cried impatiently, “But have you no sense whatever! for you are burning your hand.”

And indeed the boy had already withdrawn his hand with a grimace, for in the ardor of executing his noble gesture, as Queen Freydis saw, he had not estimated how hot her fires were.
===Chinese===
「全都、全都是预先安排好的，只除了一件事，」曼努埃尔说着，发出一声小狗般尖吠的笑，对那些正在成形的恐怖之物看也不看。他把弗蕾迪丝给他的那张羊皮纸投进火中。「不错，一切都是预先安排好的，只有一处例外：黑尔马斯没能涤净我身上那样东西——它不受雇金收买，绝不肯对你撒谎。于是深谋者的智慧，到了最后关头，终归无用。」

这时弗蕾迪丝把手一挥，将那头骇人巨怪挥退了三分之二——那怪物原是为诛灭曼努埃尔大人（Dom Manuel）而召的，尚未完全成形——弗蕾迪丝不耐烦地喊道：「可你到底长没长脑子！你的手烧坏了。」

果然，那少年早已龇牙咧嘴地缩回了手；因为正如弗蕾迪丝女王所见，他在慷慨完成那高贵姿态的一腔热忱里，没有估量准她的火有多烫。

===Original===
“It is but a little hurt to me who have taken a great hurt,” says Manuel, sullenly. “For I had thought to lie, and in my mouth the lie turned to a truth. At least, I do not profit by my false-dealing, and I wave you farewell with empty hands burned clean of theft.”

Then she who was a human woman said, “But you have burned your hand!”

“It does not matter: I have ointments yonder. Make haste, Queen Freydis, for the hour passes wherein the moon is void and powerless.”
===Chinese===
「对一个已经受了大伤的人，这点小伤算不得什么，」曼努埃尔郁郁说道，「因为我本想撒谎，可谎言到了我嘴里，却变成了真话。至少，我并未从这桩假意交易中得利；我向你挥手作别时，一双空手烧得干干净净，再无赃污。」

这时，那个已作了凡人女子的她说道：「可是你把手烧坏了！」

「不要紧，我那边备着药膏。快些，弗蕾迪丝女王，月亮空虚无力的时辰正在过去。」

===Original===
“There is time.” She brought out water from the enclosure, and swiftly bathed Dom Manuel’s hand.

From the fire now came a whispering, “Make haste, Queen Freydis! make haste, dear Fairy mistress!”

“There is time,” said Freydis, “and do you stop flurrying me!” She brought from the enclosure a pot of ointment, and she dressed Manuel’s hand.

“Borram, borram, Leanhaun shee!” the fire crackled. “Now the hour ends.”
===Chinese===
「还来得及。」她从围场里端出水来，利落地替曼努埃尔大人洗净了手。

这时火里传来一阵低语：「快点，弗蕾迪丝女王！快点，亲爱的仙子女主人！」

「来得及，」弗蕾迪丝说，「你别再搅得我心慌了！」她从围场里取出一罐药膏，替曼努埃尔敷裹了手。

「borram，borram，莲娜恩·希（Leanhaun shee）！」火焰噼啪作响。「时辰到头了。」

===Original===
Then Freydis sprang from Manuel, toward the flames beyond which she was queen of ancient mysteries, and beyond which her will was neither to loose nor to bind. And she cried hastily, “A penny, a penny, twopence﻿—”

But just for a moment she looked back at Morven, and at the man who waited upon Morven alone and hurt. In his firelit eyes she saw love out of measure and without hope. And in the breast of Freydis moved the heart of a human woman.

“I cannot help it,” she said, as the hour passed. “Somebody has to bandage it, and men have no sense in these matters.”
===Chinese===
于是弗蕾迪丝从曼努埃尔身旁一跃而起，扑向火焰——在火焰的彼端，她是诸般古老奥秘的女王；在火焰的彼端，她的意志便既无可解、亦无可缚。她匆匆喊道：「一便士，一便士，两便士——」

可是有一瞬间，她回头望了望莫尔文（Morven），望了望那个独自带着伤、守候在莫尔文山上的人。在他被火光映亮的眼睛里，她看到了无从计量、也无从指望的爱。于是弗蕾迪丝的胸中，动了一个人间女子的心。

「我拿它没法子，」时辰流逝之际，她说道，「总得有人来裹它，而男人家在这些事上是毫无见识的。」

===Original===
Whereon the fire roared angrily, and leaped, and fell dead, for the Moon-Children Bil and Hjuki had returned from the well which is called Byrgir, and the moon was no longer void and powerless.

“So, does that feel more comfortable?” said Freydis. She knew that within this moment age and sorrow and death had somewhere laid inevitable ambuscades, from which to assail her by and by, for she was mortal after the sacred fire’s extinction, and she meant to make the best of it.

For a while Count Manuel did not speak. Then he said, in a shaking voice: “O woman dear and lovely and credulous and compassionate, it is you and you alone that I must be loving eternally with such tenderness as is denied to proud and lonely queens on their tall thrones! And it is you that I must be serving always with such a love as may not be given to the figure that any man makes in this world! And though all life may be a dusty waste of endless striving, and though the ways of men may always be the ways of folly, yet are these ways our ways henceforward, and not hopeless ways, for you and I will tread them together.”
===Chinese===
于是那火怒吼一声，腾起，随即死灭——因为月中孩童比尔（Bil）与尤基（Hjuki）已经从那口名叫比尔吉尔（Byrgir）的井边回来了，月亮不再空虚而无力。

「喏，这样子觉得好受些了吗？」弗蕾迪丝说。她知道，在这一刻里，衰老、悲苦与死亡已在某处设下了躲不开的埋伏，日后要来袭击她，因为圣火熄灭之后，她便成了凡人；而她打定主意要善处此境。

曼努埃尔伯爵有一阵子没有作声。随后他开口了，声音发颤：「哦，亲爱的、可爱的、轻信的、仁慈的女人啊，我必须永远钟情的，是你，唯独是你，用那样一种柔情——那是高踞巍峨宝座、骄傲而孤寂的女王们无缘领受的！我必须始终侍奉的，也是你，用那样一种爱——那是任何人在这个世间造出的偶像都不配领受的！纵然整个人生或许是一片尘土弥漫、无尽营求的荒原，纵然世人行走的道路或许永远是愚妄之路，然而从今往后，这些路也是我们两个人的路，而且并非绝望之路，因为你我要一同把它们走完。」

===Original===
“Now certainly there is in Audela no such moonstruck nonsense to be hearing, nor any such quick-footed hour of foolishness to be living through,” Freydis replied, “as here tonight has robbed me of my kingdom.”

“Love will repay,” said Manuel, as is the easy fashion of men.

And Freydis, a human woman now in all things, laughed low and softly in the darkness. “Repay me thus, my dearest: no matter how much I may coax you in the doubtful time to come, do you not ever tell me how you happened to have the bandages and the pot of ointment set ready by the mirror. For it is bad for a human woman ever to be seeing through the devices of wise kings, and far worse for her to be seeing through the heroic antics of her husband.”

Meanwhile in Arles young Alianora had arranged her own match with more circumspection. The English, who at first demanded twenty thousand marks as her jointure, had after interminable bargaining agreed to accept her with three thousand: and she was to be dowered with Plymouth and Exeter and Tiverton and Torquay and Brixham, and with the tin mines of Devonshire and Cornwall. In everything except the husband involved, she was marrying excellently, and so all Arles that night was ornamented with flags and banners and chaplets and bright hangings and flaring lamps and torches, and throughout Provence there was festivity of every sort, and the Princess had great honor and applause.

But in the darkness of Upper Morven they had happiness, no matter for how brief a while.
===Chinese===
「说实在的，在奥德拉（Audela）可听不到这种发月狂的胡话，也不必活着捱这样一个脚下生风的愚蠢时辰，」弗蕾迪丝答道，「不像今夜在这里，它夺走了我的王国。」

「爱情自会有所报偿，」曼努埃尔说——男人说便宜话，向来是这个腔调。

而弗蕾迪丝，如今在一切方面都是个人间女子了，在黑暗中低低地、柔柔地笑了。「就这样报偿我吧，我最亲爱的：日后任凭我怎样软语央求，你也千万、千万别告诉我，你怎么恰好就把绷带和那罐药膏预先备在了镜子旁边。因为一个人间女子若是看得穿贤明国王们的机关，那已经很糟；若是看得穿自己丈夫的英雄派头把戏，那就更糟了。」

与此同时，在阿尔勒（Arles），年轻的阿莉亚诺拉（Alianora）以远为审慎的方式安排了自己的婚事。英格兰人起初索要两万马克作她的孀居产，经过无休无止的讨价还价，总算同意只消三千马克便把她娶下：普利茅斯、埃克塞特、蒂弗顿、托基、布里克瑟姆，连同德文郡与康沃尔郡的锡矿，都将充作她的妆奁。除开新郎本人这一项不谈，她这桩婚事结得再体面也没有了；于是那一夜，整个阿尔勒装点起旗帜、横幅、花彩和鲜亮的帷幔，燃起煌煌的灯盏与火把，普罗旺斯（Provence）全境处处欢庆，公主享尽了尊荣与喝彩。

而在上莫尔文的黑暗里，他们拥有幸福——不管这幸福何其短暂。

## XL Colophon / 四十 尾声

===Original===
Now some of Poictesme﻿—but not all they of Poictesme, because the pious deny this portion of the tale, and speak of an ascension﻿—some narrate that after the appalling eucharist which young Jurgen witnessed upon Upper Morven, the Redeemer of Poictesme rode on a far and troubling journey with Grandfather Death, until the two had passed the sunset, and had come to the dark stream of Lethe.

“Now we must ford these shadowy waters,” said Grandfather Death, “in part because your destiny is on the other side, and in part because by the contact of these waters all your memories will be washed away from you. And that is requisite to your destiny.”

“But what is my destiny?”

===Chinese===
如今，有些波伊捷斯人——但并非所有波伊捷斯人，因为虔敬之人否认故事的这一节，说起的是一场升天——有些波伊捷斯人讲述道：在年轻的于尔根（Jurgen）于上莫尔文（Upper Morven）亲眼目睹那场骇人的圣餐礼之后，波伊捷斯的救主便与死神祖父（Grandfather Death）一路骑行，踏上了遥远而扰人的旅途，直到两人走过了日落之处，来到忘川（Lethe）幽暗的水流。

「如今我们必须涉渡这片幽冥之水，」死神祖父说，「一半是因为你的命运在彼岸，一半是因为这些水一沾到你，你全部的记忆便会被从你身上洗去。而这对你的命运乃属必需。」

「可我的命运究竟是什么？」

===Original===
“It is that of all loving creatures, Count Manuel. If you have been yourself you cannot reasonably be punished, but if you have been somebody else you will find that this is not permitted.”

“That is a dark saying, only too well suited to this doubtful place, and I do not understand you.”

“No,” replied Grandfather Death, “but that does not matter.”

===Chinese===
「曼努埃尔伯爵（Count Manuel），那便是一切有爱生灵共同的命运。倘若你一直是你自己，那便断无道理罚你；可倘若你做过别人，你便会发现这是不被容许的。」

「这话玄暗得很，与这处疑影重重的所在再相称不过，我不明白你的意思。」

「不，」死神祖父答道，「不过那不要紧。」

===Original===
Then the black horse and the white horse entered the water: and they passed over, and the swine of Eubouleus were waiting for them, but these were not yet untethered.

So in the moment which remained Dom Manuel looked backward and downward, and he saw that Grandfather Death had spoken truly. For all the memories of Manuel’s life had been washed away from him, so that these memories were left adrift and submerged in the shadowy waters of Lethe. Drowned there was the wise countenance of Helmas, and the face of St. Ferdinand with a tarnished halo about it, and the puzzled features of Horvendile; and glowing birds and glistening images and the shimmering designs of Miramon thronged there confusedly, and among them went with moving jaws a head of sleek white clay. The golden loveliness of Alianora, and the dark splendor of Freydis and, derisively, the immortal young smile of Sesphra, showed each for a moment, and was gone. Then Niafer’s eyes displayed their mildly wondering disapproval for the last time, and the small faces of children that in the end were hers and not Manuel’s passed with her: and the shine of armor, and a tossing heave of jaunty banners, and gleaming castle turrets, and all the brilliancies and colors that Manuel had known and loved anywhere, save only the clear red and white of Suskind’s face, seemed to be passing incoherently through the still waters, like bright broken wreckage which an undercurrent was sweeping away.

And Manuel sighed, almost as if in relief. “So this,” he said, “this is the preposterous end of him who was everywhere esteemed the most lucky and the least scrupulous rogue of his day!”

===Chinese===
于是黑马与白马踏入水中：两骑涉渡而过，欧布勒斯（Eubouleus）的那群猪正候着他们，只是拴绳尚未解开。

于是在余下的那一刻里，曼努埃尔大人（Dom Manuel）回首下望，他看见死神祖父所言不虚。曼努埃尔一生的全部记忆都已从他身上洗去，只落得漂漂荡荡，沉溺在忘川幽冥的水里。溺在那儿的，有黑尔马斯（Helmas）那张智者的面容，有圣费迪南德（St. Ferdinand）的脸——顶上绕着一圈黯了色的光环——还有霍文迪尔（Horvendile）那副困惑的眉目；发光的鸟、莹亮的偶像与米拉蒙（Miramon）流光闪烁的图样乱纷纷地拥在水中，其间还有一颗白腻黏土的头颅，开合着牙关游动。阿莉亚诺拉（Alianora）金色的可爱，弗蕾迪丝（Freydis）幽暗的光华，还有那带着嘲弄的、塞斯弗拉（Sesphra）不朽的年轻微笑，各自显现一瞬，便即隐去。随后，妮亚菲尔（Niafer）的眼睛最后一回露出温和而诧异的非难，那些终究属于她而非曼努埃尔的孩子们的小脸随她一同漂逝；还有盔甲的辉光，轻快旌旗翻卷的一涌，闪亮的城堡角楼，以及曼努埃尔在任何地方曾经识得并钟爱过的一切光彩与颜色——唯独除了苏斯金德（Suskind）脸上那分明的红润白皙——仿佛都杂乱无章地漂过这片静水，像被一股潜流冲走的、明亮而破碎的残骸。

曼努埃尔叹了口气，几乎像是如释重负。「这么说来，」他说，「这便是那人的荒唐结局了——他处处被人推许为其时代最走运、最不顾廉耻的无赖！」

===Original===
“Yes, yes,” replied Grandfather Death, as slowly he untethered one by one the swine of Eubouleus. “Yes, it is indeed the end, since all your life is passing away there, to be beheld by your old eyes alone, for the last time. Thus I see nothing there but ordinary water, and I wonder what it is you find in that dark pool to keep you staring so.”

“I do not very certainly know,” said Manuel, “but, a little more and more mistily now, I seem to see drowned there all the loves and the desires and the adventures I had when I wore another body than this dilapidated gray body I now wear. And yet it is a deceiving water, for there, where it should reflect the remnants of the old fellow that is I, it shows, instead, the face of a young boy who is used to following after his own thinking and his own desires.”

“Certainly it is queer you should be saying that; for that, as everybody knows, was the favorite byword of your namesake the famous Count Manuel who is so newly dead in Poictesme yonder.﻿ ﻿… But what is that thing?”

===Chinese===
「是了，是了，」死神祖父答道，一面缓缓地逐一解开欧布勒斯那群猪的拴绳。「是了，这的的确确是终结，因为你的一生正在那边逝去，只留待你那双老眼独自将最后一遍观看。至于我，在那儿除了寻常的水什么也看不见；我不明白，你在那幽暗的水潭里究竟看见了什么，值得你这样目不转睛。」

「我不敢说确知，」曼努埃尔说，「只是此刻愈发朦朦胧胧了，我仿佛看见溺在那水里的，是我穿着另一副身躯——而非如今这副破败灰躯——时的全部情爱、欲望与奇遇。然而这是骗人的水：在那本该映出我这个老家伙残迹的地方，它显示出的，却是一个少年的脸，一个惯于追随自己的心思、自己的欲望的少年。」

「你竟说出这样的话，实在古怪；因为尽人皆知，那正是与你同名的那位大名鼎鼎的曼努埃尔伯爵生前最爱的口头禅——他此刻才在波伊捷斯那边死去，新丧未久呢。……可那是件什么东西？」

===Original===
Manuel raised from looking at the water just the handsome and florid young face which Manuel had seen reflected in the water. As his memories vanished, the tall boy incuriously wondered who might be the snub-nosed stranger that was waiting there with the miller’s pigs, and was pointing, as if in mild surprise, toward the two stones overgrown with moss and supporting a cross of old worm-eaten wood. For the stranger pointed at the unfinished, unsatisfying image which stood beside the pool of Haranton, wherein, they say, strange dreams engender.﻿ ﻿…

“What is that thing?” the stranger was asking, yet again.﻿ ﻿…

“It is the figure of a man,” said Manuel, “which I have modeled and remodeled, and cannot get exactly to my liking. So it is necessary that I keep laboring at it, until the figure is to my thinking and my desire.” Thus it was in the old days.

Explicit

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔从水面抬起头来，抬起来的，恰是他方才看见映在水中的那张俊美红润的少年面孔。随着记忆消逝，这高高的少年浑不在意地纳闷着：那个塌鼻子的陌生人是谁，竟同磨坊主的猪群一起在那儿等着，还仿佛带着温和的惊讶，指向那两块长满青苔、架着一个虫蛀旧木十字架的石头。原来那陌生人指着的，是立在哈兰顿（Haranton）水潭旁那座未完工、不称心的泥像——据说，古怪的梦便在这潭中孕育。……

「那是件什么东西？」那陌生人又问了一遍。……

「这是一座人像，」曼努埃尔说，「我塑了又塑、改了又改，却总不能合意到分毫不差。所以我必须继续在它上面劳作下去，直到这像合乎我的想法与愿望为止。」旧时便是如此。

全书终（Explicit）

## XXVI Deals with the Stork / 二十六 与鹳鸟打交道

===Original===
Now Manuel, driven out of Poictesme, went with his wife to Novogath, which had been for some seven years the capital of Philistia. Queen Stultitia, the sixtieth of that name to rule, received them friendlily. She talked alone with Manuel for a lengthy while, in a room that was walled with glazed tiles of faience and had its ceiling incrusted with moral axioms, everywhere affixed thereto in a light lettering of tin, so as to permit of these axioms being readily changed. Stultitia sat at a bronze reading-desk: she wore rose-colored spectacles, and at her feet dozed, for the while, her favorite plaything, a blind, small, very fat white bitch called Luck.

The Queen still thought that an alliance could be arranged against Duke Asmund as soon as public sentiment could be fomented in Philistia, but this would take time. “Have patience, my friend!” she said, and that was easy saying for a prosperous great lady sitting comfortably crowned and spectacled in her own palace, under her own chimneys and skylights and campaniles and domes and towers and battlements.

But in the meanwhile Manuel and Niafer had not so much as a cowshed wherein to exercise this recommended virtue. So Manuel made inquiries, and learned that Queen Freydis had taken up her abode on Sargyll, most remote of the Red Islands.

===Chinese===
这时，曼努埃尔被逐出了波伊捷斯，便携妻子前往诺沃加斯——七年来，那里一直是非利士的都城。斯图尔提蒂亚女王——以这个名号临朝的，已是第六十位——友善地接待了他们。她同曼努埃尔单独谈了许久；那间屋子的四壁镶砌着上过釉的彩瓷砖，天花板上则满嵌着道德格言，一条条用轻巧的锡字随处贴附其上，好教这些格言便于随时更换。斯图尔提蒂亚坐在一张青铜斜面书案后面：她戴着一副玫瑰色眼镜，脚边此刻正打着盹的，是她的心爱玩物——一只瞎眼的、娇小的、肥得可观的白母狗，唤作「运气」。

女王依旧认为，只消在非利士把舆情煽动起来，便可以结成一项对付阿斯蒙德公爵的同盟，只是这需要时日。「要有耐心，我的朋友！」她说。这话，出自一位富足昌泰的贵妇之口，自然来得容易——她端端正正戴着王冠、架着眼镜，舒舒服服坐在自家的宫殿里，头顶上是她自家的烟囱、天窗、钟楼、穹顶、塔楼和雉堞。

可是眼下，曼努埃尔和妮亚菲尔却连一间牛棚也没有，无处去修习这项受人推崇的德性。于是曼努埃尔四处打听，得知弗蕾迪丝女王已在萨吉尔安了家——红色群岛中最偏远的一座。

===Original===
“We will go to Freydis,” he told Niafer.

“But, surely, not after the way that minx probably believes you treated her?” said Niafer.

Manuel smiled the sleepy smile that was Manuel. “I know Freydis better than you know her, my dear.”

“Yes, but can you depend upon her?”

“I can depend upon myself, and that is more important.”

===Chinese===
「我们去找弗蕾迪丝。」他告诉妮亚菲尔。

「可是，那只小妖精多半认定你是怎样亏待过她的，这会儿还能上她门去么？」妮亚菲尔说。

曼努埃尔绽出那副只属于曼努埃尔的睡意惺忪的微笑。「亲爱的，我比你更了解弗蕾迪丝。」

「是，可她靠得住么？」

「我靠得住我自己，这才是更要紧的。」

===Original===
“But, Manuel, you have another dear friend in England; and in England, although the Lord knows I never want to lay eyes on her, we might at least be comfortable﻿—”

Manuel shook his head: “I am very fond of Alianora, because she resembles me as closely as it is possible for a woman to resemble a man. That makes two excellent reasons﻿—one for each of us, snip﻿—why we had better not go into England.”

So, in their homeless condition, they resolved to set out for Sargyll﻿—“to visit that other dear friend of yours,” as Niafer put it, in tones more eloquent than Manuel seemed quite to relish.

===Chinese===
「可是，曼努埃尔，你在英格兰还有一位亲爱的朋友；在英格兰——虽然天知道我一辈子都不想见她——我们至少还能过得舒适些——」

曼努埃尔摇了摇头：「我很喜欢阿莉亚诺拉，因为她同我相像的程度，已达一个女人所能像一个男人的极致。这就凑成了两条绝好的理由——咱们一人一条，小家伙——说明我们最好还是别踏进英格兰。」

就这样，无家可归的夫妇俩决意动身前往萨吉尔——「去拜望你那另一位亲爱的朋友」，妮亚菲尔如此措辞，那腔调意味深长，曼努埃尔听来未必十分受用。

===Original===
Dame Niafer, though, now began to complain that Manuel was neglecting her for all this statecraft and fighting and speechmaking and private conference with fine ladies; and she began to talk again about what a pity it was that she and Manuel would probably never have any children to be company for Niafer. Niafer complained rather often nowadays, about details which are here irrelevant: and she was used to lament with every appearance of sincerity that, in making the clay figure for Niafer to live in, Manuel should have been so largely guided by the elsewhere estimable qualities of innocence and imagination. It frequently put her, she said, to great inconvenience.

Now Manuel had been inquiring about this and that and the other since his arrival in Novogath, and so Manuel today replied with lordly assurance. “Yes, yes, a baby or two!” says Manuel. “I think myself that would be an excellent idea, while we are waiting for Queen Stultitia to make up her subjects’ minds, and have nothing else in particular to do﻿—”

“But, Manuel, you know perfectly well﻿—”

===Chinese===
不过，妮亚菲尔夫人如今开始抱怨起来，说曼努埃尔只顾着这一应的经国大计、征战杀伐、登台演说，还有同贵妇淑媛们的私下计议，把她冷落在一边；于是她又念叨起那桩憾事来：她同曼努埃尔多半永远不会有儿女来陪伴妮亚菲尔了。近来妮亚菲尔抱怨得相当勤，所怨的种种细节此处无关宏旨：她惯于带着再诚恳不过的神情哀叹说，想当年曼努埃尔捏塑那个供妮亚菲尔栖身的泥人时，实在不该那样一味听命于天真与想象力——这两种品质在别处倒也难能可贵。她说，这常常使她大感不便。

原来自打来到诺沃加斯，曼努埃尔便把这桩那桩、还有第三桩事情逐一打探过了，于是这一天，他以领主般的笃定答道：「是了，是了，生上一两个娃娃！我自个儿也觉得这是个绝妙的主意——趁我们还在等斯图尔提蒂亚女王拿定她众臣民的心思，眼下又没有别的什么正经事可做——」

「可是，曼努埃尔，你明明白白地知道——」

===Original===
“﻿—And I am sufficiently versed in the magic of the Apsarasas to be able to summon the stork, who by rare good luck is already indebted to me﻿—”

“What has the stork to do with this?”

“Why, it is he who must bring the babies to be company for you.”

“But, Manuel,” said Niafer, dubiously, “I do not believe that the people of Rathgor, or of Poictesme either, get their babies from the stork.”

===Chinese===
「——何况我对阿普萨拉斯天女们的法术也算精通，足以把鹳鸟召来；而难得的走运是，这位鹳鸟恰好还欠着我一份情——」

「鹳鸟同这事有什么相干？」

「哎呀，来给你作伴的娃娃，总得由他送来呀。」

「可是，曼努埃尔，」妮亚菲尔将信将疑地说，「我不信拉斯戈尔的百姓，也不信波伊捷斯的百姓，他们的娃娃是从鹳鸟那儿来的。」

===Original===
“Doubtless, like every country, they have their quaint local customs. We have no concern, however with these provincialities just now, for we are in Philistia. Besides, as you cannot well have forgotten, our main dependence is upon the half-promised alliance with Queen Stultitia, who is, as far as I can foresee, my darling, the only monarch anywhere likely to support us.”

“But what has Queen Stultitia to do with my having a baby?”

“Everything, dear snip. You must surely understand it is most important for one in my position to avoid in any way offending the sensibilities of the Philistines.”

“Still, Manuel, the Philistines themselves have babies, and I do not see how they could have conceivably objected to my having at any rate a very small one if only you had made me right﻿—”

===Chinese===
「无疑，同所有的国度一样，他们自有其古雅的乡土风俗。不过这些地方讲究眼下与我们无关，因为我们身在非利士。再说，你总不至于忘了：我们主要的指望，是斯图尔提蒂亚女王那半许下的同盟；而就我所能预见的，我亲爱的，普天之下可能扶持我们的君主，唯有她一位。」

「可斯图尔提蒂亚女王同我生娃娃有什么相干？」

「干系大着呢，亲爱的小家伙。你一定明白，对于我这等身份的人，万万不可开罪非利士人的敏感之处，这是顶顶要紧的。」

「话虽如此，曼努埃尔，非利士人自己也生娃娃；只要当初你把我造得对头，我看他们哪里想得到见怪我生上一个——哪怕顶小顶小的一个——」

===Original===
“Not at all! nobody objects to the baby in itself, now that you are a married woman. The point is that the babies of the Philistines are brought to them by the stork; and that even an allusion to the possibility of misguided persons obtaining a baby in any other way these Philistines consider to be offensive and lewd and lascivious and obscene.”

“Why, how droll of them! But are you sure of that, Manuel!”

“All their best-thought-of and most popular writers, my dear, are unanimous upon the point; and their Seranim have passed any number of laws, their oil-merchants have founded a guild, especially to prosecute such references. No, there is, to be sure, a dwindling sect which favors putting up with what babies you may find in the cabbage patch, but all really self-respecting people when in need of offspring arrange to be visited by the stork.”

“It is certainly a remarkable custom, but it sounds convenient if you can manage it,” said Niafer. “What I want is the baby, though, and of course we must try to get the baby in the manner of the Philistines, if you know that manner, for I am sure I have no wish to offend anybody.”

===Chinese===
「根本不是那回事！如今你是有夫之妇，谁也不会见怪娃娃本身。症结在于：非利士人的娃娃，一律由鹳鸟送来；哪怕仅仅影射一句，说误入歧途之辈或许能靠别的什么路子弄到娃娃，这些非利士人也要视之为冒犯的、淫邪的、猥亵的、污秽的。」

「哎呀，他们可真逗！不过这话你拿得准么，曼努埃尔！」

「他们最受敬重、最孚众望的作家们，亲爱的，在这一点上众口一词；他们的萨拉尼姆（Seranim，对非利士诸领主的称谓）通过了数不清的法令，他们的油商们还设立了行会，专办查究此类言辞的官司。当然，也有一派人丁渐稀的宗派，主张将就着收下你在白菜地里碰巧捡到的娃娃；但凡真正自爱的体面人，一旦需要子嗣，总要安排妥当，恭候鹳鸟光临。」

「这风俗确乎了不得，不过若是办得到，听着倒也方便。」妮亚菲尔说，「话又说回来，我要的是娃娃；既然你晓得那套办法，我们自然该照非利士人的方式去弄娃娃，因为我敢保我绝无意开罪任何人。」

===Original===
So Manuel prepared to get a baby in the manner preferred by the Philistines. He performed the suitable incantation, putting this and that together in the manner formerly employed by the Thessalian witches and sorcerers, and he cried aloud a very ancient if indecent charm from the old Latin, saying, as Queen Stultitia had told him to say, without any mock-modest mincing of words:

> Dictum est antiqua sandalio mulier habitavit,

 Quae multos pueros habuit tum ut potuit nullum

 Quod faciundum erat cognoscere. Sic Domina Anser.

===Chinese===
于是曼努埃尔着手按非利士人偏好的方式去弄一个娃娃。他行了合宜的祝咒，照昔日色萨利的巫婆与术士们所用的法子，把这料与那料拼合在一处，又高声诵起一道古拉丁文的咒诗——古则古矣，未免有伤风化——并且遵照斯图尔提蒂亚女王的嘱咐，毫不带半点假正经的吞吞吐吐，朗声念道：

> 「相传上古之世，有一妇人栖居于鞋屦之中，

她生养男孩众多，到后来任凭怎样使力，竟无一个

能辨认何事当为。鹅夫人如是云。」

===Original===
Then Manuel took from his breast-pocket a piece of blue chalk and five curious objects something like small black stars. With the chalk he drew upon the floor two parallel straight lines. Manuel walked on one of these chalk lines very carefully, then beckoned Niafer to him. Standing there, he put his arms about her and kissed her. Then he placed the five black stars in a row and went over to the next line.

The stork having been thus properly summoned, Manuel recalled to the bird the three wishes which had been promised when Manuel saved the stork’s life: and Manuel said that for each wish he would take a son fetched to him by the stork in the manner of the Philistines.

The stork thought it could be arranged. “Not this morning, though, as you suggest, for, indebted as I am to you, Dom Manuel, I am also a very busy bird. No, I have any number of orders that were put in months before yours, and I must follow system in my business, for you have no notion what elaborate and exact accounts are frequently required by the married men that receive invoices from me.”

“Come now,” says Manuel, “do you be accommodating, remembering how I once saved your life from the eagle, and my wife and I will order all our babies now, and spare you the trouble of keeping any accounts whatever, so far as we are concerned.”

===Chinese===
随后曼努埃尔从胸前口袋里取出一截蓝粉笔和五件古怪的物件，模样有点像黑色的小星星。他用粉笔在地板上画出两条平行的直线。曼努埃尔小心翼翼地沿着其中一条粉笔线走了一遍，然后招手唤妮亚菲尔到跟前来。他站在那里，伸出双臂搂住她，吻了她。随后他把那五颗黑星排成一列，走到另一条线上去了。

鹳鸟既已这样中规中矩地召到，曼努埃尔便向它提起当年曼努埃尔救它性命时曾经许下的那三个愿望：曼努埃尔说，每个愿望，他都要折成一个由鹳鸟按非利士人方式送来的儿子。

鹳鸟想，这倒可以安排。「不过，照您提议的今早便办，恕难从命——曼努埃尔大人，我虽欠着您的情，却也是一只忙得不可开交的鸟。不，我手头有数不清的订单，都比您的早了好几个月；我办这份营生总得讲个章程，因为您全然想象不到，那些从我手里收账单的已婚男人，动辄要求何等繁缛精确的账目。」

「行了，」曼努埃尔说，「你就行个方便罢，想想当初是我从鹰爪下救了你的性命；我同我妻子这就把我们所有的娃娃一气订齐，单我们这一户而论，也省得你记任何账目、费任何周章。」

===Original===
“Oh, if you care to deal with such wholesale irregularity, and have no more consideration than to keep casting old debts in my bill, I might stretch a point in order to be rid of you,” the stork said, sighing.
