「得了，你可别想把太多的担子压到雅赫维（Jahveh）肩上，」扎尔-普蒂察宽容地抗议道，「因为雅赫维只造过一个人，而且再不曾干过第二回。造那头一个人的光景，我记得清清楚楚，因为我是在前一天早晨被造出来的，领受的指令是在天空敞开的穹苍之中、飞翔于大地之上，所以始末我都看见了。是的，雅赫维确是在第六天造了头一个人。而我未置一词批评。因为，连着干了将近整整一星期、又造出了那么多真正要紧的东西之后，任何一位从事创造的艺术家，到了第六天不在最得心应手的状态，都不该挨责备。」

===Original===
“And did you happen to notice, sir,” asks Manuel, hopefully, “by what method animation was given to Adam?”

“No, he was drying out in the sun when I first saw him, with Gabriel sitting at his feet, playing on a flageolet: and naturally I did not pay any particular attention to such foolishness.”

“Well, well, I do not assert that the making of men is the highest form of art, yet, none the less, a geas is upon me to make myself a very splendid and admirable young man.”

“But why should you be wasting your small portion of breath and strength? To what permanent use could one put a human being even if the creature were virtuous and handsome to look at? Ah, Manuel, you have not seen them pass, as I have seen them pass in swarms, with their wars and their reforms and their great causes, and leaving nothing but their bones behind them.”

===Chinese===
「那么您可曾碰巧留意到，先生，」曼努埃尔满怀指望地问道，「往亚当（Adam）身子里灌注生气，用的是个什么法子？」

「没有。我头一回看见他时，他正在太阳地里晾着，加百列（Gabriel）坐在他脚边，吹着一支哨笛：对这样的蠢事，我自然不曾特别放在心上。」

「嗯，嗯，我并不断言造人是艺术的至高形式，然而，尽管如此，一道誓约加在我身上，要我把自身造就成一个非常辉煌出众、令人叹赏的年轻人。」

「可你又何必耗损你那一小份的气息和气力呢？就算那东西品行端方、相貌堂堂，人又能拿一个活人派什么经久不变的用场？啊，曼努埃尔，你不曾见过他们成群结队地过去——我可见过，带着他们的战争、他们的改革、他们的伟大事业，身后除了骨头，什么也不曾留下。」

===Original===
“Yes, yes, to you, at your age, who were old when Nineveh was planned, it must seem strange; and I do not know why my mother desired that I should make myself a splendid and admirable young man. But the geas is upon me.”

The Zhar-Ptitza sighed. “Certainly these feminine whims are not easily explained. Yet your people have some way of making brand-new men and women of all kinds. I am sure of this, for otherwise the race would have been extinct a great while since at the rate they kill one another. And perhaps they do adhere to Jahveh’s method, and make fresh human beings out of earth, for, now I think of it, I have seen the small, recently completed ones, who looked exactly like red clay.”

“It is undeniable that babies do have something of that look,” assented Manuel. “So then, at least, you think I may be working in the proper medium?”

“It seems plausible, because I am certain your people are not intelligent enough to lay eggs, nor could, of course, such an impatient race succeed in getting eggs hatched. At all events, they have undoubtedly contrived some method or other, and you might find out from the least foolish of them about that method.”

===Chinese===
「是，是，在你，在你这把年纪——尼尼微（Nineveh）才画图纸的时候你就老了——这必定显得古怪；我也不明白，我母亲为什么盼望我把自己造就成一个辉煌出众、令人叹赏的年轻人。可誓约毕竟加在我身上。」

扎尔-普蒂察叹了口气。「这些女性化的奇想，自然不容易解释明白。不过你们这一族人自有某种门道，能造出各色各样崭新的男人和女人。这一点我有把握，否则照他们自相残杀的那个速率，这种族老早就绝了种。而且，说不定他们当真是恪守雅赫维的法子，用泥土造出新鲜的人类，因为——如今我想起来了——我见过那些新近完工的小号产品，看上去同红黏土一模一样。」

「婴孩们确有几分那副模样，这无可否认，」曼努埃尔附和道。「那么，至少您是觉得，我干这门手艺，用的材料算是使得对了？」

「看来说得通，因为我敢肯定，你们这一族聪明不到会下蛋的地步；当然啦，这样一副急脾气的种族，也断然不能把孵蛋这件事办成。无论如何，他们无疑已经鼓捣出了某种法子，那法子，你不妨去问问人类里最不蠢的那一个。」

===Original===
“Who, then, is the least foolish of mankind?”

“Probably King Helmas of Albania, for it was prophesied by me a great while ago that he would become the wisest of men if ever he could come by one of my shining white feathers, and I hear it reported he has done so.”

“Sir,” said Manuel, dubiously, “I must tell you in confidence that the feather King Helmas has is not yours, but was plucked from the wing of an ordinary goose.”

“Does that matter?” asked the Zhar-Ptitza. “I never prophesied, of course, that he actually would find one of my shining white feathers, because all my feathers are red and gold and purple.”

===Chinese===
「那么，人类里最不蠢的是谁呢？」

「多半是阿尔巴尼亚（Albania）的黑尔马斯国王，因为许久以前我曾预言过：他若能得到我一根雪亮的白羽毛，便会成为人中的至智者；而我听说，据报道他已经到手了。」

「先生，」曼努埃尔将信将疑地说，「我得私下里告诉您：黑尔马斯国王手里那根羽毛不是您的，是从一只寻常鹅的翅膀上拔下来的。」

「那有什么要紧？」扎尔-普蒂察问。「我当然从不曾预言他当真能寻见我一根雪亮的白羽毛，因为我全身的羽毛都是红的、金的、紫的。」

===Original===
“But how can there be any magic in a goose-feather?”

“There is this magic, that, possessing it, King Helmas has faith in, and has stopped bothering about, himself.”

“Is not to bother about yourself the highest wisdom?”

“Oh, no! Oh, dear me, no! I merely said it is the highest of which man is capable.”

===Chinese===
「可是一根鹅毛里，能有什么魔法呢？」

「魔法就在这里头：得了这根羽毛，黑尔马斯国王便对自家有了信心，也不再为自家烦心了。」

「不再为自家烦心，难道不就是最高的智慧么？」

「哦，不是！哦，我的老天，不是！我不过说，那是人的能力所及的最高一等罢了。」

===Original===
“But the sages and philosophers, sir, that had such fame in the old time, and made the maxims for you birds! Why, did King Solomon, for example, rise no higher than that?”

“Yes, yes, to be sure!” said the Zhar-Ptitza, sighing again, “now that was a sad error. The poor fellow was endowed with, just as an experiment, considerable wisdom. And it caused him to perceive that a man attains to actual contentment only when he is drunk or when he is engaged in occupations not very decorously described. So Sulieman-ben-Daoud gave over all the rest of his time to riotous living and to coeducational enterprises. It was logic, but it led to a most expensive seraglio and to a very unbecoming appearance, and virtually wrecked the man’s health. Yes, that was the upshot of one of you being endowed with actual wisdom, just as an experiment, to see what would come of it: so the experiment, of course, has never been repeated. But of living persons, I dare assert that you will find King Helmas appreciably freed from a thousand general delusions by his one delusion about himself.”

“Very well, then,” says Manuel. “I suspect a wilful paradox and a forced cynicism in much of what you have said, but I shall consult with King Helmas about human life and about the figure I have to make in the world.”

===Chinese===
「可是先生，古时那些享有赫赫大名的圣贤与哲人——那些替你们鸟类编造格言的人呢！哎呀，难道连所罗门王（King Solomon）这样的人，也没能爬得比这更高么？」

「是，是，可不是嘛！」扎尔-普蒂察说着，又叹了一口气，「那是一桩可悲的谬误。那可怜的家伙被赋予过——纯粹当作一次实验——相当可观的智慧。这智慧叫他看明白：人唯有在醉乡里，或在从事那几种不大好体面形容的行当时，方能达到真正的满足。于是苏莱曼·本·达乌德（Sulieman-ben-Daoud）便把余下的光阴，尽数交给了纵情行乐和男女共学的事业。这是合乎逻辑的，只是它招来了一座靡费惊人的后宫、一副很失体统的形貌，还差不多毁掉了那人的健康。是了，这便是给你们中间的一个赋予真正智慧的下场——纯当作实验，看看会生出什么结果：所以那实验自然再不曾重复过。至于在世的人，我敢断言，你会发现黑尔马斯国王单凭他关于自家的那一个错觉，就显明地从一千种普遍的错觉里解脱出来了。」

「很好，那么，」曼努埃尔说。「您这番话里，我疑心颇多任性的悖论和勉强的玩世不恭；不过，关于人生，也关于我必须在这世上立起的那个形象，我自去同黑尔马斯国王商量。」

===Original===
So they bid each other farewell, and the Zhar-Ptitza picked up his nest of cassia and sprigs of incense, and flew away with it: and as he rose in the air the Zhar-Ptitza cried, “Fine feathers make fine birds.”

“But that is not the true proverb, sir,” Manuel called up toward the resplendent creature, “and such perversions too, they tell me, are a mark of would-be cleverness.”

“So it may seem to you now, my lad, but time is a very transforming fairy. Therefore do you wait until you are older,” the bird replied, from on high, “and then you will know better than to doubt my cry or to repeat it.”

===Chinese===
于是他们彼此道了别，扎尔-普蒂察衔起他那肉桂枝和熏香嫩枝筑成的巢，带着它飞走了：他升到空中时，扎尔-普蒂察啼道：「丽羽成佳禽。」

「可这不是那句真正的谚语，先生，」曼努埃尔朝那只光华灿烂的生灵仰面喊道，「况且人家还告诉我，这样的窜改，也是自诩聪明的一种印记。」

「此刻在你看来，兴许是这么回事，我的孩子；可时间是一位非常能改造人的仙女。所以你且等到上了年纪，」那鸟儿自高处答道，「那时你自会明白事理，不致怀疑我的啼鸣，也不致复述它。」

## XVII Magic of the Image-Makers / 十七 造像者的魔法

===Original===
It was presently noised abroad that Queen Freydis of Audela had become a human woman; and thereafter certain enchanters came to Upper Morven, to seek her counsel and her favor and the aid of Schamir. These were the enchanters, Manuel was told, who made images, to which they now and then contrived﻿—nobody seemed to know quite how, and least of all did the thaumaturgists themselves﻿—to impart life.

Once Manuel went with Freydis into a dark place where some of these magic-workers were at labor. By the light of a charcoal fire, clay images were ruddily discernible; before these the enchanters moved unhumanly clad, and doing things which, mercifully perhaps, were veiled from Manuel by the peculiarly perfumed obscurity.

As Manuel entered the gallery one of the magic-workers was chanting shrilly in the darkness below. “It is the unfinished Rune of the Blackbirds,” says Freydis, in a whisper.

Below them the troubled wailing continued:

===Chinese===
不久便有风声四下传开，说奥德拉的弗蕾迪丝女王已经变成一个人间的女人；随后便有几位魔法师来到上莫尔文，来寻求她的指点、她的眷顾和沙米尔的助力。据说——曼努埃尔听人这样讲——这些魔法师专事造像，而且时不时还能设法（似乎谁也说不清究竟是怎样设的法，最说不清的恰恰是那些奇术师本人）赋与造像生命。

有一回，曼努埃尔随弗蕾迪丝走进一处幽暗的所在，几个施法者正在那里劳作。借着炭火的光，泥像泛着微红，依稀可辨；魔法师们一身非人的装束，在像前移动，做着种种事情——那些事情，或许总算慈悲，全叫一股异香熏成的昏暗从曼努埃尔眼前遮去了。

曼努埃尔走进廊道时，下面的黑暗里有一个施法者正尖声吟诵着。「这是未完成的黑鸟咒歌（Rune of the Blackbirds），」弗蕾迪丝低声道。

他们脚下，那阵纷乱的哀吟继续着：

===Original===
“﻿—Crammed and squeezed, so entombed (on some wager I hazard), in spite of scared squawking and mutter, after the fashion that lean-faced Rajah dealt with trapped heroes, once, in Calcutta. Dared you break the crust and bullyrag ’em﻿—hot, fierce and angry, what wide beaks buzz plain Saxon as ever spoke Witenagemot! Yet, singing, they sing as no white bird does (where none rears phoenix) as near perfection as nature gets, or, if scowls bar platitude, notes for which there is no rejection in banks whose coinage﻿—oh, neat!﻿—is gratitude.”

Said, in the darkness, another enchanter:

“But far from their choiring the high King sat, in a gold-faced vest and a gold-laced hat, counting heaped monies, and dreaming of more francs and sequins and Louis d’or. Meanwhile the Queen on that fateful night, though avowing her lack of all appetite, was still at table, where, rumor said, she was smearing her seventh slice of bread (thus each turgescible rumor thrives at court) with gold from the royal hives. Through the slumberous pare, under arching trees, to her labors went singing the maid Dénise﻿—”

===Chinese===
「——塞得满满，挤得紧紧，就此入了殓（我揣度是打了一场赌），不顾一片惊慌的嘎嘎与咕哝，学的正是当年加尔各答那瘦脸的罗阇（Rajah）发落落网好汉的手段。你敢打破酥皮、去撩拨他们么——滚烫、凶悍、怒气冲冲，那一张张宽阔的鸟喙嗡嗡讲出的地道撒克逊话，赛得过贤人会议（Witenagemot）上开口说过的任何言语！然而唱起来，他们唱得没有一只白鸟能及（在那无人豢养凤凰的地方），唱到大自然所能企及的近乎圆满；或者——倘若皱眉拦得住陈词滥调——唱出这样一些音符：在以感激——啊，多爽利的币制！——为通货的银行里，这些音符永无退票之虞。」

黑暗中，另一个魔法师开腔道：

「远离着他们的合唱，尊高的国王端坐着，穿一件金面的马甲，戴一顶金绦的帽子，数着堆积起来的钱财，梦想着更多的法郎、塞奎因和路易金币。与此同时，在那个决定命运的夜晚，王后虽然一口声言自己毫无胃口，却还坐在餐桌旁——风传说，她正往第七片面包上涂抹（宫廷里每一则越滚越胀的风传都这般发迹）王家蜂巢里出产的黄金。穿过睡意沉沉的梨树林，在穹拱的树下，女仆德尼丝（Dénise）一路唱着歌，往她的活计去了——」

===Original===
A third broke in here, saying:

“And she sang of how subtle and bitter and bright was a beast brought forth, that was clad with the splendor and light of the cold fair ends of the north, like a fleshly blossom more white than augmenting tempests that go, with thunder for weapon, to ravage the strait waste fastness of snow. She sang how that all men on earth said, whether its mistress at morn went forth or waited till night﻿—whether she strove through the foam and wreckage of shallow and firth, or couched in glad fields of corn, or fled from all human delight﻿—that thither it likewise would roam.”

Now a fourth began:

“Thus sang Dénise, what while the siccant sheets and coverlets that pillowed kingly dreams, with curious undergarbs of royalty, she neatly ranged: and dreamed not of that doom which waited, yet unborn, to strike men dumb with perfect awe. As when the seventh wave poises, and sunlight cleaves it through and through with gold, as though to gild oncoming death for him that sees foredoomed﻿—and, gasping, sees death high and splendid!﻿—while the tall wave bears down, and its shattering makes an end of him: thus poised the sable bird while one might count one, two, and three, and four, and five, and six, but hardly seven﻿—”

===Chinese===
第三个在这里插进来，说道：

「她唱到一头降生的走兽何其精微、何其凛冽、何其皎洁，它周身披着北方那清冷而美好的尽头处的光华与辉光，像一朵血肉的繁花，比那些以雷霆作兵器、前去蹂躏窄狭荒芜的雪之壁垒的、愈涨愈高的风暴更加皎白。她唱到世上之人无不说：不论它的女主人是清早出行还是守到入夜——不论她是奋力跋涉过浅滩与海湾的泡沫与残骸，还是欣然卧在谷田里，还是躲避着人间一切的欢悦——它也会照样游荡到那里去。」

这时第四个开了头：

「德尼丝这样唱着，一面把那些承垫过王家梦寐的、晾得干干的床单与被褥，连同王家的种种奇巧贴身衣物，收拾得整整齐齐：她不曾梦到那桩还候在未生之中、专等着以完美的敬畏把人惊得哑口无言的厄运。正如第七个浪头高高悬起，阳光用金子把它从里到外劈透，仿佛给那眼见在劫难逃的人迎面而来的死亡镀上一层金——他喘息着，眼看死亡高踞而辉煌！——随即高浪压将下来，它的一记粉碎便结果了他：那黑色的鸟也正这样悬停着，容人数得一、二，又三、四，又五、六，却难得数到七——」

===Original===
So they continued; but Manuel listened to no more. “What is the meaning of all this?” he asked, of Freydis.

“It is an experimental incantation,” she replied, “in that it is a bit of unfinished magic for which the proper words have not yet been found: but between now and a while they will be stumbled on, and then this rune will live perpetually, surviving all those rhymes that are infected with thought and intelligent meanings such as are repugnant to human nature.”

“Are words, then, so important and enduring?”

===Chinese===
他们就这样继续下去；曼努埃尔却不再听了。「这一切究竟是什么意思？」他问弗蕾迪丝。

「这是一篇试验性的咒文，」她答道，「说它试验，是因为它是一段尚未完工的法术，合式的字眼还没有找着：不过或早或晚，总会有人磕磕绊绊撞出这些字眼来，到那时这首咒歌便会永生不死，压倒一切染上了思想与聪明含义的韵文——那些东西，是与人性相抵触的。」

「那么，词语竟是这样要紧、这样经久么？」

===Original===
“Why, Manuel, I am surprised at you! In what else, pray, does man differ from the other animals except in that he is used by words?”

“Now I would have said that words are used by men.”

“There is give and take, of course, but in the main man is more subservient to words than they are to him. Why, do you but think of such terrible words as religion and duty and love, and patriotism and art, and honor and common-sense, and of what these tyrannizing words do to and make of people!”

“No, that is chop-logic: for words are only transitory noises, whereas man is the child of God, and has an immortal spirit.”

“Yes, yes, my dearest, I know you believe that, and I think it is delightfully quaint and sweet of you. But, as I was saying, a man has only the body of an animal to get experiences in, and the brain of an animal to think them over with, so that the thoughts and opinions of the poor dear must remain always those of a more or less intelligent animal. But his words are very often magic, as you will comprehend by and by when I have made you the greatest of image-makers.”

===Chinese===
「嗳，曼努埃尔，我真想不到你会问这个！请你倒说说看，人跟其余的动物还有什么别的不同——除了人是被词语驱使的这一点？」

「要照我说，倒是词语被人使用。」

「有来有往，自然免不了，但大体而论，人伺候词语，比词语伺候人伺候得更周到。唉，你只消想想那些可怕的词——宗教啦、责任啦、爱啦，还有爱国和艺术，还有荣誉和常识——再想想这些称王称霸的词语对人们做下了什么，把人们捏成了什么样子！」

「不，这是诡辩：词语不过是一阵就散的声响，人却是上帝的孩子，怀着一个不死的灵魂。」

「是，是，我最亲爱的，我知道你信这个，而且我觉得你这样信，实在古雅得可爱、甜蜜得可爱。不过我方才要说的是：一个人只有一副兽类的身体可以用来阅历世事，只有一颗兽类的脑子可以用来把世事想一遍，于是这位可怜的宝贝，他的思想和见解便永远只能是一个多少有点聪明的动物的思想和见解。可他的词语却往往是魔法——这个你往后自会明白，等我把你造就成造像者中最伟大的那一位。」

===Original===
“Well, well, but we can let that wait a bit,” said Manuel.

And thereafter Manuel talked with Freydis, confessing that the appearance of these magic-workers troubled Manuel. He had thought it, he said, an admirable thing to make images that lived, until he saw and considered the appearance of these habitual makers of images. They were an ugly and rickety, short-tempered tribe, said Manuel: they were shiftless, spiteful, untruthful, and in everyday affairs not far from imbecile: they plainly despised all persons who could not make images, and they apparently detested all those who could. With Manuel they were particularly high and mighty, assuring him that he was only a prosperous and affected pseudo-magician, and that the harm done by the self-styled thaumaturgist was apt to be very great indeed. What sort of models, then, were these insane, mud-moulding solitary wasps for a tall lad to follow after? And if Manuel acquired their arts (he asked in conclusion), would he acquire their traits?

“The answer is perhaps no, and not impossibly yes,” replied Freydis. “For by the ancient Tuyla mystery they extract that which is best in them to inform their images, and this is apt to leave them empty of virtue. But I would have you consider that their best endures, whereas that which is best in other persons is obliterated on some battlefield or mattress or gallows That is why I have been thinking that this afternoon﻿—”

===Chinese===
「唔，唔，这件事我们且缓一缓，」曼努埃尔说。

此后曼努埃尔便同弗蕾迪丝谈起了这事，承认这些施法者的模样叫他心里不安。他说，他原以为造出活生生的像是一桩可羡的事，直到他亲眼看到、又细心思量了这些惯于造像之辈的模样。他们是一族又丑又孱弱、动辄动火的生灵，曼努埃尔说：他们好逸恶劳、心怀怨毒、满口谎话，料理起日常事务来同白痴相去无几：他们明明白白地蔑视一切不会造像的人，又分明憎恶一切会造像的人。对曼努埃尔他们格外地居高临下，一口咬定他不过是个走运而做作的冒牌魔法师，还说这位自封的奇术师闯下的祸害，往往着实不小。那么，让一个高挑的小伙子去追摹的，竟是这群疯疯癫癫、捏泥塑像的独居胡蜂么？倘若曼努埃尔学来了他们的手艺（他末了这样问），会不会也沾上他们的德性？

「答案么，或许是『不会』，也未尝不会还是『会』，」弗蕾迪丝答道。「因为凭着古老的图伊拉秘法，他们把自己身上最好的东西提炼出来，灌注进他们的造像里去，而这一来，他们自己往往就被掏空了德性。不过我要你想一想：他们最好的那部分得以长存，而旁人身上最好的那部分，却是在哪一处战场上、哪一张床垫上或哪一座绞架上被抹掉的。所以我今天下午一直在想——」

===Original===
“No, we will let that wait a bit, for I must turn this over in my mind,” said Manuel, “and my mature opinion about this matter must be expressed later.”

But while his thoughts were on the affair his fingers made him droll small images of ten of the image-makers, which he set aside unquickened. Freydis smiled at these caricatures, and asked when Manuel would give them life.

“Oh, in due time,” he said, “and then their antics may be diverting. But I perceive that this old Tuyla magic is practiced at great price and danger, so that I am in no hurry to practice any more of it. I prefer to enjoy that which is dearer and better.”

===Chinese===
「不，这件事我们且缓一缓，因为我得把这桩事在心里翻来覆去掂量掂量，」曼努埃尔说，「我对此事的成熟见解，容日后另行发表。」

然而他的心思还盘在这桩事上的时候，他的手指已经自作主张，替他捏出了十个造像者的滑稽小像，他把它们搁在一边，没有赋与生气。弗蕾迪丝瞧着这些漫画像直笑，问曼努埃尔几时给它们生命。

「哦，到了时候自然会给，」他说，「到那时他们的滑稽戏说不定很逗乐。不过我看明白了，这门古老的图伊拉魔法，施展起来代价奇大、凶险奇多，所以我并不急着再多施展。我宁可去享受那更亲爱、更美好的东西。」

===Original===
“And what can be dearer and better?”

“Youth,” Manuel answered, “and you.”

Queen Freydis was now a human woman in all things, so this reply delighted her hearing if not her reason. “Do these two possessions content you, king of my heart?” she asked him very fondly.

“No,” Manuel said, gazing out across Morven at the cloud-dappled ridges of the Taunenfels, “nor do I look ever to be contented in this world of men.”

===Chinese===
「还有什么东西更亲爱、更美好？」

「青春，」曼努埃尔答道，「还有你。」

弗蕾迪丝女王如今事事都是一个人间的女人了，所以这句答话叫她的耳朵欢喜——即便她的理智并不欢喜。「这两样家当，你可称心了么，我心尖上的王？」她万分温存地问他。

「不，」曼努埃尔说，目光越过莫尔文，望着云斑点点的一道道陶嫩费尔斯（Taunenfels）山梁，「在这世人的世界里，我也从不指望能有知足的一天。」

===Original===
“Indeed the run of men are poor thin-minded creatures, Manuel﻿—”

He answered, moodily:

“But I cannot put aside the thought that these men ought to be my fellows and my intimates. Instead, I who am a famed champion go daily in distrust, almost in fear, of these incomprehensible and shatter-pated beings. To every side there is a feeble madness over-busy about long-faced nonsense from which I recoil, who must conceal this shrinking always. There is no hour in my life but I go armored in reserve and in small lies, and in my armor I am lonely. Freydis, you protest deep love for this well-armored Manuel, but what wisdom will reveal to you, or to me either, just what is Manuel? Oh, but I am puzzled by the impermanence and the loneliness and the impotence of this Manuel! Dear Freydis, do not love my body nor my manner of speaking, nor any of the ways that I have in the flesh, for all these transiencies are mortgaged to the worms. And that thought also is a grief﻿—”

“Let us not speak of these things! Let us not think of anything that is horrid, but only of each other!”

“But I cannot put aside the thought that I, who for the while exist in this mortgaged body, cannot ever get out to you. Freydis, there is no way in which two persons may meet in this world of men: we can but exchange, from afar, despairing friendly signals, in the sure knowledge they will be misinterpreted. So do we pass, each coming out of a strange woman’s womb, each parodied by the flesh of his parents, each passing futilely, with incommunicative gestures, toward the womb of a strange grave: and in this jostling we find no comradeship. No soul may travel upon a bridge of words. Indeed there is no word for my foiled huge desire to love and to be loved, just as there is no word for the big, the not quite comprehended thought which is moving in me at this moment. But that thought also is a grief﻿—”

===Chinese===
「说实在的，寻常世人尽是些心智贫薄的可怜造物，曼努埃尔——」

他怏怏地答道：

「可我没法撇开这样一个念头：这些人本该是我的同类、我的至交。可如今，我这个名满天下的豪杰，日日夜夜怀着猜忌、几乎怀着恐惧，同这些不可理喻、脑壳开裂的生灵周旋。四面八方尽是一场场有气无力的癫狂，忙忙碌碌地张罗着板起面孔的胡话，我见了便退避三舍——还得时时刻刻把这退避遮掩起来。我这一生没有一个时辰不是披挂着矜持与小小谎言的铠甲度过的，而在这铠甲里，我是孤独的。弗蕾迪丝，你声声说你深爱着这个铠甲严实的曼努埃尔，可是有什么智慧能向你、或者向我揭示，曼努埃尔究竟是什么？唉，这曼努埃尔的朝不保夕、这孤独、这无能为力，真叫我困惑！亲爱的弗蕾迪丝，不要爱我的身体，不要爱我说话的腔调，也不要爱我这肉身里的任何做派，因为这一切转瞬即逝的东西，都已经抵押给了蛆虫。而这个念头，也是一桩伤悲——」

「别说这些了！什么可怕的事情都不要想，只想我们彼此！」

「可我没法撇开这样一个念头：我——暂且栖身在这具抵押出去的身体里的我——永远也走不到你那里去。弗蕾迪丝，在这世人的世界里，两个人没有任何法子真正相遇：我们只能远远地交换绝望而友好的信号，并且心里笃定，这些信号必定遭到误解。我们就这样擦身而过：各个从一个陌生女人的子宫里出来，各个被父母的血肉滑稽地描摹着，各个打着手势——那手势传不出半点意思——徒然地走向一座陌生坟墓的子宫：在这场推推搡搡里，我们寻不着半点情谊。没有一个灵魂能从词语搭成的桥上走过去。真的，我这份受挫的、要去爱又渴望被爱的巨大心愿，世上没有一个词可以称呼，正如此刻在我胸中涌动的这个庞大的、尚未全然领会的念头，也没有一个词可以称呼。可那个念头，也是一桩伤悲——」

===Original===
Manuel was still looking at the changing green and purple of the mountains and at the tall clouds trailing northward. The things that he viewed yonder were all gigantic and lovely, and they seemed not to be very greatly bothering about humankind.

Then Freydis said: “Let us not think too much, dear, in our youth. It is such a waste of the glad time, and of the youth that will not ever be returning﻿—”

“But I cannot put aside the thought that it will never be the true Manuel whom you will love or even know of, nor can I dismiss the knowledge that these human senses, through which alone we may obtain any knowledge of each other, are lying messengers. What can I ever be to you except flesh and a voice? Nor is this the root of my sorrowing, dear Freydis. For I know that my distrust of all living creatures﻿—oh, even of you, dear Freydis, when I draw you closest﻿—must always be as a wall between us, a low, lasting, firm-set wall which we can never pull down. And I know that I am not really a famed champion, but only a forlorn and lonely inmate of the doubtful castle of my body; and that I, who know not truly what I am, must die in this same doubt and loneliness, behind the strong defences of posturing and bluntness and jovial laughter which I have raised for my protecting. And that thought also is a grief.”

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔仍旧望着山间变幻的青青紫紫，望着向北曳行的高高的云。他在那边望见的种种事物，全都硕大而可爱，而且看上去并不怎样为人类大操其心。

这时弗蕾迪丝说道：「亲爱的，趁我们年轻，可别想得太多。这太浪费欢喜的光阴，也太浪费那一去不回的青春了——」

「可我没法撇开这样一个念头：你所爱的、甚至你所知晓的，永远不会是真正的曼努埃尔；我也挥不去这份知见：我们凭以彼此了解的唯一门径——这些人类的感官——尽是撒谎的信使。除了皮肉和一副嗓音，我还能是你的什么？而这还不是我悲伤的根由，亲爱的弗蕾迪丝。因为我知道，我对一切生灵的不信任——唉，连对你也是这样，亲爱的弗蕾迪丝，在我把你搂得最紧的时候——必得永远像一堵墙横在我们中间，一堵低矮、持久、砌得牢牢的墙，我们永远也拆它不掉。我还知道，我其实并不是什么名满天下的豪杰，只是我这肉身的疑宫里一个凄凉孤独的囚徒；我知道，我这个连自己真正是什么都未必知道的人，必将死在这同样的疑惧与孤独里，躲在我为护卫自己而筑起的坚固防线——装腔作势、直头直脑、欢声大笑——的后面。而这个念头，也是一桩伤悲。」

===Original===
Now Manuel was as Freydis had not ever seen him. She wondered at him, she was perturbed by this fine lad’s incomprehensible dreariness, with soft red willing lips so near: and her dark eyes were bent upon him with a beautiful and tender yearning which may not be told.

“I do not understand you, my dearest,” said she, who was no longer the high Queen of Audela, but a mortal woman. “It is true that all the world about us is a false seeming, but you and I are real and utterly united, for we have no concealments from each other. I am sure that no two people could be happier than we are, nor better suited. And certainly such morbid notions are not like you, who, as you said yourself, only the other day, are naturally so frank and downright.”

Now Manuel’s thoughts came back from the clouds and the green and purple of the mountains. He looked at her very gravely for an instant or two. He laughed morosely. He said, “There!”

“But, dearest, you are strange and not yourself﻿—”

“Yes, yes!” says Manuel, kissing her, “for the moment I had forgotten to be frank and downright, and all else which you expect of me. Now I am my old candid, jovial, blunt self again, and I shall not worry you with such silly notions any more. No, I am Manuel: I follow after my own thinking and my own desire; and if to do that begets loneliness I must endure it.”

===Chinese===
此刻的曼努埃尔，是弗蕾迪丝从不曾见过的模样。她望着他，心里讶异，为这俊美少年那不可理喻的阴郁而不安——那两片温柔红润、殷切可亲的嘴唇近在咫尺——她那双幽深的眼睛凝望着他，里头含着一种美好而温存的渴慕，非言语所能形容。

「我不明白你，我最亲爱的，」她说——她已不再是奥德拉至高的女王，只是一个凡人的女子。「我们周遭的整个世界诚然是一场虚假的表象，可你我是真实的，是浑然一体的，因为我们彼此之间没有半点隐瞒。我确信，没有哪两个人能比我们更幸福，也没有哪两个人比我们更相配。再说，这样病态的念头实在不像你——你自己前两天还说过，你天生就是这样坦率、这样直来直去。」

这时，曼努埃尔的思绪从云端、从山间的青紫里收了回来。他神色极为郑重地看了她一两瞬。他郁郁地笑了。他说：「这就对了！」

「可是，最亲爱的，你这样子好生古怪，不像平日的你了——」

「是，是！」曼努埃尔说着，吻了吻她，「方才那会儿，我忘了要坦率直来直去，忘了你所指望于我的一切别的德性。现在我又变回我那个素来坦白、快活、直性的自己了，再不用这类傻念头来搅你了。不，我就是曼努埃尔：我追随着我自己的思想和我自己的欲愿；倘若这样做会生出孤独，我也只好受着。」

## XVIII Manuel Chooses / 十八 曼努埃尔的抉择

===Original===
“But I cannot understand,” said Freydis, on a fine day in September, “how it is that, now the power of Schamir is in your control, and you have the secret of giving life to your images, you do not care to use either the secret or the talisman. For you make no more images, you are always saying, ‘No, we will let that wait a bit,’ and you do not even quicken the ten caricatures of the image-makers which you have already modeled.”

“Life will be given to these in due time,” said Manuel, “but that time is not yet come. Meanwhile, I avoid practice of the old Tuyla mystery for the sufficing reason that I have seen the result it has on the practitioner. A geas was upon me to make a figure in the world, and so I modeled and loaned life to such a splendid gay young champion as was to my thinking and my desire. Thus my geas, I take it, is discharged, and a thing done has an end. Heaven may now excel me by creating a larger number of living figures than I, but preeminence in this matter is not a question of arithmetic﻿—”

“Ah, yes, my squinting boy has all the virtues, including that of modesty!”

“Well, but I have seen my notion embodied, seen it take breath, seen it depart from Morven in all respects, except for a little limping﻿—which, do you know, I thought rather graceful?﻿—in well-nigh all respects, I repeat, quite indistinguishable from the embodied notions of that master craftsman whom some call Ptha, and others Jahveh, and others Abraxas, and yet others Koshchei the Deathless. In fine, I have made a figure more admirable and significant than is the run of men, and I rest upon my laurels.”

===Chinese===
「可是我不明白，」九月里一个晴朗的日子，弗蕾迪丝说道，「如今沙米尔的法力已握在你手中，你又掌握了赋泥像以生命的秘诀，你怎么就无意动用这秘诀，也无意动用那法宝呢。因为你如今再不造像了，你总是说：『不，这件事我们且缓一缓。』就连造像者们的那十个漫画像——你已经捏好了的那些——你也还不肯给它们生命。」

「到时候它们自会被赋予生命，」曼努埃尔说，「只是那个时辰还没有来。这期间我不再修习那古老的图伊拉秘法，理由也够充分：我亲眼见过它落在修习者身上的下场。曾有一道誓约加在我身上，要我在世上立起一个形象，于是我便照着自己的心思与愿望，捏出了那样一位堂堂而快活的少年英雄，把生命借给了他。这样看来，我的誓约已经卸除，而做成的事自有完结。上天如今尽可以造出比我更多的活人像来胜过我，但在这一桩上，出类拔萃原不是个算术问题——」

「啊，是了，我的斜眼小子样样美德俱全，连谦虚也不例外！」

「喏，可我已经见过我的构想化作形体，见过它呼吸，见过它离开莫尔文；它在一切方面——只除了一点微跛，这个你可知道，我还觉得跛得相当优雅？——我再说一遍，它在几乎一切方面，都同那位大匠师化出的诸般构想难以分辨：那位匠师，有人称他普塔（Ptha），有人称他雅赫维，有人称他阿布拉克萨斯（Abraxas），还有人管他叫不死的科谢伊（Koshchei the Deathless）。总而言之，我造出了一具人像，比寻常世人更可钦佩、更具分量，我便安享我的桂冠了。」

===Original===
“You have created a living being somewhat above the average, that is true: but then every woman who has a fine baby does just as much﻿—”

“The principle is not the same,” said Manuel, with dignity.

“And why not, please, big boy?”

“For one thing, my image was an original and unaided production, whereas a baby, I am told, is the result of more or less hasty collaboration. Then, too a baby is largely chance work, in that its nature cannot be exactly foreplanned and predetermined by its makers, who, in the glow of artistic creation, must, I imagine, very often fail to follow the best aesthetic canons.”

===Chinese===
「你造出了一个略高于平均水准的活人，这倒是真的：可随便哪个生下漂亮娃娃的女人，做到的不也是同一样的事么——」

「这道理可不一样，」曼努埃尔庄重地说。

「那么请问，又有什么不一样呢，大孩子？」

「其一，我的泥像是独出心裁、独力完成的造物；而婴孩呢，据我所知，是几分仓促的合作之果。再者，婴孩大体上是碰运气的活计，因为它的天性无法由它的造者们精确地预先设计、预先注定——这些造者身处艺术创作的热狂之中，我想，多半常常顾不上遵循最上乘的美学法度。」

===Original===
“As for that, nobody who makes new and unexampled things can make them exactly to the maker’s will. Even your image limped, you remember﻿—”

“Ah, but so gracefully!”

“﻿—No, Manuel, it is only those necromancers who evoke the dead, and bid the dead return to the warm flesh, that can be certain as to the results of their sorcery. For these alone of magic-workers know in advance what they are making.”

“Ah, this is news! So you think it is possible to evoke the dead in some more tangible form than that of an instructive ghost? You think it possible for a dead girl﻿—or, as to that matter, for a dead boy, or a defunct archbishop, or a deceased ragpicker﻿—to be fetched back to live again in the warm flesh?”

“All things are possible, Manuel, at a price.”

===Chinese===
「说到这个，凡造出前所未有之新事物的人，谁也不能把它造得同造者的意愿分毫不差。就连你的泥像不也跛了么，你记得的——」

「啊，可跛得多么优雅！」

「——不，曼努埃尔，唯有那些召唤亡者、命亡者回到温热血肉里去的死灵法师，才能对自己法术的结果有十足的把握。因为在一切施法者之中，唯独他们预先知道自己造的是什么。」

「啊，这可是新闻！这么说，你认为亡者可以召作一种比有益世道的鬼魂更切实可触的形相？你认为一个死去的姑娘——或者，把话说开去，一个死去的少年、一位已故的大主教、一个亡故的拾破烂的——也能被领回来，重新活在温热的血肉里？」

「万事皆有可能，曼努埃尔，只要出得起代价。」

===Original===
Said Manuel:

“What price would be sufficient to repurchase the rich spoils of Death? and whence might any bribe be fetched? For all the glowing wealth and beauty of this big round world must show as a new-minted farthing beside his treasure chests, as one slight shining unimportant coin which﻿—even this also!﻿—belongs to earth, but has been overlooked by him as yet. Presently this hour, and whatever is strutting through this hour, is added to the heaped crypts wherein lie all that was worthiest in the old time.

“Now there is garnered such might and loveliness and wisdom as human thinking cannot conceive of. An emperor is made much of here when he has conquered some part of the world, but Death makes nothing of a world of emperors: and in Death’s crowded storerooms nobody bothers to estimate within a thousand thousand of how many emperors, and tzars and popes and pharaohs and sultans, that in their day were adored as omnipotent, are there assembled pellmell, along with all that was worthiest in the old time.

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔说道：

「什么样的代价，才赎得回死神那丰厚的掠获呢？又能从哪里取来贿赂呢？因为这浑圆大地上一切灼灼生辉的财富与美色，摆到他的藏宝箱旁边，也只能像一枚新铸的法新（farthing），一枚单薄发亮、无足轻重的小钱——而连这一枚，也是属于尘世的！——只不过他至今还不曾放在眼里罢了。转眼之间，眼前这个时辰，连同在这个时辰里趾高气扬走过的一切，都要添进那一座座堆叠的地窖——窖中正躺着往昔一切至可珍贵之物。

「如今那里已经囤聚起这样的人力、美色与智慧，非凡人的思虑所能想见。在这里，一位皇帝征服了世界的某一部分，便受尽抬举；死神却不把整整一个世界的皇帝当回事：在死神拥挤的库房里，谁也懒得费神去估算那里乱纷纷堆聚着多少皇帝、多少沙皇、教皇、法老与苏丹——连百万之内的约数都不肯估——这些人在各自的当世都被奉作全能，同往昔一切至可珍贵之物堆作一处。

===Original===
“As touches loveliness, not even Helen’s beauty is distinguishable among those multitudinous millions of resplendent queens whom one finds yonder. Here are many pretty women, here above all is Freydis, so I do not complain. But yonder is deep-bosomed Semiramis, and fair-tressed Guenevere, and Magdalene that loved Christ, and Europa, the bull’s laughing bride, and Lilith, whose hot kiss made Satan ardent, and a many other ladies by whose dear beauty’s might were shaped the songs which cause us to remember all that was worthiest in the old time.

“As wisdom goes, here we have prudent men of business able to add two and two together, and justice may be out of hand distinguished from injustice by an impanelment of the nearest twelve fools. Here we have many Helmases a-cackling wisely under a goose-feather. But yonder are Cato and Nestor and Merlin and Socrates, Abelard sits with Aristotle there, and the seven sages confer with the major prophets, and yonder is all that was worthiest in the old time.

“All, all, are put away in Death’s heaped storerooms, so safely put away that opulent Death may well grin scornfully at Life: for everything belongs to Death, and Life is only a mendicant scratching at his sores so long as Death permits it. No, Freydis, there can be no bribing Death! For what bribe anywhere has Life to offer which Death has not already lying disregarded in a thousand dusty coffers along with all that was worthiest in the old time?”

===Chinese===
「论到美色，就是在那边亿兆煌煌的后妃之中，连海伦（Helen）的姿容也认不出来了。这里有的是漂亮女人，这里更坐着冠绝一切的弗蕾迪丝，所以我没什么好抱怨的。可那边有胸乳丰隆的塞弥拉弥斯（Semiramis），有美发如云的桂妮薇儿（Guenevere），有爱过基督的抹大拉的马利亚（Magdalene），有公牛那含笑的新娘欧罗巴（Europa），有莉莉丝（Lilith）——她炽热的一吻曾把撒旦烧得情热如焚——还有许许多多别的贵妇，正是凭着她们亲爱美貌的伟力，才锤炼出那些歌谣，教我们至今还记得往昔一切至可珍贵之物。

「论到智慧，我们这里有精明的生意人，会把二加二加到一起；要把公道同不公道分开，也只消就地凑齐最近的十二个傻瓜列名陪审，便可立见分晓。我们这里有许多位黑尔马斯，正握着一管鹅毛，嘎嘎地大发睿智之论。可那边有加图（Cato）、涅斯托尔（Nestor）、梅林（Merlin）与苏格拉底（Socrates），那边阿伯拉尔（Abelard）与亚里士多德（Aristotle）同坐，七贤同大先知们商谈，那边还有往昔一切至可珍贵之物。

「一切，一切，都收进了死神堆叠的库房，收得那样稳妥，以致阔绰的死神尽可以冲着生命鄙夷地咧嘴一笑：因为万事万物都属于死神，而生命不过是个叫花子，趁死神容许，才抓挠着自己的疮疤。不，弗蕾迪丝，死神是贿赂不动的！生命还能拿出什么贿赂，是死神不曾早已同往昔一切至可珍贵之物一起、丢在他一千只积尘的钱柜里正无人理会地躺着的呢？」

===Original===
Freydis replied: “One thing alone. Yes, Manuel, there is one thing only which all Death’s ravishings have never taken from Life, and which has not ever entered into Death’s keeping. It is through weighing this fact, and through doing what else is requisite, that the very bold may bring back the dead to live again in the warm flesh.”

“Well, but I have heard the histories of presumptuous men who attempted to perform such miracles, and all these persons sooner or later came to misery.”

“Why, to be sure! to whom else would you have them coming?” said Freydis. And she explained the way it was.

===Chinese===
弗蕾迪丝答道：「只有一样。是的，曼努埃尔，天下只有一样东西，是死神一切的劫掠从不曾从生命手里夺走、也从不曾收进死神掌管的。正是把这一层掂量分明，再把其余该做的事一一做到，那极大胆的人才能把亡人带回来，叫他重新活在温热的血肉里。」

「可是，我听说过那些妄自尊大之辈的事迹，他们试图行出这样的奇迹，而这些人个个迟早都归了苦难。」

「可不是嘛！不然你还要他们归到谁那儿去呢？」弗蕾迪丝说。接着，她把其中的道理讲了。

===Original===
Manuel put many questions. All that evening he was thoughtful, and he was unusually tender with Freydis. And that night, when Freydis slept, Dom Manuel kissed her very lightly, then blinked his eyes, and for a moment covered them with his hand. Standing thus, the tall boy queerly moving his mouth, as though it were stiff and he were trying to make it more supple.

Then he armed himself. He took up the black shield upon which was painted a silver stallion. He crept out of their modest magic home and went down into Bellegarde, where he stole him a horse, from the stables of Duke Asmund.

And that night, and all the next day, Dom Manuel rode beyond Aigremont and Naimes, journeying away from Morven, and away from the house of jasper and porphyry and violet and yellow breccia, and away from Freydis, who had put off immortality for his kisses. He travelled northward, toward the high woods of Dun Vlechlan, where the leaves were aglow with the funereal flames of autumn: for the summer wherein Dom Manuel and Freydis had been happy together was now as dead as that estranged queer time which he had shared with Alianora.

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔提了许多问题。那一晚他始终若有所思，待弗蕾迪丝也异乎寻常地温存。那天夜里，弗蕾迪丝睡熟之后，曼努埃尔大人极轻地吻了她一下，随后眨了眨眼，又用手把眼睛遮了片刻。他这样站着，这高个子少年古怪地嚅动着嘴，仿佛那张嘴发僵，正想把它揉得活络一些。

于是他披挂起来。他拿起那面黑盾，盾上绘着一匹白银骏马。他悄悄溜出他们那座朴素的魔法居所，下到贝勒加德，在阿斯蒙德公爵的马厩里给自己偷了一匹马。

那一夜，再加上第二天一整天，曼努埃尔大人骑马走过了艾格雷蒙（Aigremont）与奈梅斯（Naimes），一路远离莫尔文，远离那座碧玉、斑岩、紫黄二色角砾岩筑成的宅子，也远离弗蕾迪丝——她为了他的吻，早已卸下了不朽。他向北而行，朝敦·弗莱赫兰的高林赶去，那里满林秋叶正被秋天丧葬的火焰照得通明：因为曼努埃尔大人同弗蕾迪丝一同欢度的那个夏天，如今已经死了，死得就像他与阿莉亚诺拉共同消磨的那段疏远而古怪的时光一样。

## XXIII Manuel Gets His Desire / 二十三 曼努埃尔得偿所愿

===Original===

Now the tale tells that all this while, near the gray hut in Dun Vlechlan, the earthen image of Niafer lay drying out in the November sun; and that gray Dom Manuel﻿—no longer the florid boy who had come into Dun Vlechlan﻿—sat at the feet of the image, and played upon a flageolet the air which Suskind had taught him, and with which he had been used to call young Suskind from her twilit places when Manuel was a peasant tending swine. Now Manuel was an aging nobleman, and Niafer was now a homeless ghost, but the tune had power over them, none the less, for its burden was young love and the high-hearted time of youth; so that the melody which once had summoned Suskind from her low red-pillared palace in the doubtful twilight, now summoned Niafer resistlessly from paradise, as Manuel thriftily made use of the odds and ends which he had learned from three women to win him a fourth woman.

The spirit of Niafer entered at the mouth of the image. Instantly the head sneezed, and said, “I am unhappy.” But Manuel kept on playing. The spirit descended further, bringing life to the lungs and the belly, so that the image then cried, “I am hungry.” But Manuel kept on playing. So the soul was drawn further and further, until Manuel saw that the white image had taken on the colors of flesh, and was moving its toes in time to his playing; and so knew that the entire body was informed with life.

He cast down the flageolet, and touched the breast of the image with the ancient formal gestures of the old Tuyla mystery, and he sealed the mouth of the image with a kiss, so that the spirit of Niafer was imprisoned in the image which Manuel had made. Under his lips the lips which had been Misery’s cried, “I love.” And Niafer rose, a living girl just such as Manuel had remembered for more than a whole year: but with that kiss all memories of paradise and all the traits of angelhood departed from her.

“Well, well, dear snip,” said Manuel, the first thing of all, “now it is certainly a comfort to have you back again.”

===Chinese===

且说故事讲到：这一向，在敦·弗莱赫兰（Dun Vlechlan）那座灰色小屋近旁，妮亚菲尔（Niafer）的泥像一直躺在十一月的太阳里晾着；而灰色的曼努埃尔大人（Dom Manuel）——早已不是当初走进敦·弗莱赫兰的那个面色红润的少年了——坐在泥像脚边，用一支小笛（flageolet）吹起苏斯金德（Suskind）教他的那支曲子；当年曼努埃尔还是个看猪的庄稼汉时，惯用这支曲子把年轻的苏斯金德从她的暮色之地里唤出来。如今曼努埃尔已是个上了年纪的贵族，妮亚菲尔也已是个无家可归的幽魂，可这支曲子对他们俩依然有威力，只因它的副歌唱的是年轻的爱情和青春那段意气昂扬的年华；于是，这支曾在昏昧可疑的暮色里把苏斯金德从她那低矮的红柱宫殿中唤出来的旋律，此刻又无可抗拒地把妮亚菲尔从天堂里唤了回来——曼努埃尔就这样俭省地使上他从三个女人那里学来的零七八碎，为自己赢得了第四个女人。

妮亚菲尔的魂灵从泥像口中进去。霎时间，那颗脑袋打了个喷嚏，说道：「我不快活。」曼努埃尔只管继续吹。魂灵再往下走，把生气带进两肺和肚腹，泥像便叫道：「我饿了。」曼努埃尔还是只管继续吹。魂灵就这样被越引越深，直到曼努埃尔看见那白色的泥像已染上血肉的颜色，脚趾还随着他的笛声一下一下打拍子；他由此知道，整具身体都已贯注了生气。

他撂下小笛，以图伊拉（Tuyla）古老秘法那一套古板而正规的姿势抚过泥像的胸口，又用一个吻封住泥像的嘴，于是妮亚菲尔的魂灵便被囚进了曼努埃尔亲手造出的这具泥像。在他唇下，那曾经属于苦难（Misery）的双唇喊道：「我爱。」妮亚菲尔站起身来，是个活生生的姑娘，同曼努埃尔一年多来朝夕追念的那一个分毫不差：只是随着那一吻，对天堂的全部记忆和身为天使的种种品性，都从她身上消散了。

「好啦，好啦，亲爱的小家伙，」曼努埃尔头一句话便说，「你如今回来了，这实在是一件叫人宽慰的事。」

===Original===

Niafer, even in the rapture of her happiness, found this an unimpassioned greeting from one who had gone to unusual lengths to recover her companionship. Staring, she saw that Manuel had all the marks of a man in middle life, and spoke as became appearances. For it was at the price of his youth that Manuel had recovered the woman whom his youth desired: and Misery had subtly evened matters by awarding an aging man the woman for whose sake a lad had fearlessly served Misery. There was no longer any such lad, for the conquered had destroyed the conqueror.

Then, after a moment’s consideration of this tall gray stranger, Niafer also looked graver and older. Niafer asked for a mirror: and Manuel had none.

“Now but certainly I must know at once just how faithfully you have remembered me,” says Niafer.

===Chinese===

妮亚菲尔纵然正沉浸在幸福的狂喜里，也觉得这声问候未免太不热烈，不像出自一个曾大费周章寻回她陪伴的人之口。她定睛细看，只见曼努埃尔浑身已是人到中年的印记，说话行事也便依着这副表象该有的分寸。原来曼努埃尔是以青春为代价，才赢回了他青春所渴求的女人：而苦难也巧妙地摆平了这笔账——把一个女人判给一个上了年纪的人，而当年正是为了这个女人，一个小伙子才无所畏惧地侍奉过苦难。那样的小伙子如今已不复存在，因为被征服者毁掉了征服者。

随后，把这位高大灰暗的陌生人端详了片刻，妮亚菲尔自己也显得更沉肃、更见老了。妮亚菲尔讨一面镜子：曼努埃尔却没有。

「不过眼下我就一定得知道，你把我记得究竟有多真切，」妮亚菲尔说。

===Original===

He led the way into the naked and desolate November forest, and they came to the steel-colored Wolflake hard by the gray hut: and Niafer found she was limping, for Manuel had not got her legs quite right, so that for the rest of her second life she was lame. Then Niafer gazed for a minute, or it might be for two minutes, at her reflection in the deep cold waters of the Wolflake.

“Is this as near as you have come to remembering me, my dearest!” she said, dejectedly, as she looked down at Manuel’s notion of her face. For the appearance which Niafer now wore she found to be very little like that which Niafer remembered as having been hers, in days wherein she had been tolerably familiar with the Lady Gisèle’s mirrors; and it was a grief to Niafer to see how utterly the dearest dead go out of mind in no long while.

“I have forgotten not one line or curve of your features,” says Manuel, stoutly, “in all these months, nor in any of these last days that have passed as years. And when my love spurred me to make your image, Niafer, my love loaned me unwonted cunning. Even by ordinary, they tell me, I have some skill at making images: and while not for a moment would I seem to boast of that skill, and not for worlds would I annoy you by repeating any of the complimentary things which have been said about my images﻿—by persons somewhat more appreciative, my dear, of the toil and care that goes to work of this sort﻿—I certainly think that in this instance nobody has fair reason to complain.”

===Chinese===

他领着路走进十一月那光秃秃、一片荒凉的山林，来到灰色小屋近旁铁灰色的狼湖（Wolflake）边：妮亚菲尔这才发觉自己走路一瘸一拐，原来曼努埃尔没把她的两条腿做得十分端正，于是她第二条生命的余生便都跛着。随后，妮亚菲尔对着自己在狼湖幽深寒冽的湖水里的倒影，凝望了一分钟，也许有两分钟之久。

「我最亲爱的人，你记挂我，就只记得这么个近似的样子么！」她垂眼看着曼努埃尔心目中的她那张脸，颓然说道。因为妮亚菲尔发现，她如今穿戴的这副容貌，同她记忆中自己往日的模样相去甚远——在那些岁月里，她与吉赛尔夫人的镜子还算相当熟稔；眼见最亲爱的人一死，不出多久便被忘得这样干净，妮亚菲尔好不伤怀。

「你五官的一线一弯，我没有忘掉一处，」曼努埃尔斩钉截铁地说，「这许多个月里没有，这些一天抵得上几年的近来日子里也没有。而且妮亚菲尔，当我的爱策动我造你的像时，我的爱还借给了我非比寻常的巧艺。人家告诉我，即便平日，我造像也算有几分本领：虽说我不想有半刻显得在夸耀这门本领，也万万不肯复述人家对我那些像说过的种种恭维话来惹你心烦——说这些话的人哪，亲爱的，对这一类活计里要费的辛劳和心血，可比你有眼光些——可我确实认为，就眼前这一件而论，谁也没有道理抱怨。」

===Original===

She looked at his face now: and she noted what the month of living with Béda, with whom a day is as a year, had done to the boy’s face which she remembered. Count Manuel’s face was of remodeled stuff: youth had gone out of it, and the month of years had etched wrinkles in it, success had hardened and caution had pinched and self-complacency had kissed it. And Niafer sighed again, as they sat reunited under leafless trees by the steel-colored Wolflake.

“There is no circumventing time and death, then, after all,” said Niafer, “for neither of us is now the person that ascended Vraidex. No matter: I love you, Manuel, and I am content with what remains of you: and if the body you have given me is to your will it is to my will.”

But now three rascally tall ragged fellows, each blind in one eye, and each having a thin peaked beard, came into the opening before the gray hut, trampling the dead leaves there as they shouted for Mimir. “Come out!” they cried: “come out, you miserable Mirmir, and face those three whom you have wronged!”

===Chinese===

此刻她端详起他的脸来：她看出了与贝达（Béda）同住的那一个月——在贝达那里，一日便如一年——把她记忆中那张少年的脸弄成了什么光景。曼努埃尔伯爵（Count Manuel）的脸是重新塑过的材料：青春已从中逸去，那一个月的岁月在上面蚀刻出皱纹，成功使它变硬，谨慎把它掐紧，沾沾自喜又吻过了它。妮亚菲尔再叹一口气，这时两人已重聚在一处，坐在铁灰色的狼湖边光秃秃的树下。

「这么说，时间和死亡到底还是绕不过去的，」妮亚菲尔说，「因为我们俩如今都已不是当初登上弗赖德克斯的那两个人了。不要紧：我爱你，曼努埃尔，剩下的这个你，我也知足：你给我的这副身子，既合你的意，便也合我的意。」

这时却来了三个高个子、衣衫褴褛的无赖汉，个个瞎了一只眼，个个蓄着一撮尖尖的稀须，闯进灰色小屋前的空地，一边践踏满地枯叶，一边喊着叫密米尔（Mimir）出来。「出来！」他们嚷道：「出来，你这可恶的米尔米尔，来见一见被你亏害的三个人！」

===Original===

Dom Manuel rose from the bank of the Wolflake, and went toward the shouters. “There is no Mimir,” he told them, “in Dun Vlechlan, or not at least in this peculiarly irrational part of the forest.”

“You lie,” they said, “for even though you have hitched a body to your head we recognize you.” They looked at Niafer, and all three laughed cruelly. “Was it for this hunched, draggled, mud-faced wench that you left us, you squinting old villain? And have you so soon forgotten the vintner’s parlor at Neogréant, and what you did with the gold plates?”

“No, I have not forgotten these things, for I never knew anything about them,” said Manuel.

Said one of the knaves, twirling fiercely his moustachios: “Hah, shameless Mimir, do you look at me, who have known you and your blind son Oriander, too, to be unblushing knaves for these nine centuries! Now, I suppose, you will be denying the affair of the squirrel also?”

===Chinese===

曼努埃尔大人从狼湖岸边站起身，朝叫嚷的人走去。「敦·弗莱赫兰没有密米尔，」他告诉他们，「至少，这片格外不讲道理的林子一角上没有。」

「你撒谎，」他们说，「哪怕你给那颗脑袋拴上了一具身子，我们也认得你。」他们朝妮亚菲尔看了看，三个人都残忍地大笑。「就是为了这个驼背拖沓、一脸泥色的娘们，你才丢下我们的么，你这斜眼的老恶棍？这么快你就忘了内奥格莱昂（Neogréant）酒商家里的客厅，忘了你把那些金盘子怎么处置了？」

「不，这些事我没有忘，因为我从来就对它们一无所知，」曼努埃尔说。

一个无赖狠狠地捻着唇上的髭须，说道：「哈，恬不知耻的密米尔，你还敢正眼瞧我？我可是一向知道你，也知道你那瞎眼的儿子奥里安德（Oriander），这九百年来全是些不红脸的无赖！怎么样，我料想你如今连松鼠那桩事也要抵赖了吧？」

===Original===

“Oh, be off with your nonsense!” says Manuel, “for I have not yet had twenty-two years of living, and I never saw you before, and I hope never to see you again.”

But they all set upon him with cutlasses, so there was nothing remaining save to have out his sword and fight. And when each of these one-eyed persons had vanished curiously under his death-wound, Manuel told Niafer it was a comfort to find that the month of years had left him a fair swordsman for all that his youth was gone; and that he thought they had better be leaving this part of the high woods of Dun Vlechlan, wherein unaccountable things took place, and all persons behaved unreasonably.

“Were these wood-spirits unreasonable,” asks Niafer, “in saying that the countenance and the body you have given me are ugly?”

===Chinese===

「哦，把你这套胡话收起来吧！」曼努埃尔说，「我还不曾活满二十二个年头，从前从没见过你们，但愿往后也永远不要再见到你们。」

可他们抡起弯刀一齐向他扑来，他别无出路，只好拔出剑来应战。待这几个独眼人一个个在自己致命的伤创之下古怪地消失之后，曼努埃尔对妮亚菲尔说，虽然青春已经去了，那一个月的岁月好歹给他留下了一手不错的剑法，这实在叫人宽慰；他还说，依他看，他们还是离开敦·弗莱赫兰这片高林的这一角为好，因为在这里，无从索解的怪事层出不穷，人人行事都不讲道理。

「这些林中精灵说你给我的这张脸、这副身子丑，难道是他们不讲道理么？」妮亚菲尔问。

===Original===

“My dear,” replied Manuel, “it was their saying that which made me try to avoid the conflict, because it does not look well, not even in dealing with demons, to injure the insane.”

“Manuel, and can it be you who are considering appearances?”

Dom Manuel said gravely: “My dealings with Misery and with Misery’s kindred have taught me many things which I shall never forget nor very willingly talk about. One of these teachings, though, is that in most affairs there is a middle road on which there is little traffic and comparatively easy going. I must tell you that the company I have been in required a great deal of humoring, for of course it is not safe to trifle with any evil principle. No, no, one need not absolutely and openly defy convention, I perceive, in order to follow after one’s own thinking,” says Manuel, shrewdly, and waggling a gray beard.

===Chinese===

「亲爱的，」曼努埃尔答道，「正是他们说了这话，我才想方设法避开这场冲突，因为伤害疯癫之人，观之总是不雅——即便对付的是妖魔，也不例外。」

「曼努埃尔，难道如今计较起表象来的，竟会是你么？」

曼努埃尔大人正色说道：「我与苦难、与苦难的亲族打交道的那些经历，教会了我许多事；这些事我永远不会忘记，也不大愿意谈起。不过其中有一样教益是：在大多数事务里，都有一条中道，那条路上行人稀少，走起来也比较省力。我必须告诉你，我先前作伴的那一位，是极要人迁就的，因为跟任何邪恶的原理儿戏，当然都不安全。不，不，我如今明白了，人要追随自己的思考行事，未必就得绝对地、公然地触犯成规，」曼努埃尔精明地说着，一面抖动一部灰白的胡须。

===Original===

“I am so glad you have learned that at last! At least, I suppose, I am glad,” said Niafer, a little wistfully, as she recalled young Manuel of the high head.

“But, as I was saying, I now estimate that these tattered persons who would have prevented my leaving, as well as the red fellow that would have hindered my entering, this peculiarly irrational part of the forest, were spiritual intruders into Misery’s domain whom Misery had driven out of their wits. No, Niafer, I voice no criticism, because with us two this Misery of earth, whom some call Béda, and others Kruchina, has dealt very handsomely. It troubles me to suspect that he was also called Mimir; but of this we need not speak, because a thing done has an end, even a killed grandfather. Nevertheless, I think that Dun Vlechlan is unwholesome, and I am of the opinion that you and I will be more comfortable elsewhere.”

“But must we go back to looking after pigs, dear Manuel, or are you now too old for that?”

Dom Manuel smiled, and you saw that he retained at least his former lordliness. “No, now that every obligation is lifted, and we are reunited, dear snip, I can at last go traveling everywhither, so that I may see the ends of this world and judge them. And we will do whatever else we choose, for, as I must tell you, I am now a nobleman with lackeys and meadowlands and castles of my own, if only I could obtain possession of them.”

“This is excellent hearing,” said Niafer, “and much better than pig-stealing, and I am glad that the world has had sense enough to appreciate you, Manuel, and you it. And we will have rubies in my coronet, because I always fancied them. Now do you tell me how it all happened, and what I am to be called countess of. And we will talk about that traveling later, for I have already traveled a great distance today, but we must certainly have rubies.”

===Chinese===

「你到底把这一点学会了，我真高兴！至少，我想我是高兴的，」妮亚菲尔说道，语气里带着些许怅惘，一面回想起当年那个高昂着头的年轻曼努埃尔。

「不过，正像我方才说的，我如今估量，那些不放我走的褴褛人，还有那个拦我进这片格外不讲道理的林子一角的红家伙，都是闯进苦难领地的灵体，被苦难逼得失了心神。不，妮亚菲尔，我这可不是在抱怨，因为这位尘世的苦难——有人称他贝达，也有人叫他克鲁奇娜（Kruchina）——待我们两个着实优厚。只是我一想到他恐怕还有个名字叫密米尔，心里就不大安然；不过这事我们不必再提，因为凡做了的事都有个了结，连被杀死的祖父也不例外。话虽如此，我总觉得敦·弗莱赫兰这地方不宜养生，依我看，你我到别处去会住得舒坦些。」

「可是亲爱的曼努埃尔，咱们必须回去看猪么，还是说你如今上了年纪，看不动猪了？」

曼努埃尔大人微微一笑，可见他至少还保留着昔日那份尊贵的气度。「不看了。如今一切义务都已解除，我们也团圆了，亲爱的小家伙，我终于可以到处游历，去看这世界的尽头，并对它们下判断了。其余的事，我们愿意做什么便做什么，因为——我必须告诉你——我如今是个贵族了，有自己的仆役、草场和城堡，只消我能把它们拿到手里。」

「这话听着再好不过，」妮亚菲尔说，「比偷猪强多了。我很高兴这世界总算有足够的见识来赏识你，曼努埃尔，而你也没有辜负它。我的冠冕上要镶红宝石，因为我一向喜欢红宝石。现在你来讲讲这一切是怎么来的，讲讲我该称作哪一处的伯爵夫人。游历的事我们回头再谈，因为我今天已经赶了老远的路；不过红宝石是万万一定要有的。」

## II Niafer / 二 妮亚菲尔

===Original===
The first thing of all that Manuel did, was to fill a knapsack with simple and nutritious food, and then he went to the gray mountain called Vraidex, upon the remote and cloud-wrapped summit of which dread Miramon Lluagor dwelt, in a doubtful palace wherein the lord of the nine sleeps contrived illusions and designed the dreams of men. When Manuel had passed under some very old maple-trees, and was beginning the ascent, he found a smallish, flat-faced, dark-haired boy going up before him.

“Hail, snip,” says Manuel, “and whatever are you doing in this perilous place?”

“Why, I am going,” the dark-haired boy replied, “to find out how the Lady Gisèle d’Arnaye is faring on the tall top of this mountain.”

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔所做的头一件事，便是往一只背包里装满简单而滋补的吃食，随后他便动身前往那座名叫弗赖德克斯（Vraidex）的灰色山岳——在那遥远而云封雾裹的峰顶，栖居着可畏的米拉蒙·卢亚戈尔（Miramon Lluagor）。他住在一座疑真疑幻的宫殿里，九眠之主便是在那里营造幻象、设计世人的梦境。曼努埃尔从几株苍老的枫树底下走过、正要开始登山时，发现一个身材瘦小、脸庞扁平的黑发男孩走在他前头。

「你好哇，小家伙，」曼努埃尔说，「你在这等凶险之地究竟是做什么来的？」

「噢，我正要上去，」黑发男孩答道，「去看看吉赛尔·达尔奈夫人（Lady Gisèle d’Arnaye）在这座山高高的顶上境况如何。」

===Original===
“Oho, then we will undertake this adventure together, for that is my errand too. And when the adventure is fulfilled, we will fight together, and the survivor will have the wealth and broad lands and the Count’s daughter to sit on his knee. What do they call you, friend?”

“I am called Niafer. But I believe that the Lady Gisèle is already married, to Miramon Lluagor. At least, I sincerely hope she is married to this great magician, for otherwise it would not be respectable for her to be living with him at the top of this gray mountain.”

“Fluff and puff! what does that matter?” says Manuel. “There is no law against a widow’s remarrying forthwith: and widows are quickly made by any champion about whom the wise Norns are already talking. But I must not tell you about that, Niafer, because I do not wish to appear boastful. So I must simply say to you, Niafer, that I am called Manuel, and have no other title as yet, being not yet even a baron.”

===Chinese===
「哦嗬，那这场奇遇咱们便一同去历吧，因为这也正是我的差事。等奇遇功成，咱们再决斗一场，活下来的那个便得财货与广土，还得伯爵的女儿坐上他的膝头。他们怎么称呼你，朋友？」

「我叫妮亚菲尔（Niafer）。不过我相信吉赛尔夫人已经嫁人了，嫁给了米拉蒙·卢亚戈尔。至少，我衷心盼着她已嫁给这位大魔法师，否则她同他一道住在这座灰山顶上，可就不成体统了。」

「一派胡言！那有什么要紧？」曼努埃尔说。「没有哪条法律不许寡妇旋即再嫁：况且只要智慧的三女神诺恩（Norns）已在谈论哪位豪杰，寡妇便很快做得成。不过这事我可不能对你细讲，妮亚菲尔，因为我不愿显得自夸。所以我只能简单地对你说，妮亚菲尔：我名叫曼努埃尔，眼下尚无别的头衔，连男爵都还不是呢。」

===Original===
“Come now,” says Niafer, “but you are rather sure of yourself for a young boy!”

“Why, of what may I be sure in this shifting world if not of myself?”

“Our elders, Manuel, declare that such self-conceit is a fault, and our elders, they say, are wiser than we.”

===Chinese===
「得了吧，」妮亚菲尔说，「小小年纪，你倒对自己挺有把握！」

「咦，在这变幻无常的世上，若连自己都靠不住，还有什么靠得住的？」

「曼努埃尔，我们的长辈们说，这样的自负是一桩过失，而我们的长辈呢，据说比我们高明。」

===Original===
“Our elders, Niafer, have long had the management of this world’s affairs, and you can see for yourself what they have made of these affairs. What sort of a world is it, I ask you, in which time peculates the gold from hair and the crimson from all lips, and the north wind carries away the glow and glory and contentment of October, and a driveling old magician steals a lovely girl? Why, such maraudings are out of reason, and show plainly that our elders have no notion how to manage things.”

“Eh, Manuel, and will you remodel the world?”

“Who knows?” says Manuel, in the high pride of his youth. “At all events, I do not mean to leave it unaltered.”

===Chinese===
「妮亚菲尔，我们的长辈们执掌这尘世的营生由来已久，这些营生被他们料理成了什么模样，你自己也看得分明。我问你，这是个什么世道：光阴从头发里私吞了金色，从所有人的唇上私吞了绯红；北风卷走了十月的暖彩、荣光与安适；一个淌着口水的老魔法师还偷走一位可爱的姑娘。这样的劫掠简直毫无道理，明明白白显出我们的长辈压根不懂如何打理世事。」

「哎，曼努埃尔，那么你要把这世界翻修一新么？」

「谁知道呢？」曼努埃尔说，满怀青春的骄矜。「无论如何，我可不打算让它原样不动。」

===Original===
Then Niafer, a more prosaic person, gave him a long look compounded equally of admiration and pity, but Niafer did not dispute the matter. Instead, these two pledged constant fealty until they should have rescued Madame Gisèle.

“Then we will fight for her,” says Manuel, again.

“First, Manuel, let me see her face, and then let me see her state of mind, and afterward I will see about fighting you. Meanwhile, this is a very tall mountain, and the climbing of it will require all the breath which we are wasting here.”

===Chinese===
于是妮亚菲尔——他是个更务实的人——久久地端详了曼努埃尔一眼，那目光里钦佩与怜悯各占一半，但妮亚菲尔并未就此争执。这两个少年反倒立下誓约，要彼此永效忠诚，直到救出吉赛尔夫人（Madame Gisèle）为止。

「那时我们再为她决斗一场。」曼努埃尔又说。

「先让我见见她的容貌，曼努埃尔，再让我瞧瞧她的心境，之后我自会考虑同你决斗。况且这山高得很，攀爬它要用的全副气力，眼下正被咱们白白耗在这儿呢。」

===Original===
So the two began the ascent of Vraidex, by the winding road upon which the dreams traveled when they were sent down to men by the lord of the seven madnesses. All gray rock was the way at first. But they soon reached the gnawed bones of those who had ascended before them, scattered about a small plain that was overgrown with ironweed: and through and over the tall purple blossoms came to destroy the boys the Serpent of the East, a very dreadful design with which Miramon afflicted the sleep of Lithuanians and Tartars. The snake rode on a black horse, a black falcon perched on his head, and a black hound followed him. The horse stumbled, the falcon clamored, the hound howled.

===Chinese===
于是两人便沿着一条蜿蜒的路开始攀登弗赖德克斯——七狂之主把梦遣送下界给世人时，梦便是打这条路上经过的。起初一路尽是灰色的岩石。但不久他们便到了先前登山人被啃啮过的骸骨跟前，骸骨四散在一片长满斑鸠菊的小平原上：东方巨蛇（Serpent of the East）穿过并越过那高高的紫花丛，前来收拾这两个少年——那是米拉蒙加于立陶宛人与鞑靼人睡眠之上的一件极可怖的设计。那蛇骑一匹黑马，一头黑隼栖在他脑壳上，一条黑犬跟在后头。马打了个趔趄，隼厉声聒噪，犬哀哀长嚎。

===Original===
Then said the snake: “My steed, why do you stumble? my hound, why do you howl? and, my falcon, why do you clamor? For these three doings foresay some ill to me.”

“Oh, a great ill!” replies Manuel, with his charmed sword already half out of the scabbard.

But Niafer cried: “An endless ill is foresaid by these doings. For I have been to the Island of the Oaks: and under the twelfth oak was a copper casket, and in the casket was a purple duck, and in the duck was an egg: and in the egg, O Norka, was and is your death.”

===Chinese===
于是那蛇说道：「我的骏马，你为何趔趄？我的猎犬，你为何哀嚎？还有你，我的隼，为何聒噪？这三桩动静，都预兆着我有祸事要临头了。」

「哦，是天大的祸事！」曼努埃尔应道，他的魔剑已经半出剑鞘。

但妮亚菲尔高声道：「这些动静预兆的是一场无尽的祸事。因为我到过橡树岛（Island of the Oaks）：第十二棵橡树底下有一只铜匣，铜匣里有一只紫鸭，紫鸭腹中有一枚蛋：而那枚蛋里，诺尔卡（Norka）啊，从前藏着、如今还藏着你的死劫。」

===Original===
“It is true that my death is in such an egg,” said the Serpent of the East, “but nobody will ever find that egg, and therefore I am resistless and immortal.”

“To the contrary, the egg, as you can perceive, is in my hand; and when I break this egg you will die, and it is smaller worms than you that will be thanking me for their supper this night.”

The serpent looked at the poised egg, and he trembled and writhed so that his black scales scattered everywhither scintillations of reflected sunlight. He cried, “Give me the egg, and I will permit you two to ascend unmolested, to a more terrible destruction.”

===Chinese===
「我的死劫确是藏在这样一枚蛋里，」东方巨蛇说，「可谁也永远寻不着那枚蛋，所以我势不可当、长生不死。」

「恰恰相反，那枚蛋，你也瞧得见，正在我手中；等我捏碎这枚蛋，你就没命了，今夜要为晚餐谢我的，将是些比你更小的虫豸。」

巨蛇望着那枚擎在手中的蛋，抖索着、扭动着，黑鳞把反照的日光抖落得四下星芒闪烁。他喊道：「把蛋给我，我便容你们两个不受搅扰地上山，去赴一场更可怕的毁灭。」

===Original===
Niafer was not eager to do this, but Manuel thought it best, and so at last Niafer consented to the bargain, for the sake of the serpent’s children. Then the two lads went upward, while the serpent bandaged the eyes of his horse and of his hound, and hooded his falcon, and crept gingerly away to hide the egg in an unmentionable place.

“But how in the devil,” says Manuel, “did you manage to come by that invaluable egg?”

“It is a quite ordinary duck egg, Manuel. But the Serpent of the East has no way of discovering the fact unless he breaks the egg: and that is the one thing the serpent will never do, because he thinks it is the magic egg which contains his death.”

===Chinese===
妮亚菲尔并不怎么情愿这么做，但曼努埃尔觉得这样最好，于是妮亚菲尔终于应下了这笔交易——看在那蛇的孩子们的份上。随后两个少年继续上行，那蛇则给他的黑马和黑犬蒙上眼罩，给黑隼戴上兜帽，蹑手蹑脚地爬开，把那枚蛋藏到一个不便明说的所在去了。

「可是见鬼，」曼努埃尔说，「你究竟是怎么弄到手那枚千金难换的蛋的？」

「那不过是一枚再寻常不过的鸭蛋，曼努埃尔。可东方巨蛇没法识破这个底细，除非他把蛋打破：而这恰是他绝不肯做的一件事，因为他认定这便是藏着他的死劫的魔蛋。」

===Original===
“Come, Niafer, you are not handsome to look at, but you are far cleverer than I thought you!”

Now, as Manuel clapped Niafer on the shoulder, the forest beside the roadway was agitated, and the underbrush crackled, and the tall beech-trees crashed and snapped and tumbled helter-skelter. The crust of the earth was thus broken through by the Serpent of the North. Only the head and throat of this design of Miramon’s was lifted from the jumbled trees, for it was requisite of course that the serpent’s lower coils should never loose their grip upon the foundations of Norroway. All of the design that showed was overgrown with seaweed and barnacles.

“It is the will of Miramon Lluagor that I forthwith demolish you both,” says this serpent, yawning with a mouth like a fanged cave.

===Chinese===
「好啊，妮亚菲尔，你模样虽生得不俊，人却比我想的机灵得多！」

且说曼努埃尔正拍着妮亚菲尔的肩膀，路旁的树林忽然骚动起来，下层灌木噼啪爆响，高高的山毛榉折断、崩裂、乱七八糟地纷纷倒下。地壳就这样被北方巨蛇（Serpent of the North）破开了。米拉蒙这件设计只将头颅与颈项从乱树丛中抬起，因为按理，巨蛇下身的盘曲万万不能松开它对挪罗威（Norroway）根基的紧扣。这件设计凡露出形迹的部分，全都长满了海草与藤壶。

「米拉蒙·卢亚戈尔的意旨是，叫我立刻把你们两个一齐毁掉。」这条蛇说道，张着一张像生满獠牙的洞穴似的嘴打了个哈欠。

===Original===
Once more young Manuel had reached for his charmed sword Flamberge, but it was Niafer who spoke.

“No, for before you can destroy me,” says Niafer, “I shall have cast this bridle over your head.”

“What sort of bridle is that?” inquired the great snake scornfully.

“And are those goggling flaming eyes not big enough and bright enough to see that this is the soft bridle called Gleipnir, which is made of the breath of fish and of the spittle of birds and of the footfall of a cat?”

===Chinese===
年轻的曼努埃尔又一次伸手去够他的魔剑弗兰贝尔热（Flamberge），但这回开口的是妮亚菲尔。

「不成，因为不等你毁掉我，」妮亚菲尔说，「我早把这副笼头套上你的头了。」

「那是一副什么笼头？」大蛇轻蔑地问。

「你那双暴凸的、喷火的眼睛，难道还不够大、还不够亮，看不出这便是那副名叫格莱普尼尔（Gleipnir）的软笼头么？它是拿鱼的呼吸、鸟的唾液和猫的脚步声制成的。」

===Original===
“Now, although certainly such a bridle was foretold,” the snake conceded, a little uneasily, “how can I make sure that you speak the truth when you say this particular bridle is Gleipnir?”

“Why, in this way: I will cast the bridle over your head, and then you will see for yourself that the old prophecy will be fulfilled, and that all power and all life will go out of you, and that the Northmen will dream no more.”

“No, do you keep that thing away from me, you little fool! No, no: we will not test your truthfulness in that way. Instead, do you two continue your ascent, to a more terrible destruction, and to face barbaric dooms coming from the West. And do you give me the bridle to demolish in place of you. And then, if I live forever I shall know that this is indeed Gleipnir, and that you have spoken the truth.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，这样一副笼头固然早有预言，」巨蛇有些不安地让了步，「可你说这副笼头偏就是格莱普尼尔，我又怎么拿得准你讲的是真话？」

「咦，这样办：我把笼头往你头上一套，你自会亲眼看到古老的预言如何应验，看到你全部的权能与性命如何离你而去，看到北方人从此再不做梦。」

「不，快把你那玩意儿从我身边拿开，小傻瓜！不，不：咱们不用那个法子来考验你的诚实。这么着吧，你们两个只管继续上山，去赴一场更可怕的毁灭，去面对自西方而来的蛮荒厄运。你们把这笼头交给我，让我拿它代你们受毁。这样，我若得永生，便知道这果真是格莱普尼尔，你讲的也果真是真话。」

===Original===
So Niafer consented to this testing of his veracity, rather than permit this snake to die, and the foundations of Norroway (in which kingdom, Niafer confessed, he had an aunt then living) thus to be dissolved by the loosening of the dying serpent’s grip upon Middlegarth. The bridle was yielded, and Niafer and Manuel went upward.

Manuel asked, “Snip, was that in truth the bridle called Gleipnir?”

“No, Manuel, it is an ordinary bridle. But this Serpent of the North has no way of discovering this fact except by fitting the bridle over his head: and this one thing the serpent will never do, because he knows that then, if my bridle proved to be Gleipnir, all power and all life would go out of him.”

===Chinese===
于是妮亚菲尔应允了这场对他诚实的检验，而不肯让这条蛇死去，也不肯让挪罗威的根基（妮亚菲尔招认，他彼时正有一位姑母住在那个王国）因垂死的巨蛇松开它箍住米德加斯（Middlegarth）的躯体而就此消解。笼头交了出去，妮亚菲尔与曼努埃尔继续上行。

曼努埃尔问道：「小家伙，那果真是名叫格莱普尼尔的笼头么？」

「不是，曼努埃尔，那是一副平平常常的笼头。可这北方巨蛇没法识破这个底细，除非把笼头往自己头上一套：而这是他绝不肯做的一件事，因为他知道，倘若我这笼头果真是格莱普尼尔，他全部的权能与性命便都要离他而去了。」

===Original===
“O subtle, ugly little snip!” says Manuel: and again he patted Niafer on the shoulder. Then Manuel spoke very highly in praise of cleverness, and said that, for one, he had never objected to it in its place.

===Chinese===
「哦，狡黠又丑陋的小家伙！」曼努埃尔说，又拍了拍妮亚菲尔的肩膀。接着，曼努埃尔对聪明才智大事称扬，还说就他个人而言，只要用得其所，他对聪明从来不曾有过半点异议。

## XXXIII Now Manuel Prospers / 三十三 曼努埃尔如今昌盛

===Original===
They of Poictesme narrate fine tales as to the deeds that Manuel the Redeemer performed and incited in the days of his reign. They tell also many things that seem improbable, and therefore are not included in this book: for the old songs and tales incline to make of Count Manuel’s heydey a rare golden age.

So many glorious exploits are, indeed, accredited to Manuel and to the warriors whom he gathered round him in his famous Fellowship of the Silver Stallion﻿—and among whom, Holden and courteous Anavalt and Coth the Alderman and Gonfal and Donander had the preeminence, where all were hardy﻿—that it is very difficult to understand how so brief a while could have continued so many doings. But the tale-tellers of Poictesme have been long used to say of a fine action﻿—not falsely, but misleadingly﻿—“Thus it was in Count Manuel’s time,” and the tribute by and by has been accepted as a dating. So has chronology been hacked to make loftier his fame, and the glory of Dom Manuel has been a magnet that has drawn to itself the magnanimities of other days and years.

But there is no need here to speak of these legends, about the deeds which were performed by the Fellowship of the Silver Stallion, because these stories are recorded elsewhere. Some may be true, the others are certainly not true; but it is indisputable that Count Manuel grew steadily in power and wealth and proud repute. Miramon Lluagor still served him, half-amusedly, as Dom Manuel’s seneschal; kings now were Manuel’s copartners; and the former swineherd had somehow become the fair and trusty cousin of emperors. And Madame Niafer, the great Count’s wife, was everywhere stated, without any contradiction from her, to be daughter to the late Sultan of Barbary.

===Chinese===
波伊捷斯的人们讲述着许多动听的故事，说的是救主曼努埃尔在位年间亲身建立、又推动促成的种种功业。他们也讲许多听来难以凭信的事情，因此不曾收入本书：因为古老的歌谣与传说，总倾向于把曼努埃尔伯爵的全盛之期渲染成一段世所罕见的黄金时代。

的确，有那么多光荣的功业被归到曼努埃尔名下，也归到他招聚在身边、组成那赫赫有名的白银骏马会的武士们名下——这伙人里，霍尔登、彬彬有礼的阿纳瓦尔特、市参事科斯、贡法尔与多南德最为出众，虽说其间人人剽悍——以致很难明白，这样短促的一段时光，怎么盛得下这许多行事。但波伊捷斯的说书人早已惯于在讲到一桩漂亮行事时——并非虚言，只是引人生出误解——说一句「曼努埃尔伯爵的时代便是如此」，久而久之，这句颂词竟被当成了纪年之语。于是编年遭到斫削，好教他的声名愈加崇高；曼努埃尔大人的荣光成了一块磁石，把别的岁月年头里的种种豪举，都吸附到了自己身上。

但这里无须谈论这些传说，无须谈论白银骏马会建立的那些功业，因为这些故事别处已有记载。它们或者有几件是真的，其余的固然不是真的；但无可争辩的是，曼努埃尔伯爵的权势、财富与骄人的声名，都在稳步增长。米拉蒙·卢亚戈尔仍旧半带玩味地侍奉着他，做着曼努埃尔大人的总管；如今国王们已成了曼努埃尔的合伙人；而昔日那个猪倌，不知怎么竟成了众皇帝俊美而可靠的表兄弟。至于这位伟大伯爵的夫人妮亚菲尔夫人，则到处被人说成是已故巴巴里苏丹（Sultan of Barbary）的女儿——而她自己从不曾表示异议。

===Original===
Guivric the Sage illuminated the tree which showed the glorious descent of Dame Niafer from Kaiumarth, the first of all kings, and the first to teach men to build houses: and this tree hung in the main hall of Storisende. “For even if some errors may have crept in here and there,” said Dame Niafer, “it looks very well.”

“But, my dear,” said Manuel, “your father was not the Sultan of Barbary: instead, he was the second groom at Arnaye, and all this lineage is a preposterous fabrication.”

“I said just now that some errors may have crept in here and there,” assented Dame Niafer, composedly, “but the point is, that the thing really looks very well, and I do not suppose that even you deny that.”

“No, I do not deny that this glowing mendacity adds to the hall’s appearance.”

“So now, you see for yourself!” said Niafer, triumphantly. And after that her new ancestry was never questioned.

===Chinese===
智者吉夫里克为那棵谱系树施了彩绘，树上展示着妮亚菲尔夫人出自凯乌马尔特（Kaiumarth）——万王之首、第一个教人建造房屋的君王——的光荣世系：这棵树便悬在斯托里森德的主厅里。「即便这里那里难免混进了些许差错，」妮亚菲尔夫人说，「它瞧着可是非常好。」

「可是，亲爱的，」曼努埃尔说，「你父亲并不是巴巴里苏丹：他本是阿尔奈的第二马夫，而这整副世系，是一派荒唐的捏造。」

「我方才就说了，这里那里难免混进了些许差错，」妮亚菲尔夫人不动声色地应道，「可要紧的是，这东西瞧着当真非常好，我想连你也不至于否认这一点。」

「是不否认，这团金光闪闪的谎话，确实给厅堂增色。」

「这么说，你自己也看见了！」妮亚菲尔得意洋洋地说。自此以后，她这副新得的家谱再没有人质疑过。

===Original===
And in the meanwhile Dom Manuel had sent messengers over land and sea to his half-sister Math at Rathgor, bidding her sell the mill for what it would fetch. She obeyed, and brought to Manuel’s court her husband and their two boys, the younger of whom rose later to be Pope of Rome. Manuel gave the miller the vacant fief of Montors; and thereafter you could nowhere have found a statelier fine lady than the Countess Matthiette de Montors. She was still used to speak continually of what was becoming to people of our station in life, but it was with a large difference; and she got on with Niafer as well as could be expected, but no better.

And early in the summer of the first year of Manuel’s reign (just after Dom Manuel fetched to Storisende the Sigel of Scoteia, as the spoils of his famous fight with Oriander the Swimmer), the stork brought to Niafer the first of the promised boys. For the looks of the thing, this child was named, not after the father whom Manuel had just killed, but after the Emmerick who was Manuel’s nominal father: and it was this Emmerick that afterward reigned long and notably in Poictesme.

So matters went prosperously with Dom Manuel, and there was nothing to trouble his peace of mind, unless it were some feeling of responsibility for the cult of Sesphra, whose worship was now increasing everywhere among the nations. In Philistia, in particular, Sesphra was now worshipped openly in the legislative halls and churches, and all other religion, and all decency, was smothered under the rituals of Sesphra. Everywhere to the west and north his followers were delivering windy discourses and performing mad antics, and great hurt came of it all by and by. But if this secretly troubled Dom Manuel; the Count, here as elsewhere, exercised to good effect his invaluable gift for holding his tongue.

Nor did he ever speak of Freydis either, though it is recorded that when news came of the end which she had made in Teamhair under the oppression of the Druids and the satirists, Dom Manuel went silently into the Room of Ageus, and was not seen any more that day. That in such solitude he wept is improbable, for his hard vivid eyes had forgotten this way of exercise, but it is highly probable that he remembered many things, and found not all of them to his credit.

===Chinese===
与此同时，曼努埃尔大人已遣使者由陆路海路去拉斯戈尔，找他同父异母的姐姐玛特，吩咐她把磨坊能卖多少便卖多少。她遵命照办，把丈夫和他们的两个男孩带到了曼努埃尔的宫廷，其中年幼的一个，后来升作了罗马教皇。曼努埃尔把蒙托尔空缺的采邑给了那磨坊主；从此以后，你再无处能寻到一位比蒙托尔的玛蒂埃特伯爵夫人（Countess Matthiette de Montors）更气派的名媛贵妇了。她照旧不住口地念叨，什么样的言行才配得上我们这般身份的人——只是这口气里的气派已大不相同；她与妮亚菲尔相处，好得恰如所能预料，但也仅止于此。

而在曼努埃尔在位头一年的初夏——就在曼努埃尔大人把斯科泰亚的西格尔（the Sigel of Scoteia）携到斯托里森德之后不久，那是他与泅水者奥里安德那场著名之战的战利品——鹳鸟给妮亚菲尔送来了许下的男孩中的头一个。为了场面好看，这孩子取名不曾随他那刚被曼努埃尔杀死的父亲，而是随了曼努埃尔名义上的父亲埃默里克：日后长久而显赫地君临波伊捷斯的，正是这个埃默里克。

就这样，曼努埃尔大人诸事顺遂，再没有什么来搅扰他心境的安宁，除非是心里惦着塞斯弗拉崇拜的几分责任——如今这崇拜正在列国之间处处滋长。尤其是在非利士，塞斯弗拉已公然在议事厅与教堂里受人礼拜，一切别的宗教、一切体面，都闷死在塞斯弗拉的仪轨之下了。在西方和北方，他的信众到处发表空疏的讲词、演出癫狂的丑态，日后果然由此酿成大害。但若说这事暗暗烦扰着曼努埃尔大人；这位伯爵在此处也如在别处一样，把他那缄口不言的无价天赋施展得颇有成效。

他也从不曾说起弗蕾迪丝，虽然据记载，当她的结局传到之时——她在德鲁伊与讽刺诗人的欺压之下，在塔拉（Teamhair）为自己作成的结局——曼努埃尔大人一言不发地走进阿杰乌斯之室，那一天再没有人见到过他。说他在那样的独处中哭泣，是不大可能的，因为他那双坚硬明亮的眼睛早已忘掉了这一种运动；但他多半想起了许多事情，而且发觉它们并非件件都为他增光。

===Original===
So matters went prosperously with gray Manuel; he had lofty palaces and fair woods and pastures and ease and content, and whensoever he went into battle attended by his nine lords of the Silver Stallion, his adversaries perished; he was esteemed everywhere the most lucky and the least scrupulous rogue alive: to crown all which the stork brought by and by to Storisende the second girl, whom they named Dorothy, for Manuel’s mother. And about this time too, came a young poet from England (Ribaut they called him, and he met an evil end at Coventry not long thereafter), bringing to Dom Manuel, where the high Count sat at supper, a goose-feather.

The Count smiled, and he twirled the thing between his fingers, and he meditated. He shrugged, and said: “Needs must. But for her ready wit, my head would have been set to dry on a silver pike. I cannot well ignore that obligation, if she, as it now seems, does not intend to ignore it.”

Then he told Niafer he must go into England.

===Chinese===
就这样，灰色的曼努埃尔诸事顺遂；他有了巍峨的宫殿、美好的树林与牧场、安逸与满足；每当他由白银骏马会的九位领主随侍着上阵，他的对头们便都殒灭；他到处被人推许为当世最走运、最无所顾忌的无赖：而锦上添花的是，鹳鸟不久又给斯托里森德送来了第二个女孩，他们给她取名多萝西，用的是曼努埃尔母亲的名字。也大约在这时候，来了一个英格兰的年轻诗人——人们叫他里博（Ribaut），不久之后他在考文垂落了个悲惨下场——给正在进晚餐的曼努埃尔大人、那位尊贵的伯爵，送来一根鹅毛。

伯爵微微一笑，把那东西夹在指间捻着，沉吟起来。他耸了耸肩，说道：「势所必然。若不是亏了她那随机应变的急智，我的脑袋早已被挑在银枪尖上晾干了。这份情分我难以置之不理，既然如今看样子，她也不打算置之不理。」

随后他告诉妮亚菲尔，他必须去一趟英格兰。

===Original===
Niafer looked up from the marmalade with which she was finishing off her supper, to ask placidly, “And what does that dear yellow-haired friend of yours want with you now?”

“My dear, if I knew the answer to that question it would not be necessary for me to travel oversea.”

“It is easy enough to guess, though,” Dame Niafer said darkly, although, in point of fact, she too was wondering why Alianora should have sent for Manuel; “and I can quite understand how in your sandals you prefer not to have people know about such doings, and laughing at you everywhere, again.”

Dom Manuel did not reply; but he sighed.

“﻿—And if any importance whatever were attached to my opinion in this house I might be saying a few things; but, as it is, it is much more agreeable, all around, to let you go your own hardheaded way and find out by experience that what I say is true. So now, Manuel, if you do not mind, I think we had better be talking about something else a little more pleasant.”

===Chinese===
妮亚菲尔正用橘子酱给这顿晚餐收尾，闻言从酱碟上抬起眼来，安安稳稳地问：「你那位亲爱的黄头发朋友，这回又要找你做什么呀？」

「亲爱的，我若知道这个问题的答案，也就不必漂洋过海走这一遭了。」

「猜倒是不难猜，」妮亚菲尔夫人意味深长地说——尽管实际上，她自己也正纳闷阿莉亚诺拉为什么要遣人召曼努埃尔去；「你那双凉鞋里是什么滋味，我也十足明白：你宁可别让人知道这类勾当，免得人家又到处拿你取笑。」

曼努埃尔大人没有答话；只是叹了口气。

「——再说，这家里倘若对我的见解有半分看重，我本可以说上几句的；可是照眼前这样，倒还是方方面面都更惬意些：由着你去走你自己那条刚愎的路，日后凭亲身经验发觉我说的句句是真。所以呢，曼努埃尔，你要是不介意，我想我们最好还是谈点别的、稍稍愉快些的话题。」

===Original===
Dom Manuel still did not say anything. The time, as has been noted, was just after supper, and as the high Count and his wife sat over the remnants of this meal, a minstrel was making music for them.

“You are not very cheerful company, I must say,” Niafer observed, in a while, “although I do not for a moment doubt your yellow-haired friend will find you gay enough﻿—”

“No, Niafer, I am not happy tonight.”

“Yes, and whose fault is it? I told you not to take two helpings of that beef.”

“No, no, dear snip, it is not indigestion, but rather it is that music, which is plaguing me.”

“Now, Manuel, how can music bother anybody! I am sure the boy plays his violin very nicely indeed, especially when you consider his age.”

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔大人仍旧一言不发。如前所述，这时刚过晚餐，尊贵的伯爵与夫人对坐着，守着席上的残肴，一名吟游乐师正在为他们奏乐。

「不得不说，你这同伴可真不大叫人开怀，」过了半晌，妮亚菲尔说道，「虽说你那位黄头发朋友会发觉你够快活的，这一点我一刻也不怀疑——」

「不，妮亚菲尔，我今夜并不快活。」

「可不是嘛，这又是谁的错处？我早说过，叫你别吃两份牛肉的。」

「不不，亲爱的小家伙，不是积食，倒是那音乐在折磨我。」

「嗳，曼努埃尔，音乐怎么会让谁难受呢！我敢说那孩子的小提琴拉得实在好，尤其是想想他那个年纪。」

===Original===
Said Manuel:

“Yes, but the long low sobbing of the violin, troubling as the vague thoughts begotten by that season wherein summer is not yet perished from the earth, but lingers wanly in the tattered shrines of summer, speaks of what was and of what might have been. A blind desire, the same which on warm moonlit nights was used to shake like fever in the veins of a boy whom I remember, is futilely plaguing a gray fellow with the gray wraiths of innumerable old griefs and with small stinging memories of long-dead delights. Such thirsting breeds no good for staid and aging men, but my lips are athirst for lips whose loveliness no longer exists in flesh, and I thirst for a dead time and its dead fervors to be reviving, so that young Manuel may love again.

“Tonight now surely somewhere, while this music sets uncertain and probing fingers to healed wounds, an aging woman, in everything a stranger to me, is troubled just thus futilely, and she too remembers what she half forgets. ‘We that of old were one, and shuddered heart to heart, with our young lips and our souls too made indivisible,’﻿—thus she is thinking, as I think﻿—‘has life dealt candidly in leaving us to potter with half measures and to make nothing of severed lives that shrivel far apart?’ Yes, she tonight is sad as I, it well may be; but I cannot rest certain of this, because there is in young love a glory so bedazzling as to prevent the lover from seeing clearly his co-worshipper, and therefore in that dear time when we served love together I learned no more of her than she of me.

“Of all my failures this is bitterest to bear, that out of so much grieving and aspiring I have gained no assured knowledge of the woman herself, but must perforce become lachrymose over such perished tinsels as her quivering red lips and shining hair! Of youth and love is there no more, then, to be won than virginal breasts and a small white belly yielded to the will of the lover, and brief drunkenness, and afterward such puzzled yearning as now dies into acquiescence, very much as the long low sobbing of that violin yonder dies into stillness now the song is done?”

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔道：

「是啊，可是小提琴那悠长的低泣，正像那个时节所孕育的朦胧思绪一般扰人——那个时节里，夏天还不曾从大地上消亡，只在夏天残破的圣祠间苍白地流连——它诉说着既有之事，也诉说着本可有而未有事。一种盲目的欲望，正是它，曾在温暖的月夜里，像热病似的在一个我记得的男孩血管里战栗，如今正徒劳地折磨着一个灰色的人，用无数旧日悲哀的灰色幽灵，用久已死灭的欢愉那细小而刺人的记忆。这样的渴念，于持重的、上了年纪的人滋生不出半点好处；可我的唇仍渴慕着一双唇，那唇的美好已不复存在于血肉之中；我渴求一段死去的时光连同它死去的炽热重新活转过来，好教年轻的曼努埃尔得以再爱一次。

「今夜此时，确凿无疑，在某处，当这音乐把犹疑而探询的手指按上早已愈合的创口时，有一个上了年纪的女人——她在一切方面都于我是陌生人——正同样这般徒劳地受着煎熬，她也在记起她半已忘却的事。『往昔我们本是一体，心贴着心地战栗，我们年轻的唇、连我们的灵魂也曾熔铸得不可分』——她此刻这样想着，如我这样想着——『生活竟任我们拿半吊子的营生瞎混，任凭两下割离的生命各自远远枯萎、终无所成——生活如此待人，可算得坦诚？』是啊，她今夜或许同我一样悲伤，这大有可能；可我不能断然确信这一点，因为年轻的爱里有一种光荣，炫目得叫恋爱的人看不清与他一同礼拜的伴侣；所以在那段我们一同侍奉爱神的亲爱时光里，我对她所知，并不比她对我所知更多。

「在我一切的失败之中，最难堪承受的是这一桩：我从那么多悲悼与渴慕之中，竟不曾得到关于这个女人本身的任何确凿认识，却只能对着那些逝去的锡箔亮饰——她颤动的红唇、她闪亮的秀发——洒几行热泪！青春与爱情，难道此外再没有别的可以赢取，只剩下听凭恋爱人意志摆布的处女的胸乳与雪白的小腹，只剩下短暂的沉醉，随后便是这般困惑的渴念——它如今正消殒作默许的顺从，恰如那边小提琴悠长的低泣，歌既唱罢，便消殒作寂静么？」

===Original===
So it was that gray Manuel talked in a half voice, sitting there resplendently robed in gold and crimson, and twiddling between his fingers a goose-feather.

“Yes,” Niafer said, presently, “but, for my part, I think he plays very nicely indeed.”

Manuel gave an abrupt slight jerking of the head. Dom Manuel laughed. “Dear snip,” said he, “come, honestly now, what have you been meditating about while I talked nonsense?”

“Why, I was thinking I must remember to look over your flannels the first thing tomorrow, Manuel, for everybody knows what that damp English climate is in autumn﻿—”

“My dearest,” Manuel said, with grave conviction, “you are the archetype and flawless model of all wives.”

===Chinese===
灰色的曼努埃尔便是这样低低地半声说着，坐在那里，一身金红两色的袍服辉煌夺目，指间捻动着一根鹅毛。

「是啊，」过了一会儿，妮亚菲尔说，「不过要叫我说，我倒觉得他拉得实在挺好。」

曼努埃尔的头蓦地轻轻一抖。曼努埃尔大人笑了。「亲爱的小家伙，」他说，「来，老实说吧，方才我讲这些胡话的时候，你都在琢磨些什么？」

「哟，我在想啊，我必须记着明天头一件事就是把你的法兰绒衣裳检点一遍，曼努埃尔，谁都知道英格兰那潮乎乎的秋天是个什么情形——」

「我最亲爱的，」曼努埃尔说，语气郑重而笃定，「你是一切妻子的原型，是无瑕的典范。」

## XXXVII Opinions of Hinzelmann / 三十七 欣策尔曼的见解

===Original===
Now the tale tells that on Michaelmas morning little Melicent, being in a quiet mood that time, sat with her doll in the tall chair by the third window of Ageus while her father wrote at his big table. He was pausing between phrases to think and to bite at his thumbnail, and he was so intent upon this letter to Pope Innocent that he did not notice the slow opening of the third window: and Melicent had been in conference with the queer small boy for some while before Dom Manuel looked up abstractedly toward them. Then Manuel seemed perturbed, and he called Melicent to him, and she obediently scrambled into her father’s lap.

There was silence in the Room of Ageus. The queer small boy sat leaning back in the chair which little Melicent had just left. He sat with his legs crossed, and with his gloved hands clasping his right knee, as he looked appraisingly at Melicent. He displayed a beautiful sad face, with curled yellow hair hanging about his shoulders, and he was dressed in a vermilion silk coat: at his left side, worn like a sword, was a vast pair of shears. He wore also a pointed hat of four interblended colors, and his leather gloves were figured with pearls.

“She will be a woman by and by,” the strange boy said, with a soft and delicate voice, “and then she too will be coming to us, and we will provide fine sorrows for her.”

===Chinese===
故事如今讲到，米迦勒节（Michaelmas）的早晨，小梅利森特（Melicent）那一阵心境安静，抱着娃娃坐在阿杰乌斯（Ageus）的第三扇窗旁的高椅里，她父亲则在书案前写着字。他时不时在字句之间停下来思索，咬着大拇指的指甲；他一心专注在这封写给教皇英诺森（Pope Innocent）的信上，竟不曾察觉第三扇窗正被缓缓推开：待到曼努埃尔大人（Dom Manuel）恍恍惚惚抬头朝他们望去时，梅利森特已同那个古怪的小男孩晤谈了好一阵子。随后曼努埃尔似乎有些心绪不宁，便把梅利森特唤到跟前，她乖乖地爬上了父亲的膝头。

阿杰乌斯之室里一片静默。那古怪的小男孩向后靠着，坐在小梅利森特方才离开的椅子里。他跷着腿，戴手套的双手扣住右膝，一双眼睛品量似的打量着梅利森特。他露出一张俊美而忧郁的脸，鬈曲的金发披垂在肩头，身穿一件朱红色的绸上衣：左边腰间，像佩剑似的挂着一柄硕大的剪刀。他还戴着一顶四色交融的尖顶帽，皮手套上用珍珠缀出花纹。

「她迟早会长成一个女人，」那陌生的男孩用柔软纤弱的声音说道，「到那时她也会到我们这儿来，我们会为她备下精致的哀愁。」

===Original===
“No, Hinzelmann,” Count Manuel replied, as he stroked the round straw-colored head of little Melicent. “This is the child of Niafer. She comes of a race that has no time to be peering out of dubious windows.”

“It is your child too, Count Manuel. Therefore she too, between now and her burial, will be wanting to be made free of my sister Suskind’s kingdom, as you have been made free of it, at a price. Oh, very certainly you have paid little as yet save the one lock of your gray hair, but in time you will pay the other price which Suskind demands. I know, for it is I who collect my sister Suskind’s revenues, and when the proper hour arrives, believe me, Count Manuel, I shall not be asking your leave, nor is there any price which you, I think, will not be paying willingly.”

“That is probable. For Suskind is wise and strange, and the grave beauty of her youth is the fulfilment of an old hope. Life had become a tedious matter of much money and much bloodshed, but she has restored to me the gold and crimson of dawn.”

===Chinese===
「不，欣策尔曼（Hinzelmann），」曼努埃尔伯爵（Count Manuel）一面抚着小梅利森特圆圆的麦秆色脑袋，一面答道，「这是妮亚菲尔（Niafer）的孩子。她出身的那一族人，可没有闲工夫从暧昧的窗子里向外张望。」

「她也是你的孩子呀，曼努埃尔伯爵。所以，从今直到她下葬，她也会渴望得到自由出入我姐姐苏斯金德（Suskind）王国的权利，就像你已花了代价换得的一样。啊，的确，你至今所付无多，只除了那一绺灰发；可到了时候，苏斯金德索要的另一笔代价，你是要照付的。这我知道，因为替我姐姐苏斯金德收账的正是我；等到合宜的时辰一到，请相信我，曼努埃尔伯爵，我是不会来征求你的许可的；而且我想，无论什么样的代价，你没有不肯甘心支付的。」

「那多半是免不了的。因为苏斯金德又智慧又奇异，她青春的端凝之美，是一桩旧日希望的完成。生命曾沦为一桩多金钱、多杀伐的腻人营生，她却把黎明的金黄与绯红归还给了我。」

===Original===
“So, do you very greatly love my sister Suskind?” says Hinzelmann, smiling rather sadly.

“She is my heart’s delight, and the desire of my desire. It was she for whom, unwittingly, I had been longing always, since I first went away from Suskind, to climb upon the gray heights of Vraidex in my long pursuit of much wealth and fame. I had seen my wishes fulfilled, and my dreams accomplished; all the godlike discontents which ennobled my youth had died painlessly in cushioned places. And living had come to be a habit of doing what little persons expected, and youth was gone out of me, and I, that used to follow with a high head after my own thinking and my own desires, could not any longer very greatly care for anything. Now I am changed: for Suskind has made me free once more of the Country of the Young and of the ageless self-tormenting youth of the gray depths which maddened Ruric, but did not madden me.”

“Look you, Count Manuel, but that penniless young nobody, Ruric the clerk, was not trapped as you are trapped. For from the faith of others there is no escape upon this side of the window. World-famous Manuel the Redeemer has in this place his luck and prosperity to maintain until the orderings of unimaginative gods have quite destroyed the Manuel that once followed after his own thinking. For even the high gods here note with approval that you have become the sort of person in whom the gods put confidence, and so they favor you unscrupulously. Here all is prearranged for you by the thinking of others. Here there is no escape for you from acquiring a little more wealth today, a little more meadowland tomorrow, with daily a little more applause and honor and envy from your fellows, along with always slowly increasing wrinkles and dulling wits and an augmenting paunch, and with the smug approval of everybody upon earth and in heaven. That is the reward of those persons whom you humorously call successful persons.”

===Chinese===
「那么，你非常非常爱我的姐姐苏斯金德么？」欣策尔曼说，笑得颇为凄然。
