Manuel gave it up, and shrugged. “Well, let us conquer as we may, so that God be on our side.”

Miramon replied: “Never fear! He shall be, in every shape and attribute.”

===Chinese===
「这不是回答，是个问题。而这问题之所以发了霉，是因为它被人翻弄的次数太多，却从来没有一个人能把它料理清楚。」

曼努埃尔撒手不管了，耸了耸肩。「好吧，咱们只管尽力去征服，只要上帝站在咱们这边。」

米拉蒙答道：「尽管放心！他会在的——以每一种形相、每一种属性都在。」

===Original===
So Miramon did what was requisite, and from the garrets and dustheaps of Vraidex came strong allies. For, to begin with, Miramon dealt unusually with a little fish, and as a result of these dealings came to them, during the afternoon of the last Thursday in September, as they stood on the seashore north of Manneville, a darkly colored champion clad in yellow. He had four hands, in which he carried a club, a shell, a lotus and a discus; and he rode upon a stallion whose hide glittered like new silver.

Manuel said, “This is a good omen, that the stallion of Poictesme should have aid brought to it by yet another silver stallion.”

“Let us not speak of this bright stallion,” Miramon hastily replied, “for until this Yuga is over he has no name. But when the minds of all men are made clear as crystal then a christening will be appointed for this stallion, and his name will be Kalki, and by the rider upon this stallion Antan will be redeemed.”

===Chinese===
于是米拉蒙行了该行的法事，从弗赖德克斯的阁楼与灰堆里，来了强有力的盟友。因为，头一样，米拉蒙拿一条小鱼异乎寻常地作了一番法；这些作为的结果是，九月最后一个星期四的午后，正当他们站在曼纳维尔（Manneville）以北的海岸上时，来了一位肤色黝黑、身披黄衣的豪杰。他生着四只手，手中拿着一根棍棒、一枚法螺、一朵莲花和一只法轮；他骑一匹牡马，马皮闪烁如新银。

曼努埃尔说：「这是吉兆：波伊捷斯的骏马，竟由又一匹白银骏马引来了援助。」

「这匹亮晃晃的骏马咱们还是别提，」米拉蒙急忙答道，「因为在这个尤加（Yuga）完结之前，他还没有名字。可等到所有人的心智都变得水晶般澄澈，那时便要为这匹骏马择期举行命名礼，他的名字将是卡尔基（Kalki），而凭着这匹骏马上的骑手，安坦（Antan）将得救赎。」

===Original===
“Well,” Manuel said, “that seems fair enough. Meanwhile, with this dusky gentleman’s assistance, I gather, we are to redeem Poictesme.”

“Oh, no, Dom Manuel, he is but the first of our Redeemers, for there is nothing like the decimal system, and you will remember it was in our treaty that in Poictesme all things are to go by tens forever.”

Thereafter Miramon did what was requisite with some acorns, and the splutterings were answered by low thunder. So came a second champion to aid them. This was a pleasant looking young fellow with an astonishingly red beard: he had a basket slung over his shoulder, and he carried a bright hammer. He rode in a chariot drawn by four goats.

===Chinese===
「嗯，」曼努埃尔说，「这话听上去够公道的了。与此同时，据我理解，有这位黝黑的先生帮衬，我们就该去救赎波伊捷斯了。」

「哦，不，曼努埃尔大人，他不过是我们的救主里头的头一位，因为没有什么比得上十进制；你会记得，我们的秘约里订明：在波伊捷斯，一切永远都按十来办。」

此后米拉蒙又拿几枚橡子行了该行的法事，噼啪的爆响得到低低的雷声应和。于是第二位豪杰来援助他们了。这是个模样讨人喜欢的年轻汉子，蓄着一把红得惊人的胡须：他肩头挎着一只篮子，手里提着一柄亮堂堂的锤子。他乘坐一辆由四头山羊拉的战车。

===Original===
“Come, this is certainly a fine stalwart fighting-man,” says Manuel, “and today is a lucky day for me, and for this ruddy gentleman also, I hope.”

“Today is always his day,” Miramon replied, “and do you stop interrupting me in my incantations, and hand me that flute.”

So Manuel stayed as silent as that brace of monstrous allies while Miramon did yet another curious thing with a flute and a palm-branch. Thereafter came an amber-colored champion clad in dark green, and carrying a club and a noose for the souls of the dead. He rode upon a buffalo, and with him came an owl and a pigeon.

===Chinese===
「嘿，这一位确凿是一员精壮的好战将，」曼努埃尔说，「今天是我的吉利日子，我希望，对这位红彤彤的先生也是。」

「今天永远是他的日子，」米拉蒙答道，「你快别在我念咒的当口打岔了，把那支笛子递给我。」

于是曼努埃尔同那双怪异的盟友一般缄默下来，任米拉蒙拿一支笛子和一根棕榈枝又作了一件玄妙的法。随后又来了一位琥珀肤色的豪杰，身穿墨绿的衣裳，拿着一根棍棒和一条勾取亡魂的套索。他骑一头水牛，随他同来的有一只猫头鹰和一只鸽子。

===Original===
“I think﻿—” said Manuel.

“You do not!” said Miramon. “You only talk and fidget, because you are upset by the appearance of your allies; and such talking and fidgeting is very disturbing to an artist who is striving to reanimate the past.”

Thus speaking, Miramon turned indignantly to another evocation. It summoned a champion in a luminous chariot drawn by scarlet mares. He was golden-haired, with ruddy limbs, and was armed with a bow and arrows: he too was silent, but he laughed, and you saw that he had several tongues. After him came a young shining man who rode on a boar with golden bristles and bloodied hoofs: this warrior carried a naked sword, and on his back, folded up like a cloth, was a ship to contain the gods and all living creatures. And the sixth Redeemer was a tall shadow-colored person with two long gray plumes affixed to his shaven head: he carried a sceptre and a thing which, Miramon said, was called an ankh, and the beast he rode on was surprising to observe, for it had the body of a beetle, with human arms, and the head of a ram, and the four feet of a lion.

===Chinese===
「我想——」曼努埃尔说。

「你才不想呢！」米拉蒙说。「你不过是喋喋不休、坐立不安，因为你叫盟友们的模样搅得心神不宁；对一个正竭力唤起往昔还魂的艺术家说来，这样的喋喋不休和坐立不安最是扰人了。」

米拉蒙这样说着，愤然转向另一场召唤。这一场召来一位乘发光战车的豪杰，战车由几匹猩红的牝马拉着。他一头金发，四肢红润，佩着一张弓和箭：他同样缄默，但他在笑，而且你看得出他有好几条舌头。他之后来了一位容光焕发的年轻人，骑一头金鬃血蹄的野猪：这武士提着一柄出鞘的剑，背上像叠一方布似的折着一只船，一只装得下诸神与一切生灵的船。而第六位救主是个高高的、影子色的人物，剃光的头颅上插着两根长长的灰色翎羽：他拿着一柄权杖，还有一件米拉蒙说叫做「安卡（ankh）」的东西；他坐骑的模样瞧着真叫人称奇，因为它长着甲虫的身子、人的手臂、公羊的头和狮子的四足。

===Original===
“Come,” Manuel said, “but I have never seen just such a steed as that.”

“No,” Miramon replied, “nor has anybody else, for this is the Hidden One. But do you stop your eternal talking, and pass me the salt and that young crocodile.”

With these two articles Miramon dealt so as to evoke a seventh ally. Serpents were about the throat and arms of this champion, and he wore a necklace of human skulls: his long black hair was plaited remarkably; his throat was blue, his body all a livid white except where it was smeared with ashes. He rode upon the back of a beautiful white bull. Next, riding on a dappled stag, came one appareled in vivid stripes of yellow and red and blue and green: his face was dark as a raincloud, he had one large round eye, white tusks protruded from his lips, and he carried a gaily painted urn. His unspeakable attendants leaped like frogs. The jolliest looking of all the warriors came thereafter, with a dwarfish body and very short legs; he had a huge black-bearded head, a flat nose, and his tongue hung from his mouth and waggled as he moved. He wore a belt and a necklace, and nothing else whatever except the plumes of the hawk arranged as a headdress: and he rode upon a great sleek tortoiseshell cat.

===Chinese===
「嘿，」曼努埃尔说，「这样的坐骑我却当真从来没有见过。」

「可不是，」米拉蒙答道，「旁人也没有见过，因为这一位是『隐匿者』（the Hidden One）。可你把你那没完没了的话头停一停，把盐递给我，还有那条小鳄鱼。」

米拉蒙拿这两样东西行法，召来了第七位盟友。这位豪杰的脖颈和手臂上盘着蛇，颈间戴一串人头骨串成的项圈：他的黑长发编得煞是出奇；喉咙发蓝，通体青白，唯独抹着灰的地方除外。他骑在一头漂亮的白公牛背上。随后，骑一头花斑牡鹿又来了一位，穿一身黄红蓝绿、色彩鲜明的条纹衣：他的脸黑得像雨云，生着一只又大又圆的眼睛，嘴唇间支出两根白色长牙，手里捧一只彩绘得喜气洋洋的瓮。他那些难以名状的随从们像青蛙一样蹦跳。此后来的是众武士里模样最快活的一位，身量矮短，两条腿短得很；顶着一颗黑须蓬然的巨头，塌鼻梁，舌头垂在嘴外，一走动便晃荡。他系一条腰带、戴一条项圈，除此以外只有一副鹰羽插成的头冠，浑身上下再无别物：他骑一只乌光水滑的大玳瑁猫。

===Original===
Now when these unusual appearing allies stood silently aligned before them on the seashore, Dom Manuel said, with a polite bow toward this appalling host, that he hardly thought Duke Asmund would be able to withstand such Redeemers. But Miramon repeated that there was nothing like the decimal system.

“That half-brother of mine, who is lord of the tenth kind of sleeping, would nicely round off this dizain,” says Miramon, scratching his chin, “if only he had not such a commonplace, black-and-white appearance, apart from being one of those dreadful Realists, without a scrap of aesthetic feeling﻿—No, I like color, and we will levy now upon the West!”

So Miramon dealt next with a little ball of bright feathers. Then a last helper came to them, riding on a jaguar, and carrying a large drum and a flute from which his music issued in the shape of flames. This champion was quite black, but he was striped with blue paint, and golden feathers grew all over his left leg. He wore a red coronet in the shape of a rose, a short skirt of green paper, and white sandals; and he carried a red shield that had in its centre a white flower with the four petals placed crosswise. Such was he who made up the tenth.

===Chinese===
这时，这些模样非比寻常的盟友已在海岸上默然列队立于他们面前，曼努埃尔大人朝这支骇人的大军恭恭敬敬鞠了一躬，说，他简直不认为阿斯蒙德公爵（Duke Asmund）抵挡得住这样一群救主。可米拉蒙还是那句话：没有什么比得上十进制。

「我那位同父异母的兄弟，那第十种沉睡之主，本可以把这一组十人凑个囫囵，」米拉蒙挠着下巴说，「只可惜他生就一副太平淡、太非黑即白的相貌，何况还是那批可怕的现实主义者（Realist）中的一个，没有一丝一毫的美感——不，我喜欢彩色，咱们现在就向西方征调！」

于是米拉蒙接着拿一小团亮泽的羽毛行法。随后最后一位帮手来了，骑着一头美洲豹，带着一面大鼓和一支笛子，他的乐声从笛管里出来时，竟化作火焰的形状。这位豪杰浑身黢黑，却画着一道道蓝彩，左腿上生满了金色的羽毛。他头戴一顶玫瑰花形的红冠，身穿一条绿纸的短裙，脚踏白凉鞋；手里执一面红盾，盾心是一朵白花，四片花瓣摆成十字。凑足第十名的，正是这样一位。

===Original===
Now when this terrible dizain was completed the lord of the seven madnesses laid fire to a wisp of straw, and he cast it to the winds, saying that thus should the anger of Miramon Lluagor pass over the land. Then he turned to these dreadful ten whom he had revivified from the dustheaps and garrets of Vraidex, and it became apparent that Miramon was deeply moved.

Said Miramon:

“You, whom I made for man’s worship when earth was younger and fairer, hearken, and learn why I breathe new life into husks from my scrapheaps! Gods of old days, discrowned, disjected, and treated as rubbish, hark to the latest way of the folk whose fathers you succored! They have discarded you utterly. Such as remember deride you, saying:

===Chinese===
这骇人的一组十人既已凑齐，七狂之主便点着一小把稻草，抛给风，说道米拉蒙·卢亚戈尔的愤怒便要这样掠过这片大地。然后他转向这可怕的十位——他使他们从弗赖德克斯的灰堆与阁楼里重获生机的十位——可以看出，米拉蒙深深动了感情。

米拉蒙说道：

「你们——大地更年轻、更美好的年月里，我为着人的礼拜造下的你们——听着，且弄明白我为什么往我废料堆里的空壳吹进新生命！旧日的诸神，被褫夺了冠冕、被拆散掷弃、被当作垃圾的诸神，听听那些其父辈受过你们救护的百姓如今行事的最新做派！他们已把你们彻底抛弃了。还记得你们的人这样讥笑你们，说道：

===Original===
“ ‘The brawling old lords that our grandfathers honored have perished, if they indeed were ever more than some curious notions bred of our grandfathers’ questing, that looked to find God in each rainstorm coming to nourish their barley, and God in the heat-bringing sun, and God in the earth which gave life. Even so was each hour of their living touched with odd notions of God and with lunacies as to God’s kindness. We are more sensible people, for we understand all about the freaks of the wind and the weather, and find them in no way astounding. As for whatever gods may exist, they are civil, in that they let us alone in our lifetime; and so we return their politeness, knowing that what we are doing on earth is important enough to need undivided attention.’

“Such are the folk that deride you, such are the folk that ignore the gods whom Miramon fashioned, such are the folk whom today I permit you freely to deal with after the manner of gods. Do you now make the most of your chance, and devastate all Poictesme in time for an earlyish supper!”

The faces of these ten became angry, and they shouted, “Blaerde Shay Alphenio Kasbue Gorfons Albuifrio!”

===Chinese===
「『我们祖父辈崇奉过的那些喧嚣老爵爷们早已死绝了——倘若他们当真不只是我们祖父辈的求索里孵出的几种古怪念头的话：那求索指望在每一场滋养大麦的时雨里寻到上帝，在带来炎热的太阳里寻到上帝，在赐予生命的大地里寻到上帝。就连他们活着的每一时辰，也都被关于上帝的古怪念头、被关于上帝仁慈的疯癫沾染过。我们是更明白事理的人，因为风和天气的种种古怪脾气我们全都一清二楚，一点也不觉得它们有什么惊人。至于无论可能存在什么神明，他们都很讲礼貌——一辈子不来打搅我们；我们也还他们的礼，因为我们知道自己在这世上做的事要紧得很，需要专心致志、心无二用。』

「讥笑你们的是这样的百姓，无视米拉蒙所造诸神的是这样的百姓，今天我任凭你们以神明之道随意对付的，也正是这样的百姓。你们这就把这机会用足，把整个波伊捷斯毁个干净，掐准时间，还赶得上吃一顿不算太晚的晚饭！」

这十位的脸色转成怒容，齐声呐喊：「Blaerde Shay Alphenio Kasbue Gorfons Albuifrio！」

===Original===
All ten went up together from the sea, traveling more swiftly than men travel, and what afterward happened in Poictesme was for a long while a story very fearful to hear and heard everywhere.

Manuel did not witness any of the tale’s making as he waited alone on the seashore. But the land was sick, and its nausea heaved under Manuel’s wounded feet, and he saw that the pale, gurgling, glistening sea appeared to crawl away from Poictesme slimily. And at Bellegarde and Naimes and Storisende and Lisuarte, and in all the strongly fortified inland places, Asmund’s tall fighting-men beheld one or another of the angry faces which came up from the sea, and many died swiftly, as must always happen when anybody revives discarded dreams, nor did any of the Northmen die in a shape recognizable as human.

When the news was brought to Dom Manuel that his redemption of Poictesme was completed, then Dom Manuel unarmed, and made himself presentable in a tunic of white damask and a girdle adorned with garnets and sapphires. He slipped over his left shoulder a baldric set with diamonds and emeralds, to sustain the unbloodied sword with which he had conquered here as upon Vraidex. Over all he put on a crimson mantle. Then the former swineherd concealed his hands, not yet quite healed, with white gloves, of which the one was adorned with a ruby, and the other was a sapphire; and, sighing, Manuel the Redeemer (as he was called thereafter) entered into his kingdom, and they of Poictesme received him far more gladly than he them.

Thus did Dom Manuel enter into the imprisonment of his own castle and into the bonds of high estate, from which he might not easily get free to go a-traveling everywhither, and see the ends of this world and judge them. And they say that in her low red-pillared palace Suskind smiled contentedly and made ready for the future.

===Chinese===
十位一起从海上腾起，行进得比人所能行进的快得多；此后发生在波伊捷斯的事，很长一段时间里都是一个听来骇人、而且到处听人讲说的故事。

曼努埃尔独自候在海边，这段故事的酿成他一星半点也不曾目睹。可是大地病了，它的恶心在曼努埃尔受伤的脚底下起伏翻涌；他还看见那苍白、汩汩作响、波光粼粼的大海，仿佛黏腻地爬着，要躲开波伊捷斯似的。在贝勒加德（Bellegarde）、奈梅斯（Naimes）、斯托里森德（Storisende）和利苏阿尔特（Lisuarte），在所有设防坚固的内陆据点，阿斯蒙德麾下高大的战士们都瞥见了从海里上来的这一张或那一张怒容，许多人顷刻殒命——每逢有人复活被抛弃的梦，必然总有这样的结局；北方人（Northmen）死去时，也没有一个还保有可辨认为人形的形状。

当消息送到曼努埃尔大人那里，说他对波伊捷斯的救赎已经完成，曼努埃尔大人便卸了甲，把自己拾掇得体面可观：穿一袭白锦缎的束腰外衣，系一条缀着石榴石与蓝宝石的腰带。他往左肩斜挎一条嵌着钻石与祖母绿的佩剑绶带，承着那柄不曾沾血的剑——凭着它，他在弗赖德克斯怎样征服，在这里也怎样征服。最外面他披一领绯红的大氅。随后，这位昔日的猪倌用白手套遮住他那双还没有大好的手，一只手套上缀着红宝石，另一只上缀着蓝宝石；救主曼努埃尔（Manuel the Redeemer，此后人们便这样称呼他）叹息一声，进了他的王国；波伊捷斯的人们迎接他，远比他迎接他们热切得多。

就这样，曼努埃尔大人走进了他自己城堡的囚禁，走进了显贵身份的束缚，从此再不能轻易脱身，去游遍四方，看一看这世界的尽头，并对它们下断语。人们说，在她那低矮的红柱宫殿里，苏斯金德（Suskind）心满意足地微笑了，为将来做好了准备。

## XXXV The Troubling Window / 三十五 令人不安的窗户

===Original===
It seemed, in a word, that trouble had forgotten Count Manuel. None the less, Dom Manuel opened a window, at his fine home at Storisende, on a fine, sunlit, warmish morning (for this was the last day of April) to confront an outlook more perturbing than his hard vivid eyes had yet lighted on.

So he regarded it for a while. Considerately Dom Manuel now made experiments with three windows in this Room of Ageus, and found how, in so far as one’s senses could be trusted, the matter stood. Thereafter, as became an intelligent person, he went back to his writing-table, and set about signing the requisitions and warrants and other papers which Ruric the clerk had left there.

Yet all the while Dom Manuel’s gaze kept lifting to the windows. There were three of them, set side by side, each facing south. They were of thick clear glass, of a sort whose manufacture is a lost art, for these windows had been among the spoils brought back by Duke Asmund from nefarious raidings of Philistia, in which country these windows had once been a part of the temple of Ageus, an immemorial god of the Philistines. For this reason the room was called the Room of Ageus.

Through these windows Count Manuel could see familiar fields, the long avenue of poplars and the rising hills beyond. All was as it had been yesterday, and as all had been since, nearly three years ago, Count Manuel first entered Storisende. All was precisely as it had been, except, to be sure, that until yesterday Dom Manuel’s table had stood by the farthest window. He could not remember that until today this window had ever been opened, because since his youth had gone out of him Count Manuel was becoming more and more susceptible to draughts.

===Chinese===
一句话，麻烦似乎已经把曼努埃尔伯爵忘了。话虽如此，四月最后一天的这个晴朗、向阳、微暖的早晨，曼努埃尔大人还是在自己斯托里森德（Storisende）那座华美的宅邸里推开了一扇窗，迎面撞见的眺望，却比他那双冷硬明亮的眼睛迄今照见过的任何东西都更教人心神不宁。

于是他对这番景致端详了一阵。接着，曼努埃尔大人细心地在阿杰乌斯之室（Room of Ageus）的三扇窗上逐一做了试验，弄清楚了——就人的感官尚可信赖而言——事情究竟是怎样的。此后，他像一切明白人该做的那样，回到书案前，着手签署文书鲁里克（Ruric）留在那儿的征调令、令状和其他公文。

然而自始至终，曼努埃尔大人的目光总不住抬起来，落向那几扇窗。窗共有三扇，并排而设，都朝南开。那是厚而澄澈的玻璃，这一路玻璃的制法如今已成绝艺，因为这些窗是阿斯蒙德公爵（Duke Asmund）从对非利士（Philistia）罪恶昭彰的劫掠中带回的战利品之一；在那个国度，这些窗曾是非利士人邃古之神阿杰乌斯（Ageus）神庙的一部分。因为这个缘故，这间屋子便唤作阿杰乌斯之室。

透过这些窗，曼努埃尔伯爵能望见熟悉的田野、白杨夹道的林荫路和远处渐次隆起的丘陵。一切都同昨天一样，也同将近三年前曼努埃尔伯爵初入斯托里森德以来的每一天一样。一切分毫不差，只是——说真的——直到昨天，曼努埃尔大人的书案一直摆在离得最远的那扇窗边。他不记得这扇窗在今天以前可曾被打开过，因为自从青春离他而去，曼努埃尔伯爵对穿堂风越来越受不住了。

===Original===
“It is certainly very curious,” Dom Manuel said, aloud, when he had finished with his papers.

He was again approaching the very curious window when his daughter Melicent, now nearly three years old, came noisily, and in an appallingly soiled condition, to molest him. She had bright beauty later, but at three she was one of those children whom human powers cannot keep clean for longer than three minutes.

Dom Manuel kept for her especial delectation a small flat paddle on his writing-table, and this he now caught up.

“Out of the room with you, little pest!” he blustered, “for I am busy.”

So the child, as was her custom, ran back into the hallway, and stood there, no longer in the room, but with one small foot thrust beyond the doorsill, while she laughed up at her big father, and derisively stuck out a tiny curved red tongue at the famed overlord of Poictesme. Then Dom Manuel, as was his custom, got down upon the floor to slap with his paddle at the intruding foot, and Melicent squealed with delight, and pulled back her foot in time to dodge the paddle, and thrust out her other foot beyond the sill, and tried to withdraw that too before it was spanked.

===Chinese===
「这实在是古怪得很。」处理完公文之后，曼努埃尔大人出声说道。

他又朝那扇古怪透顶的窗走去，这时他的女儿梅利森特（Melicent）——她如今快满三岁了——吵吵嚷嚷、浑身脏得吓人地跑来搅扰他。她日后出落得明艳动人，可三岁上她属于那一类孩子：任凭人力，也没法让他们干净超过三分钟。

曼努埃尔大人在书案上特意备着一柄专供她取乐的小小扁平拍板，此刻他便一把抄了起来。

「滚出屋去，小捣蛋鬼！」他咋咋呼呼地喝道，「我忙着呢。」

于是那孩子照她的老规矩跑回门厅里，站在那儿——人已不在屋里，一只小脚却伸出了门槛——仰头朝她那高大的父亲直笑，还冲这位声名赫赫的波伊捷斯（Poictesme）宗主戏弄地吐出一小条弯弯的红舌头。于是曼努埃尔大人也照他的老规矩扑到地板上，挥起拍板去打那只越界的脚；梅利森特乐得尖叫，及时把脚缩回去躲开拍板，又把另一只脚伸出门槛，赶在那只脚挨打之前也急着往回收。

===Original===
So it was they gave over a quarter of an hour to rioting, and so it was that grave young Ruric found them. Count Manuel rather sheepishly arose from the floor, and dusted himself, and sent Melicent into the buttery for some sugar cakes. He told Ruric what were the most favorable terms he could offer the burgesses of Narenta, and he gave Ruric the signed requisitions.

Presently, when Ruric had gone, Dom Manuel went again to the farthest window, opened it, and looked out once more. He shook his head, as one who gives up a riddle. He armed himself, and rode over to Perdigon, whither sainted King Ferdinand had come to consult with Manuel about contriving the assassination of the Moorish general, al-Mota-Wakkil. This matter Dom Manuel deputed to Guivric the Sage; and so was rid of it.

In addition, Count Manuel had on hand that afternoon an appeal to the judgment of God, over some rather valuable farming lands; but it was remarked by the spectators that he botched the unhorsing and severe wounding of Earl Ladinas, and conducted it rather as though Dom Manuel’s heart were not in the day’s business. Indeed, he had reason, for while supernal mysteries were well enough if one were still a harebrained lad, or even if one set out in due form to seek them, to find such mysteries obtruding themselves unsought into the home-life of a well-thought-of nobleman was discomposing, and to have the windows of his own house playing tricks on him seemed hardly respectable.

All that month, too, some memory appeared to trouble Dom Manuel, in the back of his mind, while the lords of the Silver Stallion were busied in the pursuit of Othmar and Othmar’s brigands in the Taunenfels: and as soon as Dom Manuel had captured and hanged the last squad of these knaves, Dom Manuel rode home and looked out of the window, to find matters unchanged.

===Chinese===
就这样，他们把一刻钟的工夫尽数交付给了胡闹；也正是在这般光景里，神情庄重的年轻鲁里克撞见了他们。曼努埃尔伯爵颇为难为情地从地板上站起来，掸了掸身上的土，打发梅利森特到食品储藏室去拿几块糖饼。他告诉鲁里克，他能给纳伦塔（Narenta）的市民们开出的最优厚的条件是什么，又把签好的征调令交给了他。

不久，鲁里克去了，曼努埃尔大人又走到最远的那扇窗前，推开窗，再一次朝外望。他摇了摇头，像个对谜语认输的人。随后他披挂起来，骑马前往佩尔迪贡（Perdigon）——已经成圣的费迪南德国王到了那儿，要与曼努埃尔商议如何设法行刺摩尔人将军阿尔-莫塔-瓦基尔（al-Mota-Wakkil）。这件事，曼努埃尔大人委托给了智者吉夫里克（Guivric the Sage），就此脱了手。

此外，那天下午曼努埃尔伯爵手上还有一桩诉诸上帝裁决的争讼，为的是几块相当值钱的农田；但看客们注意到，他把将拉迪纳斯伯爵（Earl Ladinas）挑落马下、重创于地的那一套办得潦草失色，整场打下来，倒仿佛曼努埃尔大人的心全不在这桩当日的正事上。他也确实有他的缘故：超凡的奥秘，倘若人还是个没头没脑的愣小子，倒也罢了；甚至正经八百地动身去寻访它们，也罢了；可眼见这样的奥秘不请自来、硬闯进一位受人敬重的贵族的家常日子，就教人心烦意乱了——而自家屋子里的窗跟自己耍起把戏来，实在算不得体面。

再说，整整那一个月里，当白银骏马会（the Silver Stallion）的众领主正忙着在陶嫩费尔斯（Taunenfels）追剿奥特马尔（Othmar）和奥特马尔的匪帮时，某种记忆仿佛一直在曼努埃尔大人心底深处作祟；等到曼努埃尔大人把这伙恶徒的最后一股擒获绞决，他便骑马回家，往窗外一望——事情依然如故。

===Original===
Dom Manuel meditated. He sounded the gong for Ruric. Dom Manuel talked with the clerk about this and that. Presently Dom Manuel said: “But one stifles here. Open that window.”

The clerk obeyed. Manuel at the writing-table watched him intently. But in opening the window the clerk had of necessity stood with his back toward Count Manuel, and when Ruric turned, the dark young face of Ruric was impassive.

Dom Manuel, playing with the jeweled chain of office about his neck, considered Ruric’s face. Then Manuel said: “That is all. You may go.”

But Count Manuel’s face was troubled, and for the rest of this day he kept an eye on Ruric the young clerk. In the afternoon it was noticeable that this Ruric went often, on one pretext and another, into the Room of Ageus when nobody else was there. The next afternoon, in broad daylight, Manuel detected Ruric carrying into the Room of Ageus, of all things, a lantern. The Count waited a while, then went into the room through its one door. The room was empty. Count Manuel sat down and drummed with his fingers upon the top of his writing-table.

After a while the third window was opened. Ruric the clerk climbed over the sill. He blew out his lantern.

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔大人沉吟。他敲响铜锣，唤来鲁里克。曼努埃尔大人同文书谈了些这样那样。过了一会儿，曼努埃尔大人说：「不过这儿怪闷的。把那扇窗打开。」

文书依言开了窗。曼努埃尔坐在书案前，目不转睛地盯着他。只是开窗的时候，文书势必背对着曼努埃尔伯爵，待鲁里克转过身来，那张黝黑的年轻面孔不动声色。

曼努埃尔大人摆弄着脖子上那串镶宝石的职衔领链，端详着鲁里克的脸。随后曼努埃尔说：「就这样吧。你可以去了。」

但曼努埃尔伯爵面有忧色，这一天余下的时辰里，他一直留意着年轻文书鲁里克的动静。下午，人们看得出，这位鲁里克借口种种，屡屡趁屋里没人的时候走进阿杰乌斯之室。第二天下午，光天化日之下，曼努埃尔发觉鲁里克带进阿杰乌斯之室的东西，偏偏是一盏提灯。伯爵等了一会儿，便从那屋子唯一的一扇门走了进去。屋里空无一人。曼努埃尔伯爵坐下，手指在书案面上敲着鼓点。

过了一阵，第三扇窗开了。文书鲁里克翻过窗台爬了进来。他吹熄了提灯。

===Original===
“You are braver than I,” Count Manuel said, “it may be. It is certain you are younger. Once, Ruric, I would not have lured any dark and prim-voiced young fellow into attempting this adventure, but would have essayed it myself posthaste. Well, but I have other duties now, and appearances to keep up: and people would talk if they saw a well-thought-of nobleman well settled in life climbing out of his own windows, and there is simply no telling what my wife would think of it.”

The clerk had turned, startled, dropping his lantern with a small crash. His hands went jerkily to his smooth chin, clutching it. His face was white as a leper’s face, and his eyes now were wild and glittering, and his head was drawn low between his black-clad shoulders, so that he seemed a hunchback as he confronted his master. Another queer thing Manuel could notice, and it was that a great lock had been sheared away from the left side of Ruric’s black hair.

“What have you learned,” says Manuel, “out yonder?”

“I cannot tell you,” replied Ruric, laughing sillily, “but in place of it, I will tell you a tale. Yes, yes, Count Manuel, I will tell you a merry story of how a great while ago our common grandmother Eve was washing her children one day near Eden when God called to her. She hid away the children that she had not finished washing: and when the good God asked her if all her children were there, with their meek little heads against His knees, to say their prayers to Him, she answered, Yes. So God told her that what she had tried to hide from God should be hidden from men: and He took away the unwashed children, and made a place for them where everything stays young, and where there is neither good nor evil, because these children are unstained by human sin and unredeemed by Christ’s dear blood.”

The Count said, frowning: “What drunken nonsense are you talking at broad noon? It is not any foolish tatter of legend that I am requiring of you, my boy, but civil information as to what is to be encountered out yonder.”

===Chinese===
「你兴许比我勇敢，」曼努埃尔伯爵说，「这不好说。你比我年轻，倒是千真万确。换了从前，鲁里克，我才不会引诱哪个面色黝黑、嗓音刻板的年轻人来试这番冒险，我会亲自火速去闯一道。唉，可是如今我另有职守，还得维持体面：要是让人瞧见一位受人敬重的贵族、一个安家立业的人从自家的窗户里往外爬，人家会有闲话的；再说了，我老婆会作何感想，那简直没法说。」

文书吓了一跳，转过身来，提灯脱手，啪哒一声轻响摔在地上。他的双手抽搐着抬向自己光洁的下巴，一把攥住。他的脸白得像麻风病人的脸，此刻那双眼睛狂乱地闪着光，脑袋深深缩进黑衣的两肩之间，对着主人时活像个驼子。曼努埃尔还能注意到另一桩怪事：鲁里克黑色头发的左边，被剪掉了好大一绺。

「你在那外头都学到些什么了？」曼努埃尔问道。

「我没法告诉您，」鲁里克答道，傻里傻气地笑着，「不过我可以给您讲个故事，算是顶替。是了，是了，曼努埃尔伯爵，我给您讲一个快活的故事：很久很久以前，我们共同的祖母夏娃（Eve）有一天在伊甸（Eden）附近给她的孩子们洗澡，上帝忽然唤她。她把还没洗完的孩子藏了起来：仁慈的上帝问她，她的孩子们是不是都在跟前，是不是都乖乖把小脑袋偎着祂的膝盖、向祂祷告，她回答说，都在。于是上帝告诉她：她试图向上帝隐藏的，也要向世人隐藏。祂带走了那些没洗过的孩子，为他们另造了一个地方——那里的一切都永葆年轻，那里既没有善也没有恶，因为这些孩子既不曾被人类的罪孽玷污，也不曾被基督宝贵的血救赎。」

伯爵皱着眉头说：「光天化日的大晌午，你讲的是什么醉话？我要你交代的可不是什么愚蠢传说的破烂残片，我的孩子，我要的是正正经经的情报：那外头究竟会遇上些什么。」

===Original===
“All freedom and all delight,” young Ruric told him wildly, “and all horror and all rebellion.”

Then he talked for a while. When Ruric had ended this talking, Count Manuel laughed scornfully, and spoke as became a well-thought-of nobleman.

Ruric whipped out a knife, and attacked his master, crying, “I follow after my own thinking and my own desires, you old, smug, squinting hypocrite!”

So Count Manuel caught Ruric by the throat, and with naked hands Dom Manuel strangled the young clerk.

“Now I have ridded the world of much poison, I think,” Dom Manuel said, aloud, when Ruric lay dead at Manuel’s feet. “In any event, I cannot have that sort of talking about my house. Yet I wish I had not trapped the boy into attempting this adventure, which by rights was my adventure. I did not always avoid adventures.”

===Chinese===
「全是自由，全是欢愉，」年轻的鲁里克癫狂地告诉他，「也全是恐怖，全是反叛。」

接着他讲说了一阵。鲁里克讲完之后，曼努埃尔伯爵轻蔑地笑了，说了一番不愧为一位受人敬重的贵族的话。

鲁里克唰地拔出一把刀，扑向他的主人，喊道：「我追随的是我自己的思想、我自己的欲望，你这个上了年纪、自鸣得意的斜眼伪君子！」

于是曼努埃尔伯爵扼住鲁里克的咽喉，凭一双赤手，曼努埃尔大人扼死了这个年轻的文书。

「如今我算替世间除掉了不少毒害，我想，」鲁里克死在曼努埃尔脚边时，曼努埃尔大人出声说道，「无论如何，我家里容不得那一套言辞。不过，我还是希望当初不曾设局诱这孩子来试这番冒险——按理说，这本是我的冒险。我从前也不是事事都躲着冒险的。」

===Original===
He summoned two to take away the body, and then Manuel went to his bedroom, and was clothed by his lackeys in a tunic of purple silk, and a coronet was placed on his gray head, and the trumpets sounded as Count Manuel sat down to supper. Pages in ermine served him, bringing Manuel’s food upon gold dishes, and pouring red wine and white from golden beakers into Manuel’s gold cup. Skilled music-men played upon viols and harps and flutes while the high Count of Poictesme ate richly seasoned food and talked sedately with his wife.

They had not fared thus when Manuel had just come from herding swine, and Niafer was a servant trudging on her mistress’ errands, and when these two had eaten very gratefully the Portune’s bread and cheese. They had not any need to be heartened with rare wines when they endured so many perils upon Vraidex and in Dun Vlechlan because of their love for each other. For these two had once loved marvelously. Now minstrels everywhere made songs about their all-conquering love, which had derided death; and nobody denied that, even now, these two got on together amicably.

But tonight Dame Niafer was fretted, because the pastrycook was young Ruric’s cousin, and was, she feared, as likely as not to fling off in a huff on account of Dom Manuel’s having strangled the clerk.

“Well, then do you raise the fellow’s wages,” said Count Manuel.

===Chinese===
他唤来两个人抬走尸首，随后曼努埃尔去了寝殿，侍仆们替他换上一袭紫色丝绸的束腰外衣，一顶冠冕戴上了他花白的头，号角吹响，曼努埃尔伯爵落座进晚膳。身着貂皮的侍童们服侍他，用金盘给曼努埃尔端上饭菜，从金盏里把红酒和白酒斟进曼努埃尔的金杯。技艺娴熟的乐师们奏着维奥尔琴、竖琴和长笛，这位波伊捷斯尊贵的伯爵吃着调味浓腴的饭菜，同妻子安详从容地交谈。

他们从前可不是这般过活的——那时曼努埃尔刚从放猪的营生里出来，妮亚菲尔（Niafer）还是个替女主人跑腿、辛苦奔忙的仆人，那时这两个人吃着波尔图内（Portune）的面包和奶酪，便感激不尽。他们当年也全不需要什么珍稀的美酒来提气壮胆——在弗赖德克斯（Vraidex）山上、在敦·弗莱赫兰（Dun Vlechlan），他们为了彼此的爱熬过了重重凶险。因为他们俩曾经爱得惊天动地。如今各地的吟游诗人都在编歌，唱他们那战胜一切、嘲弄过死亡的爱情；而且谁也不否认，即便是现在，这两个人相处得也还和睦。

可今晚妮亚菲尔夫人（Dame Niafer）心烦意乱，因为点心厨子是年轻鲁里克的表亲，她担心这人十有八九会一怒之下撂挑子走人——起因则是曼努埃尔大人扼死了那位文书。

「那好，你就给那家伙涨工钱得了。」曼努埃尔伯爵说。

===Original===
“That is easily said, and is exactly like a man. Why, Manuel, you surely know that then the meat-cook, and the butler, too, would be demanding more, and that there would be no end to it.”

“But, my dear, the boy was talking mad blasphemy, and was for cutting my throat with a great horn-handled knife.”

“Of course that was very wrong of him,” said Dame Niafer, comfortably, “and not for an instant, Manuel, am I defending his conduct, as I trust you quite understand. But even so, if you had stopped for a moment to think how hard it is to replace a servant nowadays, and how unreliable is the best of them, I believe you would have seen how completely we are at their mercy.”

Then she told him all about her second waiting-woman, while Manuel said, “Yes,” and “I never heard the like,” and “You were perfectly right, my dear,” and so on, and all the while appeared to be thinking about something else in the back of his mind.

===Chinese===
「说来容易——这可正是男人才说得出的话。哎呀，曼努埃尔，你总该如何道，这么一来，掌肉的厨子，还有管家，也都要跟着多讨，那就没完没了啦。」

「可是，亲爱的，那小子满口疯癫的渎神之言，还抡着一把大号角柄刀要割我的喉咙呢。」

「他那样当然大错特错，」妮亚菲尔夫人心安理得地说，「而且我一刻也不曾替他的行径辩护，曼努埃尔，这一点我信你是心里明白的。可即便如此，你当时要是肯停下来想上一想：如今要补上一个仆人是何等困难，他们里头顶好的那些又何等靠不住——我相信你就会明白，我们完全捏在他们手心里呢。」

随后她把二等贴身女侍的桩桩件件讲给他听，曼努埃尔在一旁「是呢」「这种事我闻所未闻」「你说得再对不过了，亲爱的」如此等等地应着，而自始至终，他心底深处仿佛都想着别的什么事。

## XXVII They Come to Sargyll / 二十七 他们来到萨吉尔

===Original===
Then Manuel and Niafer put out to sea, and after two days’ voyaging they came to Sargyll and to the hospitality of Queen Freydis. Freydis was much talked about at that time on account of the way in which King Thibaut had come to his ruin through her, and on account of her equally fatal dealings with the Duke of Istria and the Prince of Camwy and three or four other lords. So the ship-captains whom Dom Manuel first approached preferred not to venture among the Red Islands. Then the Jewish master of a trading vessel﻿—a lean man called Ahasuerus﻿—said, “Who forbids it?” and carried them uneventfully from Novogath to Sargyll. They narrate how Oriander the Swimmer followed after the yellow ship, but he attempted no hurt against Manuel, at least not for that turn.

Thus Manuel came again to Freydis. He had his first private talk with her in a room that was hung with black and gold brocade. White mats lay upon the ground, and placed irregularly about the room were large brass vases filled with lotus blossoms. Here Freydis sat on a three-legged stool, in conference with a panther. From the ceiling hung rigid blue and orange and reddish-brown serpents, all dead and embalmed; and in the middle of the ceiling was painted a face which was not quite human, looking downward, with evil eyes half closed, and with its mouth half open in discomfortable laughter.

Freydis was clad in scarlet completely, and, as has been said, a golden panther was talking to her when Dom Manuel came in. She at once dismissed the beast, which smiled amicably at Dom Manuel, and then arched high its back in the manner of all the cat tribe, and so flattened out into a thin transparent goldness, and, flickering, vanished upward as a flame leaves a lampwick.
===Chinese===
于是曼努埃尔和妮亚菲尔扬帆出海，行了两天水路，来到萨吉尔，受到弗蕾迪丝女王的款待。那时节弗蕾迪丝正被人议论纷纷：一来是蒂博国王（King Thibaut）如何经由她而落得败亡，二来是她同伊斯特里亚公爵、坎威王子（the Prince of Camwy）以及其他三四位爵爷的来往，也同样是致命的。于是曼努埃尔大人最先去接洽的几位船长，宁可不到红色群岛（the Red Islands）里去冒险。这时，一艘商船的犹太船主——一个名叫亚哈随鲁（Ahasuerus）的精瘦汉子——说道：「谁禁止了？」便平平安安地把他们从诺沃加斯送到了萨吉尔。据讲述，泅水者奥里安德（Oriander）曾尾随这条黄色的船，但他没有对曼努埃尔下手加害，至少那一趟不曾。

就这样，曼努埃尔又来到了弗蕾迪丝面前。他同她的头一回密谈，是在一间悬着黑金二色织锦的屋子里。地上铺着白席，屋里错落摆着几只大铜瓶，瓶里插满荷花。弗蕾迪丝就坐在一张三条腿的凳子上，正同一头豹子议事。天花板上悬着一条条僵直的蛇，蓝的、橙的、红褐色的，条条已死，都经了防腐；天花板正中画着一张不甚像人的脸，朝下望着，一双恶眼半阖，嘴巴半张，作着叫人坐立难安的笑。

弗蕾迪丝通身上下一袭猩红；方才说过，曼努埃尔大人进屋时，一头金色的豹子正在同她说话。她当即把那头野兽打发走了。豹子朝曼努埃尔大人友爱地笑了笑，随即照猫族一概的模样，把背脊高高拱起，接着摊平成薄薄一片透明的金色，一闪一闪，像火苗离开灯芯似的，向上消散了。

===Original===
“Well, well, you bade me come to you, dear friend, when I had need of you,” says Manuel, very cordially shaking hands, “and nobody’s need could be more great than mine.”

“Different people have different needs,” Freydis replied, rather gravely, “but all passes in this world.”

“Friendship, however, does not pass, I hope.”

She answered slowly: “It is we who pass, so that the young Manuel whom I loved in a summer that is gone, is nowadays as perished as that summer’s gay leaves. What, grizzled fighting-man, have you to do with that young Manuel who had comeliness and youth and courage, but no human pity and no constant love? and why should I be harboring his lighthearted mischiefs against you? Ah, no, gray Manuel, you are quite certain no woman would do that; and people say you are shrewd. So I bid you very welcome to Sargyll, where my will is the only law.”
===Chinese===
「哎呀，哎呀，亲爱的朋友，当初是你吩咐我，等我有用得着你的地方便来找你，」曼努埃尔说道，十分亲热地握手，「而谁的需要也大不过我的了。」

「各人有各人的需要，」弗蕾迪丝答道，神色颇为庄重，「可是在这世上，万事万物都会过去。」

「不过，友谊是不会过去的——我希望。」

她缓缓答道：「过去的正是我们这些人。当年我在一个逝去的夏天里爱过的那个年轻的曼努埃尔，如今已同那年夏天的欢叶一样凋尽。嘿，花白头发的武人，你同那个年轻的曼努埃尔还有什么相干？他有俊美容颜、青春年少与一腔勇气，却没有人的怜恤，没有恒久不渝的爱，我又何苦把他那些轻狂的恶作剧记恨在你头上呢？啊，不，灰色的曼努埃尔，你十足十地笃定，没有哪个女人会那样做；人家还都说你精明呢。所以我竭诚欢迎你来到萨吉尔——在这地方，我的意志就是唯一的法律。」

===Original===
“You at least have not changed,” Dom Manuel replied, with utter truth, “for you appear today, if anything, more fair and young than you were that first night upon Morven when I evoked you from tall flames to lend life to the image I had made. Well, that seems now a lengthy while ago, and I make no more images.”

“Your wife would be considering it a waste of time,” Queen Freydis estimated.

“No, that is not quite the way it is. For Niafer is the dearest and most dutiful of women, and she never crosses my wishes in anything.”

Freydis now smiled a little, for she saw that Manuel believed he was speaking veraciously. “At all events,” said Freydis, “it is a queer thing surely that in the month which is to come the stork will be fetching your second child to a woman resting under my roof and in my golden bed. Yes, Thurinel has just been telling me of your plan, and it is a queer thing. Yet it is a far queerer thing that your first child, whom no stork fetched nor had any say in shaping, but whom you made of clay to the will of your proud youth and in your proud youth’s likeness, should be limping about the world somewhere in the appearance of a strapping tall young fellow, and that you should know nothing about his doings.”
===Chinese===
「至少你是丝毫没有变，」曼努埃尔大人答道，说得千真万确，「因为今日的你，若说有什么不同，反倒比当年莫尔文（Morven）头一个夜晚更美、更年轻了——那一夜，我把你从高高的烈焰里召出来，给我造的泥像赋予生命。唉，那如今看来已是许久以前的事了，如今我也不再造什么泥像了。」

「你那位夫人准会认为这是白白糟蹋工夫，」弗蕾迪丝女王估量道。

「不，事情不是这样的。妮亚菲尔是天下顶顶亲爱、顶顶恭顺的女人，无论什么事，她从不违拗我的心愿。」

弗蕾迪丝这才微微一笑，因为她看出来了：曼努埃尔自以为句句说的是实话。「不管怎么说，」弗蕾迪丝说道，「往后这一个月里，鹳鸟就要把你的第二个孩子，送给一个栖身在我屋檐下、静养在我金床上的女人，这确乎是桩古怪事。是啊，图里内尔（Thurinel）方才还在把你们的打算讲给我听呢，这真是桩古怪事。然而远更古怪的是：你的头一个孩子，鹳鸟不曾送过他来，在捏塑他的事上也没插过一句嘴，他是你拿黏土、凭你骄傲青春的意志、照你骄傲青春的模样造出来的，如今却顶着一副魁梧高大的年轻人的相貌，在世上什么地方一瘸一拐地晃荡，而对他的所作所为，你竟一无所知。」

===Original===
“Ah! what have you heard? and what do you know about him, Freydis?”

“I suspicion many things, gray Manuel, by virtue of my dabblings in that gray art which makes neither for good nor evil.”

“Yes,” said Manuel, practically, “but what do you know?”

She took his hand again. “I know that in Sargyll, where my will is the only law, you are welcome, false friend and very faithless lover.”

He could get no more out of her, as they stood there under the painted face which looked down upon them with discomfortable laughter.
===Chinese===
「啊！你都听说了些什么？关于他，你又知道些什么，弗蕾迪丝？」

「我疑心着许许多多的事，灰色的曼努埃尔，凭的是我在那门灰色法术里的几番涉猎——那门法术既不为善，也不作恶。」

「是啊，」曼努埃尔讲求实际地说，「可你到底知道些什么？」

她又拉起他的手。「我知道，在萨吉尔这地方——我的意志就是唯一的法律——你是受欢迎的：虚伪的朋友，负心透顶的情人。」

他再没能从她嘴里问出更多的话来。两人就那样立在那里，头顶那张画出来的脸朝下望着他们，作着叫人坐立难安的笑。

===Original===
So Manuel and Niafer remained at Sargyll until the baby should be delivered. King Ferdinand, then in the midst of another campaign against the Moors, could do nothing for his vassal just now. But glittering messengers came from Raymond Bérenger, and from King Helmas, and from Queen Stultitia, each to discuss this and that possible alliance and aid by and by. Everybody was very friendly if rather vague. But Manuel for the present considered only Niafer and the baby that was to come, and he let statecraft bide.

Then two other ships, that were laden with Duke Asmund’s men, came also, in an attempt to capture Manuel: so Freydis despatched a sending which caused these soldiers to run about the decks howling like wolves, and to fling away their swords and winged helmets, and to fight one against the other with hands and teeth until all were slain.

The month passed thus uneventfully. And Niafer and Freydis became the best and most intimate of friends, and their cordiality to each other could not but have appeared to the discerning rather ominous.
===Chinese===
于是曼努埃尔和妮亚菲尔便在萨吉尔住下，静候婴孩降生。费迪南德国王（King Ferdinand）那时正陷在对摩尔人的又一场战役之中，一时帮不上他的封臣什么忙。倒有衣饰煌煌的信使们从雷蒙德·贝朗热（Raymond Bérenger）那里、从黑尔马斯国王（King Helmas）那里、从斯图尔提蒂亚女王（Queen Stultitia）那里络绎而来，一个个来商谈日后这种那种可能的结盟与援助。人人都十分友好，只是未免语焉不详。但曼努埃尔眼下记挂的只有妮亚菲尔和那个即将出世的孩子，治国之术嘛，他且由它搁在一旁。

随后又有两条船，载着阿斯蒙德公爵（Duke Asmund）的部众开了过来，企图擒拿曼努埃尔：于是弗蕾迪丝遣出一道法咒，教这些士兵中了疯癫，在甲板上像狼一样嗥叫着四处乱窜，扔掉刀剑和带翼的头盔，赤手空拳、牙齿并用，自相残杀，直到尽数毙命。

这一个月就这样平平安安地过去了。妮亚菲尔和弗蕾迪丝成了最要好、最知心的朋友，两人之间那份亲热劲儿，落在眼尖的人眼里，免不了要觉得颇为不祥。

===Original===
“She seems to be a very good-hearted sort of a person,” Niafer conceded, in matrimonial privacy, “though certainly she is rather queer. Why, Manuel, she showed me this afternoon ten of the drollest figures to which﻿—but, no, you would never guess it in the world﻿—to which she is going to give life some day, just as you did to me when you got my looks and legs and pretty much everything else all wrong.”

“When does she mean to quicken them?” Dom Manuel asked: and he added, “Not that I did, dear snip, but I shall not argue about it.”

“Why, that is the droll part of it, and I can quite understand your unwillingness to admit how little you had remembered about me. When the man who made them has been properly rewarded, she said, with, Manuel, the most appalling expression you ever saw.”

“What were these images like?” asked Dom Manuel.
===Chinese===
「她瞧着倒是个心肠顶好的人，」妮亚菲尔在两口子的私房话里让步道，「虽说她也着实古怪。嗳，曼努埃尔，她今天下午给我看了十个小像，个顶个的滑稽，这些小像呢——不，不，你打死也猜不着——这些小像她打算日后给它们生命，就像你当年待我那样：你把我的相貌和两条腿，差不多把其余的一切，全都弄岔了。」

「她打算几时赋与它们生气呢？」曼努埃尔大人问道：接着又补一句，「我倒没那么弄岔过，亲爱的小家伙，不过这事我也不跟你争。」

「嗳，这正是滑稽的地方。你死不肯认，不认你那时关于我记得的那样少，这我完全体谅。她说：『等造这些小像的人得到了应得的报偿……』——曼努埃尔，说这话时她那副神情，是你平生所见最骇人的一副。」

「那些小像都是些什么模样？」曼努埃尔大人问。

===Original===
Niafer described them: she described them unsympathetically, but there was no doubt they were the images which Manuel had left unquickened upon Upper Morven.

Manuel nodded, smiled, and said: “So the man who made these images is to be properly rewarded! Well, that is encouraging, for true merit should always be rewarded.”

“But, Manuel, if you had seen her look! and seen what horrible misshapen creatures they were﻿—!”

“Nonsense!” said Manuel, stoutly: “you are a dear snip, but that does not make you a competent critic of either physiognomy or sculpture.”
===Chinese===
妮亚菲尔把它们一一描绘了：她描绘得毫无好感，但毫无疑问，这些正是曼努埃尔撂在上莫尔文、没有赋与生气的那几尊泥像。

曼努埃尔点点头，微微一笑，说：「这么说，造这些泥像的人就要得到应得的报偿了！好啊，这可叫人鼓舞，因为真正的功业原该永远得到报偿。」

「可是，曼努埃尔，你要是瞧见她那副神情！再瞧瞧那些东西是些多么狰狞、多么奇形怪状的造物——！」

「胡说！」曼努埃尔斩钉截铁地说，「你是个亲爱的小家伙，可这并不能让你变成品评面相或雕刻的行家。」

===Original===
So he laughed the matter aside; and this, as it happened, was the last that Dom Manuel heard of the ten images which he had made upon Upper Morven. But they of Poictesme declared that Queen Freydis did give life to these figures, each at a certain hour, and that her wizardry set them to live as men among mankind, with no very happy results, because these images differed from naturally begotten persons by having inside them a spark of the life of Audela.

Thus Manuel and his wife came uneventfully to August; all the while there was never a more decorous or more thoughtful hostess than Queen Freydis; and nobody would have suspected that sorcery underlay the running of her household. It was only through Dom Manuel’s happening to arise very early one morning, at the call of nature, that he chanced to be passing through the hall when, at the moment of sunrise, the night-porter turned into an orange-colored rat, and crept into the wainscoting: and Manuel of course said nothing about this to anybody, because it was none of his affair.
===Chinese===
于是他一笑把这事撇开了；而事有凑巧，这竟是曼努埃尔大人最后听到的、关于他在上莫尔文所造那十尊泥像的下落。可是波伊捷斯的人们却断言，弗蕾迪丝女王后来当真给这些泥像一一赋予了生命——每一尊都在某个特定的时辰——还说她的巫术打发他们化作男人、活在我们人类中间，结果却不怎么美满，因为这些泥像同自然孕育的人有所不同：他们的身体里面，装着一点奥德拉（Audela）的生命火花。

就这样，曼努埃尔夫妇平平安安地过到了八月；这一向，再没有哪位女主人比弗蕾迪丝女王更端庄知礼、更体贴入微的了；谁也不会疑心，这座宅院的操持底下竟垫着巫术。只因有一天清晨，曼努埃尔大人碰巧内急，起得极早，恰好在日出那一刻路过厅堂，看见守夜人化作一只橘红色的老鼠，钻进护墙板里去了：这件事，曼努埃尔自然对谁也没有提起，因为那不关他的事。

## XIV They Duel on Morven / 十四 他们在莫尔文决斗

===Original===
So by the light of the seven candles Dom Manuel first saw Queen Freydis in her own shape, and in the appearance which she wore in her own country. What Manuel thought there was never any telling: but every other man who saw Queen Freydis in this appearance declared that instantly all his past life became a drugged prelude to the moment wherein he stood face to face with Freydis, the high Queen of Audela.

Freydis showed now as the most lovely of womankind. She had black plaited hair, and folds of crimson silk were over her white flesh, and over her shoulders was a black cloak embroidered with little gold stars and ink-horns, and she wore sandals of gilded bronze. But in her face was such loveliness as may not be told.

Now Freydis went from one side of the place to the other side, and saw the magics that protected the enclosure. “Certainly, you have me fast,” the high Queen said. “What is it you want of me?”
===Chinese===
就这样，借着七支蜡烛的光，曼努埃尔大人（Dom Manuel）头一回见到了弗蕾迪丝（Freydis）女王本来的形貌，见到了她在自己国度里惯常穿着的那副容颜。曼努埃尔心里想的是什么，从来无人说得出来：但其他每一个见过弗蕾迪丝女王这副容颜的男人都宣称：刹那之间，他过去的一生尽数化作了一剂蒙药的序曲，只引向他当面站着、与奥德拉（Audela）至高的女王弗蕾迪丝相对的这一刻。

此刻显形的弗蕾迪丝，是女人中最美的一位。她编着乌黑的发辫，雪白的肌肤上覆着一层层绯红的绸衣，肩头披一领黑斗篷，上头绣着小小的金色星星和墨水瓶，脚上穿一双鎏金青铜的凉鞋。可她脸上那一种美，却不是言语讲得出的。

这时弗蕾迪丝从场地这一头走到那一头，看清了护住围场的种种法术。「不消说，你确确实实把我困住了，」至高的女王说道，「你想要我做什么？」

===Original===
Manuel showed her the three images which he had made, set there arow. “I need your aid with these.”

Queen Freydis looked at them, and Freydis smiled. “These frozen abortions are painstakingly made. What more can anybody demand?”

Dom Manuel told her that he desired to make an animated and lively figure.

Whereupon she laughed, merrily and sweetly and scornfully, and replied that never would she give such aid.

“Very well, then,” said Manuel, “I have ready the means to compel you.” He showed this lovely woman the instruments of her torture. His handsome young face was very grave, as though already his heart were troubled. He thrust her hand into the cruel vise which was prepared. “Now, sorceress, whom all men dread save me, you shall tell me the Tuyla incantation as the reward of my endeavors, or else a little by a little I shall destroy the hand that has wrought so many mischiefs.”
===Chinese===
曼努埃尔给她看她捏的那三尊泥像，它们一列排开。「我需要你帮这些忙。」

弗蕾迪丝女王看了看它们，弗蕾迪丝笑了。「这些冻僵的怪胎捏得倒是煞费苦心。除此之外，谁还能要求什么呢？」

曼努埃尔大人告诉她，他想要造出一尊会动会活的形象。

她闻言笑了，笑得快活，笑得甜美，又笑得轻蔑，回答说这样的忙她永远也不会帮。

「那好，」曼努埃尔说，「逼你就范的手段，我早已备下了。」他让这位可爱的女人看了看给她预备的刑具。他那张英俊的年轻脸庞十分凝重，仿佛他的心已经在受煎熬了。他把她那只手塞进备好的无情虎钳里。「来吧，女巫，世人个个怕你，唯独我不怕——你就把图伊拉（Tuyla）咒文说出来，算是对我这份辛劳的酬报；不然，我就一点一点地毁掉这只造下无数灾祸的手。」

===Original===
Freydis in the light of the seven candles showed pale as milk. She said: “I am frail and human in this place, and have no power beyond the power of every woman, and no strength at all. Nevertheless, I will tell you nothing.”

Manuel set his hand to the lever, ready to loose destruction. “To tell me what I desire you to tell me will do you no hurt﻿—”

“No,” replied Freydis: “but I am not going to take orders from you or any man breathing.”

“﻿—And for defying me you will suffer very terribly﻿—”

“Yes,” replied Freydis. “And much you will care!” she said, reproachfully.
===Chinese===
七支烛光之下的弗蕾迪丝，脸色白得像牛奶。她说：「我在这地方孱弱而属凡人，除了每个女人都有的那点本事，再没有任何力量，气力更是半分也没有。尽管如此，我也什么都不告诉你。」

曼努埃尔把手搭上拉杆，随时准备放出毁伤。「把我要你说的话说给我听，于你并无损伤——」

「那倒不，」弗蕾迪丝答道，「可我不打算听你号令，也不打算听任何活着的男人号令。」

「——而你忤逆了我，将受极大的苦楚——」

「是啊，」弗蕾迪丝答道，「可你才不会心疼呢！」她带着嗔怪的口气说道。

===Original===
“﻿—Therefore I think that you are acting foolishly.”

Freydis said: “You make a human woman of me, and then expect me to act upon reason. It is you who are behaving foolishly.”

Count Manuel meditated, for this beyond doubt sounded sensible. From the look of his handsome young face, his heart was now exceedingly troubled. Queen Freydis breathed more freely, and began to smile, with the wisdom of women, which is not superhuman, but is ruthless.

“The hand would be quite ruined, too,” said Manuel, looking at it more carefully. Upon the middle finger was a copper ring, in which was set a largish black stone: this was Schamir. But Manuel looked only at the hand.

He touched it. “Your hand, Queen Freydis, whatever mischief it may have executed, is soft as velvet. It is colored like rose-petals, but it smells more sweet than they. No, certainly, my images are not worth the ruining of such a hand.”

Then Manuel released her, sighing. “My geas must stay upon me, and my images must wait,” says Manuel.
===Chinese===
「——所以我认为，你这样做未免愚蠢。」

弗蕾迪丝说：「你把我变成了一个凡人女子，却又指望我按道理行事。行事愚蠢的，恰恰是你。」

曼努埃尔伯爵沉吟起来，因为这话听去无疑在理。从他那张英俊的年轻脸庞的神色看，他的心此刻已烦恼至极。弗蕾迪丝女王呼吸顺畅了些，开始微笑，带着女人的智慧——那智慧并非超人的智慧，却是无情的智慧。

「再说，这只手也会整个儿毁掉的。」曼努埃尔说，端详得更仔细了。它中指上戴着一枚铜戒指，镶着一颗不小的黑色石头：那便是沙米尔（Schamir）。可曼努埃尔只顾看着这只手。

他碰了碰它。「弗蕾迪丝女王，你这只手，无论它行过多少恶，却柔软得如同天鹅绒。它的颜色像玫瑰花瓣，香气却比玫瑰花瓣更甜。不，当然不，我的泥像犯不上搭上这样一只手去毁。」

于是曼努埃尔叹着气放开了她。「我的誓约（geas）仍须担在我身上，我的泥像也只得等待。」曼努埃尔说道。

===Original===
“Why, do you really like my hands?” asked Freydis, regarding them critically.

Manuel said: “Ah, fair sweet enemy, do not mock at me! All is in readiness to compel you to do my will. Had you preserved some ugly shape I would have conquered you. But against the shape which you now wear I cannot contend. Dragons and warlocks and chimaeras and such nameless monsters as I perceive to be crowding about this enclosure of buttered willow wands I do not fear at all, but I cannot fight against the appearance which you now wear.”

“Why, do you really like my natural appearance?” Freydis said, incredibly surprised. “It is a comfort, of course, to slip into it occasionally, but I had never really thought much about it one way or the other﻿—”

She went to the great mirror which had been set ready as Helmas directed, “I never liked my hair in these severe big plaits, either. As for those monsters yonder, they are my people, who are coming out of the fire to rescue me, in some of the forgotten shapes, as spoorns and trows and calcars, and other terrors of antiquity. But they cannot get into this enclosure of buttered willow wands, poor dears, on account of your magickings. How foolish they look﻿—do they not?﻿—leering and capering and gnashing their teeth, with no superstitious persons anywhere to pay attention to them.”
===Chinese===
「咦，你当真喜欢我的手吗？」弗蕾迪丝问道，挑剔地打量着它们。

曼努埃尔说：「啊，美丽而可爱的仇敌，别嘲弄我！逼你依我意愿行事的一切手段都已齐备。你若守着哪一副丑陋的形貌，我早就降服你了。可对你如今穿的这一副，我无力相争。巨龙、术士、喀迈拉（chimaeras），还有我察觉正围聚在这涂了黄油的柳条围场四周的种种无名怪物，我一概不怕；唯独你现在穿的这副容颜，我跟它打不了仗。」

「咦，你当真喜欢我天生的模样？」弗蕾迪丝说，惊讶得难以置信。「偶尔换上它，自然是一桩舒服事，可我自己从来也不曾真正想过它好还是不好——」

她走到那面照黑尔马斯（Helmas）的指点备好的大镜子跟前。「我也从不喜欢我这头发编成这一条条板板的大辫子。至于那边那些怪物，他们是我的人民，正化成一些被遗忘的形貌从火里出来救我，什么斯普恩（spoorns）啦、特罗（trows）啦、卡尔卡（calcars）啦，还有太古的其他种种可怖之物。可他们进不了这涂了黄油的柳条围场，可怜见的，都怪你那些法术。他们瞧着多蠢哪——可不是么？——挤眉弄眼，又蹦又跳，咬牙切齿，偏偏四下里连一个迷信的人也没有，没人来理会他们。」

===Original===
The Queen paused: she coughed delicately. “But you were talking some nonsense or other about my natural appearance not being bad looking. Now most men prefer blondes, and, besides, you are not really listening to me, and that is not polite.”

“It is so difficult to talk collectedly,” said Manuel, “with your appalling servitors leering and capering and gnashing double sets of teeth all over Upper Morven﻿—”

She saw the justice of this. She went now to that doorway through which, unless a man lifted her over the threshold, she might not pass, on account of the tonthecs and the spaks and the horseshoes.

She cried, in a high sweet voice: “A penny, a penny, twopence, a penny and a half, and a halfpenny! Now do you go away, all of you, for the wisdom of Helmas is too strong for us. There is no way for you to get into, nor for me to get out of, this place of buttered willow wands, until I have deluded and circumvented this pestiferous, squinting young mortal. Go down into Bellegarde and spill the blood of Northmen, or raise a hailstorm, or amuse yourselves in one way or another way. Anyhow, do you take no thought for me, who am for the while a human woman: for my adversary is a mortal man, and in that duel never yet has the man conquered.”
===Chinese===
女王顿了顿，轻巧地咳了一声。「可是方才，你正胡扯着什么我天生的模样不算难看。要知道，大多数男人更喜欢金发的女人；再说，你根本没有认真在听我说话，这可不大礼貌。」

「要镇定自若地谈话实在太难了，」曼努埃尔说，「你那些骇人的仆役在整个上莫尔文（Upper Morven）挤眉弄眼、又蹦又跳，两副牙咬得咯咯作响——」

她看出这话在理。于是她走向那道门口——因为碍着汤特克（tonthecs）、斯帕克（spaks）和那些马蹄铁，除非有男人把她抱过门槛，她不得从那里通过。

她尖起甜甜的嗓音喊道：「一便士，一便士，两便士，一便士半，再加半便士！你们全都走吧，黑尔马斯的智慧太强，咱们斗不过。你们进不了这涂了黄油的柳条围场，我也出不去，除非等我哄骗了、算计了这个讨厌的、斜眼的年轻凡人。你们下贝勒加德（Bellegarde）去放北方人（Northmen）的血，或者降一场冰雹，或者这样那样地自己找乐子去。无论如何，谁也不必为我操心——我眼下是个凡人女子：因为我的对手是个凡人男子，而在那样的决斗里，男人还从来不曾赢过。」

===Original===
She turned to Manuel. She said:

“The land of Audela is my kingdom. But you embraced my penalties, you have made a human woman of me. So do I tread with wraiths, for my lost realm alone is real. Here all is but a restless contention of shadows which pass presently; here all that is visible and all the colors known to men are shadows dimming the true colors; here time and death, the darkest shadows known to men, delude you with false seemings: for all such things as men hold incontestable, because they are apparent to sight and sense, are a weariful drifting of fogs that veil the world which is no longer mine. So in this twilit world of yours do we of Audela appear to be but men and women.”

“I would that such women appeared more often,” said Manuel.
===Chinese===
她转向曼努埃尔。她说：

奥德拉的土地是我的王国。可你领受了我的罚则，把我变成了一个凡人女子。于是我行走在幽灵中间，因为唯有我失去的国度才是真实的。在这里，一切不过是行将消逝的影子无休无止的争斗；在这里，一切可见的东西、世人知晓的一切颜色，都是遮暗真彩的影子；在这里，时间和死亡——世人所知最黑暗的影子——用虚假的表象哄骗你们：因为凡世人据以为无可争辩的一切——只因它们显现在目力与感官之前——都不过是一片令人倦怠的雾霭漂流，遮掩着那不再属于我的世界。所以在你们这暮色朦胧的世界里，我们奥德拉之民看上去不过是些凡男凡女。

「但愿这样的女人出现得更勤些。」曼努埃尔说。

===Original===
“The land of Audela is my kingdom, where I am Queen of all that lies behind this veil of human sight and sense. This veil may not ever be lifted; but very often the veil is pierced, and noting the broken place, men call it fire. Through these torn places men may glimpse the world that is real: and this glimpse dazzles their dimmed eyes and weakling forces, and this glimpse mocks at their lean might Through these rent places, when the opening is made large enough, a few men here and there, not quite so witless as their fellows, know how to summon us of Audela when for an hour the moon is void and powerless: we come for an old reason: and we come as men and women.”

“Ah, but you do not speak with the voices of men and women,” Manuel replied, “for your voice is music.”

“The land of Audela is my kingdom, and very often, just for the sport’s sake, do I and my servitors go secretly among you. As human beings we blunder about your darkened shadow world, bound by the laws of sight and sense, but keeping always in our hearts the secrets of Audela and the secret of our manner of returning thither. Sometimes, too, for the sport’s sake, we imprison in earthen figures a spark of the true life of Audela: and then you little persons, that have no authentic life, but only the flickering of a vexed shadow to sustain you in brief fretfulness, say it is very pretty; and you negligently applaud us as the most trivial of men and women.”

“No; we applaud you as the most beautiful,” says Manuel.
===Chinese===
奥德拉的土地是我的王国，在那里，我是女王，君临人类目力与感官这层帷幕背后的一切。这层帷幕永远不许揭开；但它常常被戳破，人们认出那破口，便称之为火。透过这些撕裂之处，人们得以窥见那个真实的世界：这一瞥晃花他们昏花的眼睛和孱弱的官能，这一瞥嘲弄着他们那干瘦的膂力。透过这些破口，待开口裂得够大，东一处西一处总有那么几个男人——不像他们的同类那样没头没脑——懂得趁月亮空虚无力的那一个时辰，召唤我们奥德拉之民：我们为一桩古老的缘由而来：我们以男人女人的模样而来。

「啊，可你说话用的不是男人的嗓音，也不是女人的嗓音，」曼努埃尔答道，「因为你的声音是音乐。」

奥德拉的土地是我的王国，而我和我的仆役们，常常单单为了取乐，悄悄走到你们中间。我们化作凡人，在你们这暗沉沉的影子世界里跌跌撞撞，受着目力与感官之律的束缚，心里却永远藏着奥德拉的种种奥秘，藏着我们怎样回到那边去的奥秘。有时，也是为了取乐，我们把奥德拉真实生命的一星火花囚进泥像：于是你们这些小人物——你们本没有真正的生命，只靠一缕受搅扰的影子明灭闪烁，支撑着你们短促的烦躁——便说这真是好看极了；便漫不经心地给我们鼓掌，把我们当作男人女人中最不足道的一类。

「不；我们鼓掌，是因为你是最美丽的。」曼努埃尔说道。

===Original===
“Come now, Count Manuel, and do you have done with your silly flatterings, which will never wheedle anything out of me! So you have trapped Queen Freydis in mortal flesh. Therefore I must abide in the body of a human woman, and be subject to your whims, and to your beautiful big muscles, you think, until I lend a spark of Audela’s true life to your ridiculous images. But I will show you better, for I will never give in to you nor to any man breathing.”

In silence Count Manuel regarded the delightful shaping and the clear burning colors of this woman’s face. He said, as if in sadness: “The images no longer matter. It is better to leave them as they are.”

“That is very foolish talk,” Queen Freydis answered, promptly, “for they need my aid if ever any images did. Not that, however, I intend to touch them.”

“Indeed, I forbid you to touch them, fair enemy. For were the images made as animated and lively as I wish them to be, I would be looking at them always, and not caring for any woman: and no woman anywhere would have the power to move me as your beauty moves me now, and I would not be valuing you the worth of an old onion.”

“That is not the truth,” says Freydis, angrily, “for the man who is satisfied with the figure he has made is as great a fool about women as any other man. And who are you to be forbidding me anything?”

“I would have you remember,” said Manuel, very masterfully, “that they are my images, to do with as I wish. Also I would have you remember that, whatever you may pretend to be in Audela, here I am stronger than you.”
===Chinese===
「行了，曼努埃尔伯爵，你那些蠢话一般的恭维也该收场了，它们休想从我这里哄出任何东西！就这样，你把弗蕾迪丝女王困进了凡人的血肉。于是你想，我就得栖身在一个凡人女子的躯壳里，听凭你一时兴起的摆布，听凭你那漂亮的大块肌肉的摆布，直到我把奥德拉真实生命的一星火花借给你那些可笑的泥像。可我偏要叫你看看，因为我永远不向你让步，也永远不向任何活着的男人让步。」

曼努埃尔伯爵默然端详着这张脸可爱的轮廓与那鲜明如燃的色彩。他像是悲伤似的说道：「泥像已无关紧要了。就让它们保持原样，反而更好。」

「这话蠢得很，」弗蕾迪丝女王立刻答道，「因为若说天下的泥像有几副需要我帮忙，就是这几副。不过，我也并不打算去碰它们。」

「我正是要禁止你去碰它们，美丽的仇敌。因为倘若泥像真造得像我希望的那样会动会活，我就会时时刻刻望着它们，再不把任何女人放在心上：普天之下，任何女人都将不再有力量像你的美貌此刻打动我这样打动我，而你的身价，在我眼里就连一只老洋葱都不值了。」

「那不是真话，」弗蕾迪丝怒道，「因为对自己捏出的形象心满意足的男人，在女人这件事上，同天下任何男人一样是个大傻瓜。再说，你算什么人，样样都要来禁我？」

「我要你记住，」曼努埃尔说，口气斩钉截铁，「那是我的泥像，我爱怎么处置就怎么处置。我还要你记住：无论你在奥德拉自命为什么，在这里，我都比你强。」

===Original===
Now the proud woman laughed. Defiantly she touched the nearest image, with formal ancient gestures, and you could see the black stone Schamir taking on the colors of an opal. Under her touch the clay image which had the look of Alianora shivered, and drew sobbing breath. The image rose, a living creature that was far more beautiful than human kind, and it regarded Manuel scornfully. Then it passed limping from the enclosure: and Manuel sighed.

“That is a strong magic,” said Manuel: “and this is almost exactly the admirable and significant figure that I desired to make in the world. But, as I now perceive too late, I fashioned the legs of this figure unevenly, and the joy I have in its life is less than the shame that I take from its limping.”

“Such magic is a trifle,” Freydis replied, “although it is the only magic I can perform in an enclosure of buttered willow wands. Now, then, you see for yourself that I am not going to take orders from you. So the figure you have made, will you or nil you, must limp about in all men’s sight, for not more than a few centuries, to be sure, but long enough to prove that I am not going to be dictated to.”

“I do not greatly care, O fairest and most shrewd of enemies. A half-hour since, it seemed to me an important matter to wrest from you this secret of giving life to images. Now I have seen the miracle; I know that for the man who has your favor it is possible to become as a god, creating life, and creating lovelier living beings than any god creates, and beings which live longer, too: and even so, it is not of these things that I am really thinking, but only of your eyes.”
===Chinese===
这时那骄傲的女人笑了。她带着一套正式的古老手势，公然反抗地碰了碰最近的那尊泥像，你可以看见黑石头沙米尔泛出了蛋白石般的色彩。在她的触碰下，那尊眉眼像阿莉亚诺拉（Alianora）的泥像打了个颤，抽抽噎噎地吸进一口气。泥像站起身来——一个远比人类更美丽的活物——轻蔑地望了曼努埃尔一眼。随后它一瘸一拐地走出了围场：曼努埃尔叹了一口气。

「这是强有力的魔法，」曼努埃尔说，「而这也几乎分毫不差，正是我渴望在这世上造出的那尊可羡而有意义的形象。只是如今我察觉得太迟了：我把这形象的两条腿捏得不一般齐，我从它的生命里得到的欢喜，抵不过我从它的跛行里领受的羞惭。」

「这样的魔法不过是雕虫小技，」弗蕾迪丝答道，「虽说在这涂了黄油的柳条围场里，它是我施得出的唯一魔法。好了，这下你亲眼看见了，我是不会听你号令的。于是你捏的这个形象，不管你愿意不愿意，都得在众目睽睽之下到处跛行了——当然，不过区区几个世纪，可也够长久了，足够证明我绝不受人发号施令。」

「我并不怎么在意，啊，仇敌中最美丽、最精明的一位。半个时辰之前，从你手里夺得这赋予泥像生命的秘密，在我看来还是一桩要紧的大事。如今我见过了这桩奇迹；我知道，凡得你眷顾的男人，都有可能变得如同神明，创造生命，创造出比任何神明所造更可爱的活物，寿命也更长：即便如此，我心里真正想着的也不是这些事情，而只是你的眼睛。」

===Original===
“Why, do you like my eyes!” says Freydis﻿—“you, who if once you could make living images would never be caring about any woman any more?”

But Manuel told her wherein her eyes were different from the eyes of any other person, and more dangerous, and she listened, willingly enough, for Freydis was not a human woman. Thereafter it appeared that a grieving and a great trouble of mind had come upon Manuel because of the loveliness of Freydis, for he made this complaint:

“There is much loss in the world, where men war ceaselessly with sorrow, and time like a strong thief strips all men of all they prize. Yet when the emperor is beaten in battle and his broad lands are lost, he, shrugging, says, ‘In the next battle I may conquer.’ And when the bearded merchant’s ship is lost at sea, he says, ‘The next voyage, belike, will be prosperous.’ Even when the life of an old beggar departs from him in a ditch, he says, ‘I trust to be tomorrow a glad young seraph in paradise.’ Thus hope serves as a cordial for every hurt: but for him who had beheld the loveliness of Freydis there is no hope at all.

“For, in comparison with that alien clear beauty, there is no beauty in this world. He that has beheld the loveliness of Freydis must go henceforward as a hungry person, because of troubling memories: and his fellows deride him enviously. All the world is fretted by his folly, knowing that his faith in the world’s might is no longer firm-set, and that he aspires to what is beyond the world’s giving. In his heart he belittles the strong stupid lords of earth; and they, being strong, plan vengeance, the while that in a corner he makes images to commemorate what is lost: and so for him who has beheld the loveliness of Freydis there is no hope at all.
===Chinese===
「咦，你竟然喜欢我的眼睛！」弗蕾迪丝说道——「你啊，一旦造得出活的泥像，从此就再不会把任何女人放在心上了，不是么？」

可曼努埃尔却对她讲，她的眼睛同任何旁人的眼睛有怎样的不同，又怎样的更加危险；她听着，听得相当情愿，因为弗蕾迪丝并不是凡人女子。随后看得出，一桩忧伤、一重心头的大烦恼，已经因弗蕾迪丝的美落到曼努埃尔身上，因为他发出了这样的哀叹：

「这世上多有损失：在这里，人们同哀愁无休无止地交战，时间像一个强壮的贼，把人人珍视的一切都剥抢了去。然而皇帝打了败仗、广袤的国土沦丧了，他耸耸肩，说：『下一仗我也许能赢。』蓄须的商人在海上折了船，他说：『下一趟航程，多半会是顺遂的。』就连老乞丐的性命在阴沟里离开了他，他也说：『我信得过，明天就能在乐园里做一个欢欢喜喜的小炽天使。』就这样，希望给每一处创伤当提神的药酒：可凡见过弗蕾迪丝之美的人，就再也没有任何指望了。」

「因为，同那异样澄澈的美一比，这世上便再没有美。凡见过弗蕾迪丝之美的人，从此只得像个饿汉似的行走，只因那些搅扰不休的回忆：他的同伴们怀着嫉妒讥笑他。整个世界都被他的痴愚弄得意乱心烦，因为世人知道，他对这世间伟力的信仰已不再坚牢，知道他渴求着超出这世界所能给予的东西。他心里看轻世上那些强壮而愚蠢的领主；他们呢，仗着强壮，筹划着复仇；他却躲在一个角落里捏泥像，追纪念念那失去的一切：于是凡见过弗蕾迪丝之美的人，就再也没有任何指望了。」

===Original===
“He that has willed to look upon Queen Freydis does not dread to consort with serpents nor with swine; he faces the mirror wherein a man beholds himself without self-deceiving; he views the blood that drips from his soiled hands, and knows that this, too, was needed: yet these endurings purchase but one hour. The hour passes, and therewith passes also Freydis, the high Queen. Only the memory of her hour remains, like a cruel gadfly, for which the crazed beholder of Queen Freydis must build a lodging in his images, madly endeavoring to commingle memories with wet mud: and so for him who has beheld the loveliness of Freydis there is no hope at all.”

Freydis heard him through, considerately. “But I wonder to how many other women you have talked such nonsense about beauty and despair and eternity,” said Freydis, “and they very probably liking to hear it, the poor fools! And I wonder how you can expect me to believe you, when you pretend to think me all these fine things, and still keep me penned in this enclosure like an old vicious cow.”

“No, that is not the way it is any longer. For now the figure that I have made in the world, and all else that is in the world, and all that is anywhere without this enclosure of buttered willow wands, mean nothing to me, and there is no meaning in anything save in the loveliness of Freydis.”
===Chinese===
「存心要瞻望弗蕾迪丝女王的人，不怕与蛇为伍，也不怕与猪同群；他直面那面镜子——人在其中照见毫不自欺的自己；他注视自己污秽的双手滴下的血，并且知道，这也是必要的：然而这些忍受买来的不过一个时辰。时辰过去，弗蕾迪丝、那至高的女王，也随之而去。只剩她那个时辰的记忆留存下来，像一只残酷的牛虻，弗蕾迪丝女王那疯癫的瞻望者只得在自己的泥像里为它造一所居处，发疯似的力图把回忆同湿泥搅和到一处：于是凡见过弗蕾迪丝之美的人，就再也没有任何指望了。」

弗蕾迪丝体贴地听他讲完。「只是我纳闷，这样的蠢话——关于美、关于绝望、关于永恒的蠢话——你对多少个别的女人也讲过，」弗蕾迪丝说，「而她们十有八九还爱听呢，可怜的傻瓜们！我还纳闷，你一面假意把我看成这许多好东西，一面却仍旧把我圈在这围场里，像头又老又凶的母牛，你又怎么能指望我相信你。」

「不，事情再也不是那样了。因为如今，我在世上造出的这尊形象，还有世上的其余一切，还有这涂了黄油的柳条围场之外的任何一切，于我都没有半点意义；万事万物里都再没有意义，唯独弗蕾迪丝的美里有意义。」

===Original===
Dom Manuel went to the door of the enclosure then to the windows, sweeping away the gilded tonthecs and the shining spaks, and removing from the copper nails the horseshoes that had been cast by Mohammed’s mare and Hrimfaxi and Balaam’s ass and Pegasus. “You were within my power. Now I destroy that power, and therewith myself. Now is the place unguarded, and all your servitors are free to enter, and all your terrors are untrammeled, to be loosed against me, who have no longer anything to dread. For I love you with such mortal love as values nothing else beside its desire, and you care nothing for me.”

After a little while of looking she sighed, and said uneasily: “It is the foolish deed of a true lover. And, really, I do like you, rather. But, Manuel, I do not know what to do next! Never at any time has this thing happened before, so that all my garnered wisdom is of no use whatever. Nobody anywhere has ever dared to snap his fingers at the fell power of Freydis as you are doing, far less has anybody ever dared to be making eyes at her. Besides, I do not wish to consume you with lightnings, and to smite you with insanity appears so unnecessary.”

“I love you,” Manuel said, “and your heart is hard, and your beauty is beyond the thinking of man, and your will is neither to loose nor to bind. In a predicament so unexampled, how can it at all matter to me whatever you may elect to do?”

“Then certainly I shall not waste any of my fine terrors on you!” said Freydis, with a vexed tossing of her head. “Nor have I any more time to waste upon you either, for presently the Moon-Children will be coming back to their places: and before the hour is out wherein the moon stays void and powerless I must return to my own kingdom, whither you may not follow, to provoke me with any more of your nonsense. And then you will be properly sorry, I dare say, for you will be remembering me always, and there will be only human women to divert you, and they are poor creatures.”
===Chinese===
曼努埃尔大人于是走到围场的门口，又走向各扇窗口，把镀金的汤特克、亮闪闪的斯帕克尽数扫开，又从铜钉上摘下那些马蹄铁——穆罕默德的母马、赫里姆法克西（Hrimfaxi）、巴兰的驴和珀伽索斯（Pegasus）落下的马蹄铁。「你曾在我掌握之中。如今我毁掉这份权势，也连带毁掉我自己。如今这地方再无守卫，你的仆役们尽可以自由进来，你的种种恐怖尽数挣脱了束缚，只管朝我放出来——我再没有什么可怕的了。因为我爱你，用的是这样一种凡人之爱：除了它自己的欲望，什么都不放在眼里。而你，全不把我放在心上。」

她望了他一会儿，叹了口气，局促不安地说：「这是一个真心情人干的傻事。而且，说真的，我也确实有点喜欢你。可是，曼努埃尔，我不知道下一步该怎么办！这样的事情从来没有发生过，于是我攒下的一应智慧全然派不上用场。无论在哪里，从来没有人敢像你这样，对弗蕾迪丝的可怕权势弹响指，更没有人敢对她抛媚眼。再说，我也不想用雷电把你烧成灰，而用疯狂击打你，又似乎大可不必。」

「我爱你，」曼努埃尔说，「而你的心是硬的，你的美超出人的思议，你的意志既无可解、亦无可缚。处在这般未有先例的窘境里，你无论选择做什么，于我还有什么要紧？」

「那我肯定不把我的那些精妙恐怖浪费在你身上！」弗蕾迪丝说着，恼恼地把头一甩。「我也再没有工夫在你身上浪费了，因为月中孩童（Moon-Children）转眼就要回到各自的位子上去了：趁月亮仍空虚无力的时辰还没有完结，我必须回我自己的王国去——那是你跟不去的，免得你再用这些蠢话来撩惹我。到那时你就该乖乖地懊悔了，我敢说，因为你会永远记着我，而能给你解闷的只剩下一群凡人女子，她们可都是些可怜的家伙。」

===Original===
Freydis went again to the mirror, and she meditated there. “Yes, you will be remembering me with my hair in these awful plaits, and that is a pity, but still you will remember me always. And when you make images they will be images of me. No, but I cannot have you making any more outrageous parodies like astonished corpses, and people everywhere laughing at Queen Freydis!”

She took up the magical pen, laid ready as Helmas had directed, and she wrote with this gryphon’s feather. “So here is the recipe for the Tuyla incantation with which to give life to your images. It may comfort you a little to perform that silly magic. It, anyhow, will prevent such good-for-nothing minxes as may have no more intelligence than to take you seriously, from putting on too many airs and graces around the images which you will make of me with my hair done so very unbecomingly.”

“Nothing can ever comfort me, fair enemy, when you have gone away,” said Manuel.

But he took the parchment.
===Chinese===
弗蕾迪丝又走到镜子跟前，在那里出了会儿神。「不错，你记住的我，将是我编着这一头难看辫子的模样，这实在可惜；可你仍旧会永远记着我。等你捏泥像的时候，捏出来的都会是我的像。不，不行——我可不能让你再捏出那种吓呆的尸首似的不像话的仿品，害得天下人处处耻笑弗蕾迪丝女王！」

她拿起那支魔法笔——照黑尔马斯的指点早早备下的——用这支狮鹫（gryphon）的羽毛写了起来。「喏，这就是图伊拉咒文的方子，拿去赋予你的泥像生命吧。演一演那套蠢魔法，或许能稍稍安慰安慰你。无论如何，它总归能叫那些一无是处的轻佻丫头——但凡有点脑子，也不至于拿你的话当真的那种——收敛些，别围着你将捏出的我摆太多的架子、卖太多的风情；你捏的我，可是顶着这头梳得要多难看有多难看的头发。」

「你走之后，便再没有什么能安慰我了，美丽的仇敌。」曼努埃尔说。

可他还是接过了那张羊皮纸。

## XXIV Three Women / 二十四 三个女人

===Original===
So Manuel put on his armor, and with Manuel telling as much as he thought wise of the adventures which he had encountered while Niafer was dead, they left this peculiarly irrational part of the forest, and fared out of the ruined November woods; and presently, in those barren fields that descend toward the sand dunes of Quentavic, came face to face with Queen Freydis and the Princess Alianora, where these two royal ladies and many other fine people rode toward the coast.

Alianora went magnificently this morning, on a white horse, and wearing a kirtle of changeable green like the sea’s green in sunlight: her golden hair was bound with a gold frontlet wherein were emeralds. Freydis, dark and stately, was in crimson embroidered with small gold stars and ink-horns: a hooded falcon sat on her gloved wrist.

Now Freydis and Alianora stared at the swarthy, flat-faced, limping peasant girl in brown drugget that was with Count Manuel. Then Alianora stared at Freydis.

===Chinese===
于是曼努埃尔披挂起盔甲，一路上把妮亚菲尔死去期间自己经历的种种冒险，拣着觉得讲出来无妨的部分讲给她听；就这样，他们离开了这片林子里格外悖理的一隅，走出十一月的残林败木。不久，在那向下铺展、直抵康坦维克（Quentavic）沙丘的一片荒瘠田野上，他们迎面遇上了弗蕾迪丝女王与阿莉亚诺拉公主——这两位贵妇正偕同许许多多别的体面人物，骑马往海边去。

这天早晨，阿莉亚诺拉出行得极尽华贵：她骑一匹白马，身着一袭闪色变幻的绿裙，恰似阳光里海面的碧绿；她的金发用一圈缀着祖母绿的金额饰束着。弗蕾迪丝则肤色深暗、仪态庄严，一身绯红，上头绣着小小的金星和墨水瓶；一只戴着眼罩的猎鹰，栖在她戴手套的腕上。

弗蕾迪丝和阿莉亚诺拉直勾勾打量着与曼努埃尔伯爵同行的那位农家姑娘——她肤色黝黑、面庞扁平、一瘸一拐，穿着一身棕色粗呢。随后，阿莉亚诺拉又直勾勾打量起弗蕾迪丝来。

===Original===
“Is it for this dingy cripple,” says Alianora, with her proud fine face all wonder, “that Dom Manuel has forsaken us and has put off his youth? Why, the girl is out and out ugly!”

“Our case is none the better for that,” replied Freydis, the wise Queen, whose gazing rested not upon Niafer but on Manuel.

“Who are those disreputable looking, boldfaced creatures that are making eyes at you?” says Niafer.

===Chinese===
「难道就是为了这个灰扑扑的残废丫头，」阿莉亚诺拉说道，她那张骄傲姣好的脸上满是惊诧，「曼努埃尔大人才抛弃了我们，才褪下了他的青春吗？哎呀，这姑娘可是彻头彻尾的丑！」

「那咱们的处境也未见得好上半分，」贤明的女王弗蕾迪丝答道；她的凝望并不落在妮亚菲尔身上，而是落在曼努埃尔身上。

「那两个模样不三不四、脸皮老厚、直朝你抛媚眼的货色，都是些什么人？」妮亚菲尔说。

===Original===
And Manuel, marveling to meet these two sorceresses together, replied, as he civilly saluted them from a little distance, “Two royal ladies, who would be well enough were it not for their fondness for having their own way.”

“And I suppose you think them handsome!”

“Yes, Niafer, I find them very beautiful. But after looking at them with aesthetic pleasure, my gaze returns adoringly to the face I have created as I willed, and to the quiet love of my youth, and I have no occasion to be thinking of queens and princesses. Instead, I give thanks in my heart that I am faring contentedly toward the nearest priest with the one woman in the world who to my finding is desirable and lovely.”

===Chinese===
曼努埃尔见这两位女魔法师竟聚在一处，心下暗暗称奇，便在稍远处客客气气向她们行了个礼，答道：「两位王室贵妇，若不是都太爱自行其是，本也是够好的。」

「我料想你还要觉得她们标致哩！」

「是啊，妮亚菲尔，我觉得她们非常美。不过，在怀着审美的愉悦端详过她们之后，我的目光便又怀着爱慕，回到这张我照自己心意造出来的脸庞上，回到我青年时代那份娴静的爱情上，也就再没有必要去想什么女王和公主了。恰恰相反，我打心底里感谢上苍，让我正携着这世上依我之见可人心意、温柔可爱的那唯一女子，心满意足地往最近的神父那里去。」

===Original===
“It is very sweet of you to say that, Manuel, and I am sure I hope you are telling the truth, but my faith would be greater if you had not rattled it off so glibly.”

Then Alianora said: “Greetings, and for the while farewell, to you, Count Manuel! For all we ride to Quentavic, and thence I am passing over into England to marry the King of that island.”

“Now, but there is a lucky monarch for you!” says Manuel, politely. He looked at Freydis, who had put off immortality for his kisses, and whom he had deserted to follow after his own thinking: these re-encounters are always awkward, and Dom Manuel fidgeted a little. He asked her, “And do you also go into England?”

===Chinese===
「你这话说得真甜，曼努埃尔，我当然也盼望你说的是实话；可你若不是念经似的讲得这样顺溜，我的信心本会更大些。」

随后阿莉亚诺拉说道：「向你问好，曼努埃尔伯爵，也暂且向你道别！因为我们这一行人都要骑马去康坦维克，然后我要从那里渡海去英格兰，嫁给那座岛上的国王。」

「哎呀，这位君主可真是好福气！」曼努埃尔彬彬有礼地说。他望向弗蕾迪丝——她曾为他的吻舍下不朽，而他又弃了她，去追随自己的思考：这样的重逢总是尴尬，曼努埃尔大人不觉微微有些局促。他问她：「那么您也要去英格兰吗？」

===Original===
She told him very quietly, no, that she was only going to the coast, to consult with three or four of the water-demons about enchanting one of the Red Islands, and about making her home there. She had virtually decided, she told him, to put a spell upon Sargyll, as it seemed the most desirable of these islands from what she could hear, but she must first see the place. Queen Freydis looked at him with rather embarrassing intentness all the while, but she spoke quite calmly.

“Yes, yes,” Dom Manuel said, cordially, “I dare say you will be very comfortable there, and I am sure I hope so. But I did not know that you two ladies were acquainted.”

“Indeed, our affairs are not your affairs,” says Freydis, “any longer. And what does it matter, on this November day which has a thin sunlight and no heat at all in it? No, that girl yonder has today. But Alianora and I had each her yesterday; and it may be the one or it may be the other of us three who will have tomorrow, and it may be also that the disposal of that tomorrow will be remarkable.”

===Chinese===
她十分平静地告诉他：不，她只是到海边去，同三四位水魔商谈给红色群岛中的一座施魔法、并在那里安家的事情。她告诉他说，她其实已经差不多拿定了主意，要对萨吉尔施下一道咒——据她所闻，那是诸岛之中最称心如意的一座——只是她必须先亲眼看看那地方。弗蕾迪丝女王自始至终目不转睛地望着他，望得人相当难为情，说出话来却十分平和。

「是了，是了，」曼努埃尔大人热诚地说，「我敢说你在那儿会住得非常舒坦，我也真心希望如此。只是我原先并不知道你们两位贵妇彼此相识。」

「的确，我们的事情，」弗蕾迪丝说，「往后再也不是你的事情了。再说，在这一个十一月的日子里——天光稀薄，日头里没有半点热气——那又有什么要紧？不，那边的那个丫头，拥有的是今天。而阿莉亚诺拉和我，各自都曾有过自己的昨天；至于明天，也许是我们三人中的这一个、也许是那一个将得到它；而那明天的归属处置，也说不定会非同小可。」

===Original===
“Very certainly,” declared Alianora, with that slow, lovely, tranquil smile of hers, “I shall have my portion of tomorrow. I would have made you a king, and by and by the most powerful of all kings, but you followed after your own thinking, and cared more for messing in wet mud than for a throne. Still, this nonsense of yours has converted you into a rather distinguished looking old gentleman, so when I need you I shall summon you, with the token that we know of, Dom Manuel, and then do you come posthaste!”

Freydis said: “I would have made you the greatest of image-makers; but you followed after your own thinking, and instead of creating new and godlike beings you preferred to resurrect a dead servant girl. Nevertheless, do I bid you beware of the one living image you made, for it still lives and it alone you cannot ever shut out from your barred heart, Dom Manuel: and nevertheless, do I bid you come to me, Dom Manuel, when you need me.”

Manuel replied, “I shall always obey both of you.” Niafer throughout this while said nothing at all. But she had her private thoughts, to the effect that neither of these high-and-mighty trollops was in reality the person whom henceforward Dom Manuel was going to obey.

===Chinese===
「千真万确，」阿莉亚诺拉带着她那迟缓、可爱而安详的微笑宣称，「明天里必有一份是属于我的。我本可以把你造就成一位国王，而且要不了多久，是最有权势的国王；可你去追随了自己的思考，比起一顶王座来，更在乎在湿泥巴里搅和。话虽如此，你这场胡闹也把你变成了一个模样相当体面的老绅士；所以等我用得上你的时候，我会用我们俩晓得的那件信物召唤你，曼努埃尔大人，到那时你可要快马加鞭赶来！」

弗蕾迪丝说：「我本可以把你造就成造像者中最伟大的一位；可你去追随了自己的思考，不去创造崭新而神一般的生灵，倒宁愿去复活一个死掉的使女。话虽如此，我仍要叮嘱你提防你亲手造出的那尊独一的活像：因为它至今还活着，而且唯独它，是你永远无法关在你那上了闩的心门之外的，曼努埃尔大人；话虽如此，我也仍要叮嘱你，等你需要我的时候，就到我这儿来，曼努埃尔大人。」

曼努埃尔回答：「我会永远服从你们两位。」这半晌，妮亚菲尔一言未发。可她心里自有私下的盘算：这两个趾高气扬的荡妇，哪一个实际上都不是曼努埃尔大人今后要服从的那个人。

===Original===
So the horns sounded. The gay cavalcade rode on, toward Quentavic. And as they went young Osmund Heleigh (Lord Brudenel’s son) asked for the gallant King of Navarre, “But who, sire, was that time-battered gray vagabond, with the tarnished silver stallion upon his shield and the mud-colored cripple at his side, that our Queens should be stopping for any conference with him?”

King Thibaut said it was the famous Dom Manuel of Poictesme, who had put away his youth for the sake of the girl that was with him.

“Then is the old man a fool on every count,” declared Messire Heleigh, sighing, “for I have heard of his earlier antics in Provence, and no lovelier lady breathes than Dame Alianora.”

===Chinese===
于是号角吹响。欢快的马队继续前行，往康坦维克去。途中，年轻的奥斯蒙德·赫利（Osmund Heleigh，布鲁德内尔勋爵〔Lord Brudenel〕之子）向英武的纳瓦拉国王问道：「可是，陛下，方才那个饱经时光捶打的灰色流浪汉是谁？盾牌上画着一匹失了光泽的白银骏马，身边还跟着个泥巴色的跛子——咱们两位女王怎么还要停下来同他攀谈？」

蒂博国王说，那便是大名鼎鼎的波伊捷斯曼努埃尔大人，此人为了身边那个姑娘，把自己的青春都撇下了。

「那么，这位老人家可就是样样都蠢到家了，」赫利老爷叹着气断言，「因为他在普罗旺斯早年的那些荒唐行径我早有耳闻，而世上呼吸着的女子，没有一个比阿莉亚诺拉夫人更可爱的。」

===Original===
“I consider Queen Freydis to be the handsomer of the two,” replied Thibaut, “but certainly there is no comparing either of these inestimable ladies with Dom Manuel’s swarthy drab.”

“She is perhaps some witch whose magic is more terrible than their magic, and has besotted this ruined champion?”

“It is either enchantment or idiocy, unless indeed it be something far higher than either.” King Thibaut looked grave, then shrugged. “Oy Dieus! even so, Queen Freydis is the more to my taste.”

Thus speaking, the young King spurred his bay horse toward Queen Freydis (from whom he got his ruin a little later), and all Alianora’s retinue went westward, very royally, while Manuel and Niafer trudged east. Much color and much laughter went one way, but the other way went contentment, for that while.

===Chinese===
「依我看，这两位里要数弗蕾迪丝女王更标致些，」蒂博答道，「不过，这两位贵不可言的夫人，任凭哪一位，也都万万比不得曼努埃尔大人那个黝黑的粗贱婆娘。」

「她兴许是个女巫，法术比她们的更可怕，把这员败落的豪杰迷得神魂颠倒了？」

「这若不是魅惑，便是愚痴，除非当真是比这两样都高出许多的什么东西。」蒂博国王神色转为郑重，随即耸了耸肩。「噢，天主！话虽如此，弗蕾迪丝女王还是更合我的口味。」

这样说着，年轻的国王便一夹马刺，赶着他那匹枣红马朝弗蕾迪丝女王去了（不久之后，他的毁灭正是从她那里领来的）；阿莉亚诺拉的全班仪仗扈从浩浩荡荡、堂堂皇皇地向西行去，而曼努埃尔和妮亚菲尔则徒步跋涉，往东而行。许多的色彩、许多的欢笑，走的是这一路；走另一路的，却是知足——姑且就那一阵子而论。

## XXI Touching Repayment / 二十一 动人的偿还

===Original===
So Count Manuel’s youth went out of him as he became more and more intimate with Misery, and an attachment sprang up between them, and the two took counsel as to all Manuel’s affairs. They often talked of the royal ladies whom Manuel had loved and loved no longer.

“For at one time,” Manuel admitted, “I certainly fancied myself in love with the Princess Alianora, and at another time I was in love with Queen Freydis. And even now I like them well enough, but neither of these royal ladies could make me forget the slave girl Niafer whom I loved on Vraidex. Besides, the Princess and the Queen were fond of having their own way about everything, and they were bent on hampering me with power and wealth and lofty station and such other obstacles to the following of my own thinking and my own desires. I could not endure the eternal arguing this led to, which was always reminding me, by contrast, of the quiet dear ways of Niafer and of the delight I had in the ways of Niafer. So it seemed best for everyone concerned for me to break off with Freydis and Alianora.”

“As for these women,” the head estimated, “you may be for some reasons well rid of them. Yet this Alianora has fine eyes and certain powers.”

===Chinese===
就这样，随着曼努埃尔伯爵同苦难一天比一天亲熟，他的青春也从他身上流逝了出去；两人之间生出了一份情谊，凡是曼努埃尔的事务，两人都要商量一番。他们时常谈起曼努埃尔爱过而不再爱了的那些王室贵妇。

「因为有一个时期，」曼努埃尔承认道，「我确实自以为爱上了阿莉亚诺拉公主，另一个时期又爱上了弗蕾迪丝女王。就是如今，我也还满喜欢她们俩，可是这两位王室贵妇，谁也不能叫我忘掉我在弗赖德克斯爱过的那个女奴妮亚菲尔。再说，公主和女王样样事情都爱自行其是，还一门心思要拿权势、财富、高位，以及诸如此类别的绊脚石来束缚我，妨害我追随自己的思考、自己的欲愿。由此引来的无休无止的争执，我实在消受不了：因为那些争执总在对照之下，叫我想起妮亚菲尔那些安静而可爱的做派，想起我从她的做派里尝到的欢愉。所以，为着各方有关的人，我同弗蕾迪丝和阿莉亚诺拉断绝往来，似乎是最妥当不过的。」

「至于这几个女人，」那颗头掂量着说，「为着种种缘故，你脱手了她们或许倒是好事。不过这个阿莉亚诺拉，眼睛生得美，也有些本事。」

===Original===
“She is a princess of the Apsarasas,” Manuel replied, “and therefore she has power over the butterflies and the birds and the bats, and over all creatures of the air. I know, because she has disclosed to me some of the secrets of the Apsarasas. But over her own tongue and temper the Princess Alianora has no power and no control whatever, and if I had married her she would have eventually pestered me into being a king, and giving my life over to politics and the dominion of men.”

“This Freydis, too, has beautiful black hair﻿—and certain powers﻿—”

“She was once Queen of Audela, and therefore she retains power over all figures of earth. I know, because she has disclosed to me some of the secrets of Audela. But the worst enemy of Freydis also goes in red, and is housed by the little white teeth of Freydis, for it was this enemy that betrayed her: and if I had married her she would have coaxed me, by and by, into becoming a great maker of images, and giving my life over to such arts.”

===Chinese===
「她是阿普萨拉斯的一位公主，」曼努埃尔答道，「所以蝴蝶、飞鸟、蝙蝠，以及一切空中的生灵，都归她辖制。这我知道，因为她向我透露过阿普萨拉斯的一些秘密。可是对她自己的舌头和脾气，阿莉亚诺拉公主却毫无权力、毫无管束；倘若我娶了她，她到头来准会纠缠得我去做国王，把一辈子交给政治和治人之业。」

「这位弗蕾迪丝呢，也有一头美丽的黑发——也有些本事——」

「她从前做过奥德拉的女王，因此对大地的种种形象至今保有法力。这我知道，因为她向我透露过奥德拉的一些秘密。可是弗蕾迪丝最坏的仇敌也是一身红的行头，就寓居在弗蕾迪丝那一口小小的白牙中间，因为当年出卖她的正是这个仇敌：倘若我娶了她，她早晚也会哄得我去做一个了不起的造像者，把一辈子交给这一类技艺。」

===Original===
Misery said: “You have had love from these women, you have gained power and knowledge from these women. Therefore you leave them, to run after some other woman who can give you no power and knowledge, but only a vast deal of trouble. It is not heroic, Manuel, but it is human, and your reasoning is well fitted to your time of life.”

“It is true that I am young as yet, sir﻿—”

“No, not so very young, for my society is maturing you, and already you are foreplanning and talking the follies of a man in middle life.”

“No matter what my age may come to be, sir, I shall always remember that when I first set up as a champion, and was newly come from living modestly in attendance upon the miller’s pigs, I loved the slave girl Niafer. She died. I did not die. Instead, I relinquished Niafer to Grandfather Death, and at that price I preserved my own life and procured a recipe through which I have prospered unbelievably, so that I am today a nobleman with fine clothes and lackeys, and with meadowlands and castles of my own, if only I could obtain them. So I no longer go ragged at the elbows, and royal ladies look upon me favorably, and I find them well enough. But the joy I took in Niafer is not to be found in any of these things.”

===Chinese===
苦难说道：「你从这几个女人那里得到过爱，从这几个女人那里赢到过法力和学问。于是你离开她们，去追另一个女人——这个女人给不了你法力和学问，只能给你天大的麻烦。这算不得英雄气概，曼努埃尔，倒也是人之常情，而且你这套推论，同你眼下的年纪正相称。」

「这话不错，我如今还算年轻，先生——」

「不然，也算不得怎么年轻了，因为与我作伴正使你老成起来，你已经在预作筹划、说着中年人的种种蠢话了。」

「不论我的年纪往后会变成什么，先生，我总会记得：当初我初出道充任豪杰、才从规规矩矩伺候磨坊主猪群的营生里脱身出来的时候，我爱上了那个女奴妮亚菲尔。她死了。我没有死。是我把妮亚菲尔让给了死神祖父，以那个代价保全了自己的性命，还弄到手一张方子，靠它发达得令人难以置信，于是我如今成了贵族，有体面的衣裳和仆役，有自家的草场和城堡——只消我能把它们拿到手里。这样一来，我穿得不再肘弯绽线，王室贵妇们看我的眼色也青眼有加，我觉得她们也都还好。可是我从妮亚菲尔身上得到的那份欢愉，在这些东西里是样样也寻不出的。」

===Original===
“That too is an old human story,” the head said, “and yours is a delusion that comes to most men in middle life. However, for a month of years you have served me faithfully, except for twice having failed to put enough venom in my soup, and for having forgotten to fetch in any ice that evening the Old Black One was here. Still, nobody is perfect; your time of service is out; and I must repay you as need is. Will you have happiness, then, and an eternal severance between you and me?”

“I have seen but one happy person,” Manuel replied. “He sat in a dry ditch, displaying vacant glittering eyes, and straws were tangled in his hair, but Tom o’ Bedlam was quite happy. No, it is not happiness I desire.”

The head repeated: “You have served me. I repay, as need is, with the payment you demand. What is it you demand?”

===Chinese===
「这也是一出人间老戏，」那颗头说，「你这份痴念，也是大多数男人一到中年便要犯上的。不过，在这岁月之月里，你侍奉我总算尽心，只有两回没往我的汤里搁足毒药，又有一晚黑老倌（the Old Black One）在这儿，你忘了把冰取进来。罢了，人无完人；你的工期满了；我总得照着需要偿还你。那么，你可要幸福，要你我之间一场永久的分离么？」

「我只见过一个幸福的人，」曼努埃尔答道。「他坐在一条干涸的水沟里，露着一双空茫发亮的眼睛，头发里乱糟糟缠着草茎，可贝德兰的汤姆（Tom o’ Bedlam）实在幸福。不，我想要的不是幸福。」

那颗头又说了一遍：「你侍奉过我。我照着需要，凭你讨的报酬偿还。你讨的是什么？」

===Original===
Dom Manuel said, “I demand that Niafer who was a slave girl, and is now a ghost in her pagan paradise.”

“Do you think, then, that to recall the dead is possible?”
